100. In many African countries, customary law is recognized provided it does not conflict with
     A. the opinion of the executive.
     B. the manifesto of the ruling party.
     C. the constitution and written laws.
     D. the traditions of other communities.
     E. international trade agreements.
     **Answer:** C
     **Explanation:** The text notes that customary law is applied in many African nations "provided it does not conflict with the constitution or written laws (e.g., human rights)."

---

### **Part IV: Electoral Systems and Political Participation (Chapters 7-8)**

**Chapter 7: Electoral Processes and Systems**

101. An electoral system where the candidate with the most votes wins, even without a majority, is
      A. Proportional Representation.
      B. First-Past-the-Post.
      C. Alternative Vote.
      D. Two-Round System.
      E. Mixed Member Proportional.
      **Answer:** B
      **Explanation:** In the First-Past-the-Post (FPTP) system, "The candidate who receives the most votes (a plurality) in that constituency wins the seat, even if they do not have an absolute majority."

102. A major disadvantage of the First-Past-the-Post system is that it
      A. is too complex for voters to understand.
      B. often leads to unstable coalition governments.
      C. can produce disproportionate results and disadvantage smaller parties.
      D. gives too much power to the judiciary.
      E. requires a second round of voting.
      **Answer:** C
      **Explanation:** FPTP "Often results in disproportionate representation" and can "disadvantage smaller parties and minority groups."

103. Proportional Representation (PR) systems aim to
      A. ensure a strong link between a representative and a constituency.
      B. produce single-party majority governments.
      C. allocate seats to parties in proportion to their share of the vote.
      D. simplify the voting process.
      E. eliminate all political parties.
      **Answer:** C
      **Explanation:** PR systems "aim to allocate seats to political parties in direct proportion to their share of the national vote."

104. The process of enrolling eligible citizens on an official list to vote is
      A. election monitoring.
      B. political campaigning.
      C. vote counting.
      D. voter registration.
      E. gerrymandering.
      **Answer:** D
      **Explanation:** Voter Registration is defined as "the process by which eligible citizens are enrolled on an official list (voters' register...)"

105. An independent body established to organize and supervise elections is the
      A. Judiciary.
      B. Electoral Management Body (EMB).
      C. National Assembly.
      D. Civil Service Commission.
      E. Ministry of Interior.
      **Answer:** B
      **Explanation:** An Electoral Management Body (EMB), such as an Electoral Commission, is "specifically established by law to be responsible for organizing, conducting, and supervising elections."

106. In an electoral system, "wasted votes" are most common under
      A. Party-List PR.
      B. Mixed Member Proportional.
      C. First-Past-the-Post.
      D. Alternative Vote.
      E. a Two-Round System.
      **Answer:** C
      **Explanation:** The text mentions that FPTP "can lead to 'wasted votes' (votes for losing candidates have no effect)."

107. The drawing of electoral district boundaries to favor one political party is known as
      A. delimitation.
      B. gerrymandering.
      C. franchising.
      D. canvassing.
      E. lobbying.
      **Answer:** B
      **Explanation:** Gerrymandering is the manipulation of electoral constituency boundaries to create an undue advantage for a party or group. While the word itself is not in the text, it is the correct term for this practice, and "delimitation" is the neutral term for drawing boundaries, making gerrymandering the implied opposite.

108. The observation of an election by independent domestic and international groups is called
      A. election campaigning.
      B. election monitoring.
      C. election rigging.
      D. election petition.
      E. voter education.
      **Answer:** B
      **Explanation:** Election Monitoring (Observation) is defined as "The observation of an election process by independent domestic and/or international observers to assess its fairness."

109. Which of the following is NOT a typical function of an Electoral Management Body?
      A. Voter registration.
      B. Delimitation of constituencies.
      C. Appointing government ministers.
      D. Announcing official election results.
      E. Civic and voter education.
      **Answer:** C
      **Explanation:** Appointing government ministers is a function of the chief executive (President or Prime Minister), not the EMB. All other options are listed as EMB functions.

110. A major barrier to women's participation in politics mentioned in the text is
       A. lack of interest in public affairs.
       B. constitutional prohibition.
       C. socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes.
       D. inability to vote.
       E. higher levels of education.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes that assign women to domestic roles" as a key barrier.

111. Reserved seats or party list quotas are strategies used to
       A. enhance youth participation.
       B. limit the number of candidates.
       C. enhance women's participation.
       D. reduce the cost of elections.
       E. promote a one-party system.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Quotas are listed under "Strategies to Enhance Women's Participation."

112. The Two-Round System is designed to ensure the winner receives
       A. a plurality of votes.
       B. an absolute majority of votes.
       C. the support of the legislature.
       D. the approval of the judiciary.
       E. less than 50% of the vote.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A Two-Round System is a type of Majority System where the "Candidate must win an absolute majority (more than 50%) of votes to be elected."

113. An electoral system that combines FPTP and PR elements to achieve overall proportionality is
       A. Parallel Voting.
       B. Two-Round System.
       C. Alternative Vote.
       D. Mixed Member Proportional (MMP).
       E. Party-List PR.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** MMP is described as a system where PR list seats are used to "top up" representation "to achieve overall proportionality."

114. The secrecy of the ballot is essential for
       A. ensuring all eligible citizens vote.
       B. allowing voters to make a free choice without intimidation.
       C. helping the ruling party win.
       D. speeding up the vote counting process.
       E. reducing the cost of printing ballots.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text highlights "Secrecy of Ballot" as "Essential for free choice."

115. A key role of security agencies during elections is to
       A. campaign for the ruling party.
       B. count the votes.
       C. provide an impartial and peaceful environment.
       D. monitor the media.
       E. fund political parties.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Security agencies are responsible for "maintaining law and order during the electoral period to ensure a peaceful environment." They must act impartially.

**Chapter 8: Political Parties and Interest Groups**

116. Unlike political parties, interest groups primarily seek to
       A. win elections and control government.
       B. influence public policy on specific issues.
       C. nominate candidates for all public offices.
       D. form a coalition government.
       E. replace the constitution.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Interest groups "seek to influence government policies on specific issues without necessarily seeking office themselves."

117. A Chamber of Commerce is an example of a/an
       A. social interest group.
       B. single-issue interest group.
       C. ethnic interest group.
       D. business interest group.
       E. labor union.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Chambers of Commerce are listed as a prime example of a Business Group, which is a type of Economic Interest Group.

118. A trade union is an interest group that represents
       A. business owners.
       B. professional associations.
       C. farmers.
       D. workers.
       E. consumers.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Labor Unions (Trade Unions) "Represent workers in specific industries or professions."

119. A single-issue interest group focuses on
       A. a wide range of social problems.
       B. winning presidential elections.
       C. one specific issue or a narrow set of issues.
       D. the economic welfare of all citizens.
       E. overthrowing the government.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Single-issue interest groups "Focus intensely on one specific issue or a narrow set of related issues."

120. When interest groups use letter-writing campaigns and petitions to mobilize the public, they are engaging in
       A. direct lobbying.
       B. grassroots lobbying.
       C. litigation.
       D. electioneering.
       E. civil disobedience.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Grassroots lobbying is described as "Mobilizing their members and the public to contact policymakers."

121. The media's role in influencing which issues are seen as important is called its
       A. watchdog role.
       B. entertainment role.
       C. agenda-setting function.
       D. socialization role.
       E. profit-making function.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The media has an "agenda-setting function," which is "Influencing which issues are perceived as important by the public and policymakers."

122. The "Fourth Estate" refers to the
       A. Judiciary.
       B. Executive.
       C. Legislature.
       D. Media.
       E. Civil Service.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text describes the Role of Media in Politics and Governance as the "'Fourth Estate'."

123. Civil Society refers to the "third sector" of society, distinct from
       A. government and business.
       B. political parties and interest groups.
       C. men and women.
       D. youth and elders.
       E. legislature and judiciary.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** Civil society is defined as the "'third sector' of society, distinct from government (state) and business (market)."

124. All of the following are components of civil society EXCEPT
       A. Non-Governmental Organizations (NGOs).
       B. Government Ministries.
       C. Community-Based Organizations (CBOs).
       D. Faith-Based Organizations (FBOs).
       E. Trade Unions.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Government Ministries are part of the state/government, which is explicitly distinct from civil society. All other options are listed as components of civil society.

125. A major concern regarding campaign finance is the
       A. use of public funds for campaigns.
       B. small number of individual donors.
       C. undue influence of wealthy donors on politicians.
       D. lack of advertising in campaigns.
       E. shortness of the campaign period.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Influence of Money in Politics" as a key concern, specifically the potential for "undue influence over candidates and elected officials" from large contributions.

126. Vote buying and intimidation of voters are examples of
       A. ethical campaign practices.
       B. functions of political parties.
       C. grassroots lobbying.
       D. unethical electoral practices.
       E. roles of the media.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** "Vote buying" and "intimidation of voters/opponents" are listed as examples of Unethical Practices.

127. The term for bringing together diverse interests into a coherent policy proposal is interest
       A. articulation.
       B. aggregation.
       C. opposition.
       D. mobilization.
       E. formulation.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Interest Aggregation is the function of bringing "together diverse interests, demands, and concerns from various segments of society and mold them into a broader, more coherent set of policy proposals."

128. The spread of misinformation and disinformation is a major challenge associated with the rise of
       A. print media.
       B. radio.
       C. television.
       D. social media.
       E. public broadcasting.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text notes that the "Rise of Social Media...presents challenges (e.g., spread of misinformation/disinformation - 'fake news')."

129. Using lawsuits to challenge laws or advance a cause is a tactic known as
       A. lobbying.
       B. electioneering.
       C. litigation.
       D. demonstration.
       E. negotiation.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Litigation is listed as a method used by interest groups, involving "Using lawsuits and court action to challenge laws or policies."

130. A Political Action Committee (PAC) is primarily involved in
       A. passing legislation.
       B. adjudicating legal cases.
       C. contributing to election campaigns.
       D. monitoring government accountability.
       E. providing public services.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text mentions that interest groups engage in "Electioneering and Political Action Committees (PACs): Supporting or opposing candidates for office, contributing to campaigns."

---

### **Part V: Public Policy and Governance (Chapters 9-10)**

**Chapter 9: Policy‐Making Process and Implementation**

131. The first stage in the policy-making cycle is
       A. policy adoption.
       B. policy implementation.
       C. policy evaluation.
       D. agenda setting.
       E. policy formulation.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The policy cycle begins with Agenda Setting, which is the "process by which certain problems or issues come to the attention of policymakers."

132. Major events like natural disasters or economic crises that thrust issues onto the policy agenda are known as
       A. policy options.
       B. focusing events.
       C. feedback mechanisms.
       D. implementation challenges.
       E. executive orders.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Focusing Events, such as crises, are listed as a way that issues get on the policy agenda.

133. The stage where potential solutions to a problem are developed is
       A. agenda setting.
       B. policy adoption.
       C. policy formulation.
       D. policy implementation.
       E. policy evaluation.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Policy Formulation is defined as "The development of potential solutions or alternative courses of action (policy options)."

134. Policy adoption refers to the
       A. identification of a problem.
       B. development of policy options.
       C. formal authorization of a policy option.
       D. process of putting a policy into action.
       E. assessment of a policy's impact.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Policy Adoption is the stage where "a specific policy option is formally chosen and authorized by a legitimate government body."

135. The primary actors responsible for policy implementation are
       A. political parties.
       B. the judiciary.
       C. the bureaucracy and government agencies.
       D. interest groups.
       E. the media.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text states that the Bureaucracy (Civil Service, Government Agencies, Ministries) are the "Primary implementers" of policy.

136. A common challenge to effective policy implementation is
       A. too much political will.
       B. overly clear policy objectives.
       C. an excess of resources.
       D. insufficient resources and funding.
       E. a highly flexible bureaucracy.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** "Insufficient Resources: Lack of adequate funding, personnel, or technical capacity" is listed as a key challenge to implementation.

137. The systematic assessment of a policy's effectiveness and impact is called
       A. policy formulation.
       B. policy adoption.
       C. agenda setting.
       D. policy evaluation.
       E. policy implementation.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Policy Evaluation is defined as "The systematic assessment of the effectiveness, efficiency, and impact of a public policy."

138. Citizen complaints, public opinion polls, and media reports are all forms of
       A. policy adoption.
       B. executive orders.
       C. feedback mechanisms.
       D. policy formulation.
       E. legal statutes.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** These are all listed as sources and types of feedback that inform the policy process.

139. The policy cycle is considered a 'cycle' because
       A. it is a rigid, one-way process.
       B. it only applies to economic policy.
       C. feedback from evaluation can lead to new agenda setting and reforms.
       D. it is managed by a single government agency.
       E. it never changes once adopted.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text describes the process as a dynamic cycle "where evaluation and feedback lead to policy review, potential reformulation of problems or solutions, and new rounds of decision-making."

140. When analyzing education policy, 'enrollment rates' and 'literacy rates' would be used as
       A. implementation challenges.
       B. evaluation metrics.
       C. agenda setting tools.
       D. adoption mechanisms.
       E. formulation actors.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Enrollment rates, literacy rates, exam pass rates" as Evaluation Metrics for education policy.

**Chapter 10: Human Rights, Civil Liberties, and Social Justice**

141. The concept of 'negative liberty' refers to
       A. freedom to achieve one's potential.
       B. freedom from external interference.
       C. the ability to vote.
       D. the right to education.
       E. the absence of freedom.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Negative Liberty is defined as "Freedom from external interference, constraints, or oppression."

142. 'Equality of opportunity' means that all individuals should have
       A. the same income and wealth.
       B. an equal chance to succeed in life.
       C. the right to break the law.
       D. freedom from all government rules.
       E. the same political opinions.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Equality of Opportunity is the idea that "All individuals should have an equal chance to succeed in life, regardless of their background."

143. Fairness in the allocation of resources and benefits in society refers to
       A. retributive justice.
       B. procedural justice.
       C. restorative justice.
       D. distributive justice.
       E. absolute justice.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Distributive Justice concerns "Fairness in the allocation of resources, benefits, and burdens in society."

144. The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) was adopted by the
       A. African Union.
       B. European Union.
       C. United Nations General Assembly.
       D. World Trade Organization.
       E. International Court of Justice.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The UDHR was "Adopted by the United Nations General Assembly on December 10, 1948."

145. Which of the following is a legally binding international human rights treaty?
       A. The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR).
       B. The International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights (ICCPR).
       C. The Preamble to the UN Charter.
       D. A UN General Assembly resolution on trade.
       E. The Brundtland Commission Report.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text notes that the UDHR itself is not legally binding, but the ICCPR and ICESCR are "key legally binding treaties."

146. A unique feature of the African Charter on Human and Peoples' Rights is its recognition of
       A. only civil and political rights.
       B. only economic and social rights.
       C. peoples' rights and individual duties.
       D. the supremacy of customary law.
       E. the right to form colonial empires.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The African Charter is "Unique in that it recognizes not only individual rights but also peoples' rights...and individual duties."

147. Freedom of speech is NOT absolute and can be limited to prevent
       A. criticism of the government.
       B. unpopular opinions.
       C. artistic expression.
       D. incitement to violence and defamation.
       E. political debate.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text lists "incitement to violence, defamation" as justifiable limitations on freedom of speech.

148. The right to gather peacefully in groups for a common purpose is
       A. freedom of expression.
       B. freedom of religion.
       C. freedom of assembly.
       D. freedom of movement.
       E. right to privacy.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Freedom of Assembly is defined as "The right of individuals to gather together peacefully in groups for a common purpose."

149. NGOs like Amnesty International and Human Rights Watch primarily work to
       A. provide development loans to countries.
       B. monitor and document human rights abuses.
       C. enforce international trade laws.
       D. deploy peacekeeping troops.
       E. fund political campaigns.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Monitoring and Documenting Abuses" as a key role of human rights NGOs.

150. An independent public body established by a government to promote and protect human rights within a country is a
       A. Non-Governmental Organization (NGO).
       B. Political Party.
       C. National Human Rights Institution (NHRI).
       D. Ministry of Justice.
       E. Civil Society Organization (CSO).
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** National Human Rights Institutions (NHRIs) are defined as "Independent public bodies established by government...with a mandate to promote and protect human rights."

151. Social movements are large, informal groupings focused on
       A. winning elections for a specific party.
       B. bringing about or resisting social change through collective action.
       C. providing financial aid to corporations.
       D. managing the day-to-day affairs of government.
       E. interpreting constitutional law.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Social Movements are described as being "focused on specific political or social issues, aiming to bring about or resist social change through collective action."

152. Equity differs from equality in that equity focuses on
       A. treating everyone exactly the same.
       B. fairness in providing what individuals need to succeed.
       C. ensuring everyone has the same outcome.
       D. ignoring individual disadvantages.
       E. applying the law in a discriminatory way.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text distinguishes the two: "Equity involves fairness and justice in providing what individuals need to succeed (may mean treating people differently based on their needs...)"

153. The right to form a trade union is protected under the
       A. freedom of speech.
       B. freedom of assembly.
       C. freedom of association.
       D. freedom of movement.
       E. freedom of religion.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Freedom of Association is the "right of individuals to form and join groups or organizations (e.g., political parties, trade unions...)."

154. The International Bill of Human Rights is comprised of the UDHR and the
       A. Geneva Conventions.
       B. African Charter and European Convention.
       C. two International Covenants (ICCPR and ICESCR).
       D. Convention on the Rights of the Child and CEDAW.
       E. UN Charter.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The "International Bill of Human Rights" is a term often used to refer collectively to the UDHR, the ICCPR, and the ICESCR.

155. Procedural justice is concerned with
       A. the fairness of outcomes.
       B. the fairness of processes used to make decisions.
       C. the equal distribution of wealth.
       D. the punishment of wrongdoers.
       E. the restoration of community harmony.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Procedural Justice is defined as "Fairness in the processes and procedures used to make decisions and administer laws."

---

### **Part VI: International Relations and Global Governance (Chapters 11-12)**

**Chapter 11: Theories of International Relations**

156. The IR theory that views the international system as anarchic and states as primarily seeking power and security is
       A. Liberalism.
       B. Constructivism.
       C. Realism.
       D. Marxism.
       E. Feminism.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Realism's core assumptions include an anarchic system where states' primary goal is survival and security, and power is the key currency.

157. Liberalism in IR emphasizes the importance of
       A. military power above all else.
       B. the inevitability of conflict.
       C. international organizations and economic interdependence for cooperation.
       D. class struggle as the driver of history.
       E. the social construction of reality.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Liberalism focuses on how "Shared interests, economic interdependence, democratic values, and international institutions can foster cooperation and peace."

158. The idea that "anarchy is what states make of it" is central to
       A. Realism.
       B. Liberalism.
       C. Marxism.
       D. Constructivism.
       E. Feminism.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text quotes Alexander Wendt's famous Constructivist statement, "Anarchy is what states make of it."

159. The IR theory that focuses on global inequality and the exploitation of periphery states by core states is
       A. Realism.
       B. Liberalism.
       C. Constructivism.
       D. Marxism.
       E. Democratic Peace Theory.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Marxism and its related theories focus on the "unequal division of wealth and power between dominant core (developed capitalist) states and exploited periphery (developing) states."

160. A state's ability to attract and co-opt rather than coerce is known as
       A. hard power.
       B. smart power.
       C. military power.
       D. economic power.
       E. soft power.
       **Answer:** E
       **Explanation:** Joseph Nye's concept of Soft Power is defined as the "Ability to attract and co-opt rather than coerce; based on appeal of culture, political values, foreign policies."

161. The supreme and independent authority of a state over its territory is
       A. power.
       B. sovereignty.
       C. national interest.
       D. diplomacy.
       E. globalization.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Sovereignty is defined as "The supreme and independent authority of a state over its territory and population, free from external interference."

162. The principle of non-intervention is a key aspect of
       A. globalization.
       B. sovereignty.
       C. soft power.
       D. constructivism.
       E. an alliance.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Non-intervention, the idea that other states should not interfere in a state's domestic affairs, is listed as a key aspect of Westphalian Sovereignty.

163. A formal agreement between states to cooperate on matters of security is a/an
       A. treaty.
       B. alliance.
       C. national interest.
       D. diplomatic mission.
       E. balance of power.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** An alliance is defined as a "Formal agreements or coalitions between two or more states to cooperate on matters of mutual security or defense."

164. In realist theory, the concept that states will form coalitions to prevent any single state from becoming too dominant is
       A. collective security.
       B. balance of power.
       C. national interest.
       D. diplomacy.
       E. constructivism.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Balance of Power describes a situation where "states...strive to ensure that no single state or bloc becomes overwhelmingly powerful."

165. The process of increasing interconnectedness and interdependence between countries worldwide is
       A. nationalism.
       B. isolationism.
       C. globalization.
       D. regionalism.
       E. federalism.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Globalization is defined as "A complex and multifaceted process characterized by increasing interconnectedness and interdependence between countries and societies worldwide."

166. Feminist IR theory primarily critiques traditional theories for
       A. focusing too much on cooperation.
       B. ignoring economic factors.
       C. being too optimistic.
       D. ignoring gender and women's experiences.
       E. underestimating the role of the state.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Feminist IR argues that "traditional IR theories have often ignored or marginalized women's experiences and perspectives."

167. A state's goals and ambitions in its interactions with other states is its
       A. sovereignty.
       B. diplomacy.
       C. national interest.
       D. constitution.
       E. power.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** National Interest is defined as "The goals, objectives, and ambitions of a state in its interactions with other states."

168. The art and practice of conducting negotiations between states is
       A. war.
       B. diplomacy.
       C. espionage.
       D. propaganda.
       E. sanctions.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Diplomacy is "The art and practice of conducting negotiations and managing relations between states."

169. According to Democratic Peace Theory, a central concept in Liberalism,
       A. democracies are more likely to start wars.
       B. democracies rarely fight each other.
       C. authoritarian states are peaceful.
       D. all states are equally prone to conflict.
       E. economic sanctions are ineffective.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Democratic peace theory, a key idea in Liberalism, posits that "democracies rarely fight each other."

170. A major political implication of globalization is the
       A. strengthening of state sovereignty.
       B. challenge to state sovereignty.
       C. decline of international organizations.
       D. decrease in international trade.
       E. end of cultural exchange.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Challenges to State Sovereignty" as a key political implication of globalization.

**Chapter 12: International Organizations and Treaties**

171. The UN organ with primary responsibility for maintaining international peace and security is the
       A. General Assembly.
       B. Security Council.
       C. ECOSOC.
       D. International Court of Justice.
       E. Secretariat.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The Security Council has the "Primary responsibility for maintaining international peace and security."

172. The 'veto power' in the UN Security Council is held by the
       A. ten non-permanent members.
       B. Secretary-General.
       C. five permanent members.
       D. President of the General Assembly.
       E. judges of the ICJ.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The five Permanent Members (P5) "have veto power."

173. The main deliberative and representative organ of the UN, with one vote per member state, is the
       A. Security Council.
       B. Secretariat.
       C. ECOSOC.
       D. Trusteeship Council.
       E. General Assembly.
       **Answer:** E
       **Explanation:** The General Assembly is the "main deliberative, policymaking, and representative organ" where "All member states have equal representation (one state, one vote)."

174. The principal judicial organ of the UN is the
       A. International Criminal Court (ICC).
       B. European Court of Human Rights.
       C. International Court of Justice (ICJ).
       D. African Court on Human and Peoples' Rights.
       E. UN Security Council.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The International Court of Justice (ICJ) is "The principal judicial organ of the UN."

175. The African Union (AU) is the successor to the
       A. Organization of African Unity (OAU).
       B. Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS).
       C. European Union (EU).
       D. League of Nations.
       E. Non-Aligned Movement.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The AU was "Established in 2002 (successor to Organization of African Unity - OAU)."

176. ECOWAS is primarily a
       A. military alliance.
       B. global human rights organization.
       C. regional economic integration organization.
       D. cultural exchange program.
       E. judicial body.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** ECOWAS is a "Regional economic integration organization for West African countries."

177. A key feature of the European Union (EU) that demonstrates deep integration is its
       A. single market and common currency (Euro).
       B. reliance on veto power for all decisions.
       C. lack of a parliament.
       D. focus on military conquest.
       E. exclusion of smaller countries.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The text highlights the EU's "Single market...[and] common currency (Euro)" as key features of its deep integration.

178. The formal, legally binding written agreements between states are called
       A. resolutions.
       B. declarations.
       C. communiques.
       D. treaties.
       E. conventions of war.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** International Treaties are defined as "Formal, legally binding written agreements between states."

179. The fundamental principle of international law that "agreements must be kept" is
       A. pacta sunt servanda.
       B. jus cogens.
       C. stare decisis.
       D. non-intervention.
       E. self-determination.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** "Pacta sunt servanda (agreements must be kept) is a fundamental principle of international law."

180. The Paris Agreement is a major international treaty concerning
       A. human rights.
       B. international trade.
       C. the law of the sea.
       D. climate change.
       E. nuclear non-proliferation.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The Paris Agreement, under the UNFCCC, is listed as a key environmental treaty aiming "to limit global warming."

181. UN Peacekeeping operations are authorized by the
       A. General Assembly.
       B. Secretary-General.
       C. Security Council.
       D. host country's government.
       E. World Bank.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Peacekeeping operations are "Authorized by UN Security Council."

182. The use of military force by external actors to stop widespread human rights violations is known as
       A. peacekeeping.
       B. peacebuilding.
       C. humanitarian intervention.
       D. diplomacy.
       E. conflict resolution.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Humanitarian Intervention is "The use of military force by external actors...to prevent or stop widespread and grave violations of human rights."

183. The international norm that states have a responsibility to protect their populations from atrocities is called
       A. Responsibility to Protect (R2P).
       B. The Geneva Convention.
       C. The Law of the Sea.
       D. Humanitarian Law.
       E. The Paris Agreement.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The Responsibility to Protect (R2P) is the norm asserting that states have a primary responsibility to protect their populations from specific atrocities.

184. The administrative organ of the UN, headed by the Secretary-General, is the
       A. General Assembly.
       B. Security Council.
       C. ECOSOC.
       D. Secretariat.
       E. ICJ.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The Secretariat is the "Administrative organ of the UN, headed by the Secretary-General."

185. A key difference between the UN General Assembly and the Security Council is that
       A. all UN members are in the Security Council.
       B. Security Council resolutions can be legally binding, while General Assembly resolutions are generally not.
       C. the General Assembly has veto power.
       D. the Security Council is responsible for economic issues.
       E. General Assembly decisions require unanimity.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The Security Council "Can make binding decisions," while General Assembly resolutions "are generally non-binding."

---

### **Part VII: Development Studies and Public Administration (Chapters 13-14)**

**Chapter 13: Concepts of Development**

186. The concept of development that focuses only on the increase in a country's output of goods and services is
       A. human development.
       B. sustainable development.
       C. economic growth.
       D. political development.
       E. community development.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Economic Growth "Focuses primarily on the increase in a country's output of goods and services, typically measured by...GDP."

187. The Human Development Index (HDI) measures all of the following EXCEPT
       A. life expectancy.
       B. education levels.
       C. income inequality.
       D. standard of living (GNI per capita).
       E. health.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The HDI combines measures of health (life expectancy), education, and standard of living (GNI). Income inequality is measured by a separate indicator like the GINI coefficient.

188. Sustainable development is development that
       A. prioritizes economic growth above all else.
       B. meets the needs of the present without compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
       C. focuses solely on environmental protection.
       D. is funded entirely by foreign aid.
       E. benefits only the urban population.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** This is the classic definition of sustainable development from the Brundtland Commission Report, as cited in the text.

189. The total monetary value of all finished goods and services produced within a country's borders is its
       A. Gross National Income (GNI).
       B. Gross Domestic Product (GDP).
       C. Human Development Index (HDI).
       D. GINI Coefficient.
       E. Foreign Direct Investment (FDI).
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** This is the definition of Gross Domestic Product (GDP).

190. The GINI coefficient is a measure of
       A. economic growth.
       B. human development.
       C. income inequality.
       D. poverty depth.
       E. infrastructure quality.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The GINI Coefficient is "A measure of income inequality or wealth inequality within a nation."

191. A GINI coefficient of 1 represents
       A. perfect equality.
       B. perfect inequality.
       C. high human development.
       D. low human development.
       E. zero poverty.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A GINI of 1 indicates "Perfect inequality (one person has all the income/wealth, everyone else has none)."

192. The primary mandate of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) is to
       A. fund long-term development projects.
       B. ensure the stability of the international monetary system.
       C. promote free trade exclusively.
       D. provide humanitarian aid.
       E. protect the environment.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The IMF's primary mandate is "To ensure the stability of the international monetary system."

193. The World Bank Group's main focus is on
       A. short-term balance of payments support.
       B. global military security.
       C. long-term economic development and poverty reduction.
       D. managing currency exchange rates.
       E. prosecuting war criminals.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The World Bank's "Main focus is on long-term economic development and poverty reduction in developing countries."

194. The African Development Bank (AfDB) is a
       A. global financial institution.
       B. regional multilateral development finance institution.
       C. commercial bank for individuals.
       D. non-profit charity organization.
       E. subsidiary of the IMF.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The AfDB is described as a "regional multilateral development finance institution."

195. A major challenge to development in many countries is
       A. a surplus of infrastructure.
       B. extremely low levels of inequality.
       C. a lack of poverty.
       D. a significant infrastructure deficit.
       E. zero unemployment.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** "Infrastructure Deficit" is listed as one of the major challenges to achieving sustainable development.

196. The "trickle-down effect" is an assumption associated with the concept of
       A. sustainable development.
       B. human development.
       C. economic growth.
       D. community development.
       E. political development.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The economic growth model "Assumes that economic growth will lead to improved living standards ('trickle-down effect')."

197. Which of the following best describes multidimensional poverty?
       A. Having an income just below the national poverty line.
       B. Being poor relative to the national average.
       C. Experiencing deprivations in health, education, and living standards, not just income.
       D. Being unemployed for a short period.
       E. Lacking access to luxury goods.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) "Measures poverty based on deprivations in multiple dimensions (health, education, living standards) rather than just income."

198. Historically, a major criticism of IMF and World Bank loans was the
       A. lack of available funds.
       B. low interest rates.
       C. imposition of conditionalities (SAPs).
       D. focus on helping only the poorest countries.
       E. refusal to work with governments.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Conditionalities attached to loans (can impose unpopular or harmful policies, undermine national sovereignty)" as a key criticism.

199. The Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) provide a global framework for
       A. achieving sustainable development by 2030.
       B. establishing a world government.
       C. promoting economic growth at any cost.
       D. managing the global financial system.
       E. funding military interventions.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The SDGs "provide a global framework for achieving sustainable development by 2030."

200. Human Development, as a concept, was pioneered by economists like
       A. Adam Smith and David Ricardo.
       B. Karl Marx and Friedrich Engels.
       C. John Maynard Keynes.
       D. Amartya Sen and Mahbub ul Haq.
       E. Milton Friedman.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text credits "economists like Amartya Sen and Mahbub ul Haq" with pioneering the human development approach.

**Chapter 14: Public Administration and Leadership**

201. The leadership theory that focuses on an exchange relationship between leaders and followers using rewards and punishments is
       A. Transformational Leadership.
       B. Servant Leadership.
       C. Trait Theory.
       D. Transactional Leadership.
       E. Behavioral Theory.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Transactional Leadership "Focuses on the exchange relationship between leaders and followers" using rewards and corrective action.

202. A leader who inspires followers to achieve extraordinary outcomes by articulating a compelling vision is a
       A. transactional leader.
       B. laissez-faire leader.
       C. transformational leader.
       D. bureaucratic leader.
       E. autocratic leader.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Transformational Leadership involves "Inspirational Motivation: Articulates a compelling vision, inspires enthusiasm and optimism."

203. Servant Leadership, as a philosophy, emphasizes
       A. the leader's authority and power.
       B. achieving goals at any cost.
       C. the growth and well-being of people and the community.
       D. a strict chain of command.
       E. maintaining the status quo.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Servant Leadership "Focuses primarily on the growth and well-being of people and the communities to which they belong."

204. Acting in the best interests of the public, rather than for personal gain, is the ethical principle of
       A. diligence.
       B. confidentiality.
       C. objectivity.
       D. public interest.
       E. integrity.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The primacy of Public Interest means "Acting in the best interests of the public, rather than for personal gain or partisan advantage."

205. The abuse of public office for private gain is known as
       A. leadership.
       B. ethics.
       C. bureaucracy.
       D. administration.
       E. corruption.
       **Answer:** E
       **Explanation:** Corruption is defined as "The abuse of entrusted power or public office for private gain."

206. Showing undue favor to relatives in appointments is a form of corruption called
       A. bribery.
       B. extortion.
       C. embezzlement.
       D. fraud.
       E. nepotism.
       **Answer:** E
       **Explanation:** Nepotism is defined as "Showing undue favor to relatives or friends in appointments or contracts."

207. Which of the following is a key anti-corruption measure?
       A. Reducing the salaries of public officials.
       B. Abolishing codes of conduct.
       C. Ensuring government secrecy.
       D. Protecting whistleblowers.
       E. Weakening the judiciary.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** "Whistleblower Protection: Protecting individuals who report corruption" is listed as a key anti-corruption measure.

208. The use of ICTs by government to provide services to citizens is called
       A. New Public Management (NPM).
       B. Performance Management.
       C. E-Government.
       D. Decentralization.
       E. Civil Service Reform.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** E-Government is "The use of Information and Communication Technologies (ICTs) by government agencies to provide information and services to citizens."

209. A major benefit of E-Government is its potential to
       A. increase the digital divide.
       B. increase opportunities for face-to-face corruption.
       C. reduce efficiency and transparency.
       D. increase convenience and accessibility for citizens.
       E. make government more complex.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text lists "Increased convenience and accessibility for citizens" as a key benefit of E-Government.

210. The systematic process of planning, monitoring, and evaluating the performance of government organizations is
       A. performance management.
       B. e-government.
       C. administrative reform.
       D. public sector ethics.
       E. servant leadership.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** This is the definition of Performance Management in the Public Sector.

211. When a bureaucracy focuses on rules for their own sake rather than achieving policy goals, it is known as
       A. goal displacement.
       B. red tape.
       C. impersonality.
       D. hierarchy.
       E. specialization.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** Goal Displacement is a criticism of bureaucracy, meaning "Focusing on rules and procedures for their own sake rather than achieving the original policy goals."

212. A key component of Transformational Leadership is 'Intellectual Stimulation', which involves
       A. acting as a role model.
       B. challenging assumptions and encouraging creativity.
       C. acting as a coach or mentor.
       D. articulating a compelling vision.
       E. rewarding good performance.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Intellectual Stimulation "Challenges assumptions, encourages creativity and innovation, stimulates critical thinking."

213. A public official whose personal business interests clash with their official duties is in a
       A. conflict of interest.
       B. state of integrity.
       C. position of objectivity.
       D. leadership role.
       E. merit-based system.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** Conflict of Interest is when "a public official's personal interests conflict with their official duties."

214. The New Public Management (NPM) reform movement sought to
       A. increase the size of the bureaucracy.
       B. centralize all government functions.
       C. introduce private sector management techniques into the public sector.
       D. reduce transparency and accountability.
       E. abolish performance indicators.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** NPM involved "Introducing market-based principles and private sector management techniques into public sector."

215. A significant challenge for E-Government implementation is the
       A. digital divide.
       B. lack of paper records.
       C. high level of public trust.
       D. speed of the internet.
       E. over-training of staff.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The Digital Divide, meaning "unequal access to ICTs," is listed as a key challenge.

---

### **Part VIII: Local Government and Community Development (Chapter 15)**

216. The existence and powers of local government are typically defined in the
       A. national budget.
       B. manifestoes of political parties.
       C. national constitution and local government acts.
       D. annual reports of the central bank.
       E. decrees of the military.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text states that the framework for local government is usually defined by "The National Constitution" and "National or State/Regional Legislation (Local Government Acts/Laws)."

217. The principle that public functions should be handled by the lowest competent level of government is
       A. subsidiarity.
       B. centralization.
       C. sovereignty.
       D. accountability.
       E. transparency.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** Subsidiarity is "the principle that public functions should be exercised by the lowest level of government competent to perform them effectively."

218. The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level is the
       A. Mayor's office.
       B. Traditional Council.
       C. Local Council.
       D. Community Development Committee.
       E. Local Government Service Commission.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The Local Council is described as "The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level."

219. Laws made by a local council are commonly known as
       A. decrees.
       B. acts of parliament.
       C. constitutions.
       D. by-laws or ordinances.
       E. executive orders.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** A function of the local council is "Making local laws (by-laws or ordinances)."

220. The head of the executive arm of a local government is often a
       A. Speaker.
       B. Chief Judge.
       C. Chairperson or Mayor.
       D. Town Clerk.
       E. Councillor.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text identifies the "Chairperson/Mayor/Council Leader" as the head of the local executive.

221. In many African countries, traditional authorities play a key role in
       A. passing national legislation.
       B. commanding the armed forces.
       C. conducting foreign policy.
       D. resolving disputes using customary methods.
       E. printing currency.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** A potential role for traditional authorities is "Dispute resolution (using customary methods)."

222. Property rates and market levies are examples of
       A. grants from the central government.
       B. foreign aid.
       C. locally generated revenue.
       D. loans from commercial banks.
       E. capital expenditure.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** "Property rates/taxes" and "Market levies and fees" are listed under "Locally Generated Revenue."

223. Grants from the central government that must be used for a particular program like education are
       A. general purpose grants.
       B. specific purpose grants (conditional).
       C. equalization grants.
       D. block grants.
       E. local taxes.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Specific Purpose Grants are described as being "earmarked" and "Must be used for particular sectors or programs."

224. In the local government budget cycle, the final approval of the budget is given by the
       A. Mayor or Chairperson.
       B. Finance Department.
       C. Central Government.
       D. Local Council.
       E. Traditional Ruler.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The draft budget is "submitted to the local council for review, debate, modification, and final approval."

225. Spending on the construction of schools and roads by a local government is classified as
       A. recurrent expenditure.
       B. capital expenditure.
       C. personal emoluments.
       D. overhead costs.
       E. debt servicing.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Capital Expenditure is defined as "Investment in long-term assets (e.g., construction of schools, roads, clinics...)."

226. Community participation in local governance is important because it
       A. ensures that local leaders are not held accountable.
       B. increases secrecy in decision-making.
       C. promotes local ownership and sustainability of projects.
       D. centralizes all power in the hands of the chairperson.
       E. makes service delivery less efficient.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** An important aspect of community participation is that it "Promotes local ownership and sustainability of development efforts."

227. Participatory budgeting is a mechanism for
       A. the central government to set the local budget.
       B. the chairperson to decide the budget alone.
       C. involving citizens in budget decision-making.
       D. auditing past expenditures.
       E. raising local taxes.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Participatory budgeting processes are listed as a mechanism for community participation.

228. Rural development primarily aims to
       A. increase urbanization.
       B. improve the economic and social well-being of people in rural areas.
       C. focus only on industrial growth in cities.
       D. encourage migration to urban centers.
       E. promote large-scale commercial farming exclusively.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** Rural development is defined as a "process aimed at improving the economic and social well-being of people living in rural areas."

229. Which of the following is NOT a typical function of local government?
       A. Provision of primary education and healthcare.
       B. Waste management and collection.
       C. Management of national defense and foreign policy.
       D. Maintenance of local roads and street lighting.
       E. Registration of births, deaths, and marriages.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** National defense and foreign policy are functions of the central/national government, not local government. All other options are typical local government functions.

230. A major source of revenue for many local governments, especially those with weak tax bases, is
       A. intergovernmental fiscal transfers.
       B. income from international trade.
       C. selling national assets.
       D. donations from citizens.
       E. printing money.
       **Answer:** A
       **Explanation:** The text notes that "Intergovernmental Fiscal Transfers (Grants...)" are "Often a major source of revenue, especially for local governments with limited local tax bases."

### **Part IX: Combined and Applied Concepts (Chapters 16-17 and Review)**

231. A constitution that can be amended by a simple majority in the legislature is said to be
       A. rigid.
       B. federal.
       C. written.
       D. flexible.
       E. confederal.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** A flexible constitution "Can be amended relatively easily, often by the same process as ordinary laws (e.g., by a simple majority vote...)." (Ch. 3)

232. The fusion of the executive and legislative branches is a key feature of
       A. separation of powers.
       B. the presidential system.
       C. the parliamentary system.
       D. judicial independence.
       E. a federal state.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The parliamentary system is characterized by a "Fusion of executive and legislative powers (to a large extent)." (Ch. 2)

233. In a federal system, governmental powers are shared between the
       A. executive and the judiciary.
       B. central government and component units.
       C. upper and lower houses of the legislature.
       D. ruling party and the opposition.
       E. government and traditional rulers.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A federal system is one where "Sovereign power is divided and shared between a central (national or federal) government and constituent regional governments." (Ch. 2)

234. An organized group that seeks to capture governmental power is a
       A. pressure group.
       B. political party.
       C. civil society organization.
       D. trade union.
       E. professional association.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A political party seeks to "influence or control government policy and personnel by...electing candidates to public office." (Ch. 2)

235. The electorate refers to
       A. all members of a political party.
       B. all officials in the government.
       C. persons qualified to vote in an election.
       D. candidates standing for election.
       E. members of the electoral commission.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The electorate is the body of citizens who are eligible to vote.

236. The right of a citizen to vote is called
       A. a referendum.
       B. a recall.
       C. suffrage or franchise.
       D. an initiative.
       E. a plebiscite.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Suffrage or franchise is the right to vote in public, political elections.

237. The principle of collective responsibility is a feature of the
       A. judicial branch.
       B. civil service.
       C. cabinet in a parliamentary system.
       D. two-party system.
       E. federal system.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text highlights "Collective Responsibility" as a key feature of the Cabinet, "especially in Parliamentary Systems." (Ch. 4)

238. Which of the following is NOT an agent of political socialization?
       A. The family.
       B. The school.
       C. Political parties.
       D. The media.
       E. Climatic conditions.
       **Answer:** E
       **Explanation:** Political socialization is the process of acquiring political values. Family, school, parties, and media all play a role. Climate does not. (Concept is in Ch. 1 and Ch. 2).

239. A government formed by a combination of two or more political parties is a
       A. national government.
       B. majority government.
       C. coalition government.
       D. unitary government.
       E. minority government.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** Coalition governments are formed when multiple parties join together, typically in a multi-party system where no single party has a majority. (Ch. 2)

240. The power of a head of state to nullify the punishment of a convicted person is called
       A. veto.
       B. pardon.
       C. impeachment.
       D. appeal.
       E. injunction.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** The power of "Granting Pardons and Reprieves" is listed as a role of the chief executive. (Ch. 4)

241. A major advantage of a one-party system is that it
       A. promotes diverse viewpoints.
       B. guarantees human rights.
       C. may lead to quick decision-making.
       D. ensures free and fair elections.
       E. provides wide voter choice.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** By eliminating opposition, one-party systems can make decisions quickly, though this comes at the cost of democratic principles. This is an inferred advantage from its structure.

242. In international relations, the acronym NGO stands for
       A. National Government Organization.
       B. Non-Governmental Organization.
       C. New Governmental Order.
       D. National Governance Office.
       E. Non-Governing Official.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** NGO stands for Non-Governmental Organization, a key type of actor in civil society and international relations. (Ch. 10 & 11)

243. The Commander-in-Chief of the Armed Forces in most presidential systems is the
       A. Chief of Defence Staff.
       B. Minister of Defence.
       C. President.
       D. Speaker of the Legislature.
       E. Chief Justice.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The President holds the role of "Commander-in-Chief of Armed Forces." (Ch. 4)

244. A unicameral legislature is one which has
       A. two houses.
       B. one house.
       C. three houses.
       D. no power.
       E. an elected speaker.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A unicameral legislature is a law-making body with a single chamber or house. (Ch. 3)

245. A law passed by the legislature is called
       A. a decree.
       B. an edict.
       C. a bill.
       D. an act or statute.
       E. a motion.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** Once a bill is passed by the legislature and receives assent, it becomes an Act or Statute. (Ch. 3)

246. The term 'civil service' refers to
       A. all politicians in government.
       B. the armed forces and the police.
       C. the body of professional, non-political government officials.
       D. all workers in the private sector.
       E. members of the judiciary.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The Civil Service is "The body of professional, non-political government officials (civil servants) who are employed on a permanent basis..." (Ch. 4)

247. The process of removing an elected official from office by a popular vote before their term ends is
       A. an impeachment.
       B. a recall.
       C. a referendum.
       D. an initiative.
       E. a vote of no confidence.
       **Answer:** B
       **Explanation:** A Recall is a mechanism of direct democracy where "Citizens can vote to remove an elected official from office before their term ends." (Ch. 2)

248. The foreign policy of a country is primarily driven by its
       A. judicial precedents.
       B. electoral system.
       C. national interest.
       D. form of legislature.
       E. civil service rules.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** A state's foreign policy is guided by its National Interest, which encompasses its goals and objectives in the international arena. (Ch. 11)

249. Which of the following pairs of countries operate a presidential system of government?
       A. USA and UK.
       B. Nigeria and Canada.
       C. UK and India.
       D. USA and Nigeria.
       E. Germany and France.
       **Answer:** D
       **Explanation:** The text cites the USA and Nigeria as key examples of presidential systems, while the UK, Canada, and India are parliamentary. (Ch. 2)

250. Good governance is crucial for achieving
       A. authoritarian rule.
       B. national disintegration.
       C. sustainable development and public trust.
       D. political apathy.
       E. economic recession.
       **Answer:** C
       **Explanation:** The text concludes Chapter 1 by stating, "Good governance is crucial for sustainable development, democratic consolidation, and public trust in institutions."


Of course. Here are 250 WAEC-standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations, derived from the foundational textbook text you provided. The questions cover all relevant chapters (1-15) and are designed to test recall, understanding, and application of the concepts.

---

### **Chapter 1: Meaning and Scope of Government and Governance**

1.  **Which term refers to the formal institutions and processes through which a state is ruled?**
    A) Governance
    B) Politics
    C) Government
    D) Society
    E) Administration

    **Answer:** C) Government
    **Explanation:** The text defines government as "the formal institutions, structures, and processes through which a state or a community is ruled." Governance is the broader process, politics is the struggle for power, society is the community, and administration is a function of government.

2.  **The concept of "who gets what, when, and how" is famously associated with the definition of**
    A) Governance
    B) Politics
    C) Government
    D) The State
    E) Authority

    **Answer:** B) Politics
    **Explanation:** The text directly quotes Harold Lasswell's famous description of politics as "'who gets what, when, and how'", highlighting its focus on the allocation of scarce resources.

3.  **Governance is a broader concept than government because it includes**
    A) only the legislature and judiciary.
    B) only the head of state.
    C) both formal institutions and informal actors like civil society.
    D) only the activities of political parties.
    E) only the enforcement of laws by the police.

    **Answer:** C) both formal institutions and informal actors like civil society.
    **Explanation:** Governance encompasses not just the formal institutions of government but also "the various actors (e.g., civil society organizations, private sector, international bodies, citizens) and informal mechanisms" that influence decision-making.

4.  **The monopoly on the legitimate use of force is a key characteristic of**
    A) Politics
    B) Government
    C) Governance
    D) Civil Society
    E) The Electorate

    **Answer:** B) Government
    **Explanation:** The text lists "The legitimate authority to use force or coercion (e.g., through police, military)" as a key characteristic of government.

5.  **Which of the following is NOT a primary function of government?**
    A) Maintenance of law and order
    B) Provision of public services
    C) Economic management
    D) Formation of private corporations
    E) National defense

    **Answer:** D) Formation of private corporations
    **Explanation:** While government regulates the economy and may own state enterprises, its primary functions are security, service provision, and economic management. The formation of private corporations is an activity of the private sector.

6.  **Building roads, funding schools, and providing healthcare are examples of which function of government?**
    A) Maintenance of law and order
    B) Economic management
    C) Political socialization
    D) Provision of public services
    E) Conflict resolution

    **Answer:** D) Provision of public services
    **Explanation:** The text lists infrastructure, education, and healthcare as prime examples of public and social services provided by the government.

7.  **The management of government revenue and expenditure to influence the economy is known as**
    A) Monetary policy
    B) Trade policy
    C) Fiscal policy
    D) Judicial review
    E) Political policy

    **Answer:** C) Fiscal policy
    **Explanation:** The text defines fiscal policy as "Managing government revenue (taxation) and expenditure (public spending) to influence the economy." Monetary policy deals with money supply and interest rates.

8.  **A system of government where supreme power is vested in the people and exercised through representation is called**
    A) Monarchy
    B) Oligarchy
    C) Authoritarianism
    D) Democracy
    E) Theocracy

    **Answer:** D) Democracy
    **Explanation:** Democracy is defined as a system where "supreme power is vested in the people and exercised by them directly or indirectly through a system of representation."

9.  **Which form of government is characterized by the rule of a single individual who often seizes power by force?**
    A) Constitutional Monarchy
    B) Dictatorship
    C) Oligarchy
    D) Representative Democracy
    E) Anarchy

    **Answer:** B) Dictatorship
    **Explanation:** A dictatorship, a subtype of authoritarianism, is defined as "Rule by a single individual (dictator) who has absolute power, often obtained and maintained by force."

10. **A government ruled by a small, wealthy elite is best described as a(n)**
    A) Plutocracy
    B) Democracy
    C) Theocracy
    D) Monarchy
    E) Anarchy

    **Answer:** A) Plutocracy
    **Explanation:** The text notes that an oligarchy is "rule by the few," and a specific form of it, plutocracy, is "rule by the wealthy."

11. **The principle of good governance that ensures government officials are answerable for their actions is**
    A) Participation
    B) Transparency
    C) Accountability
    D) Rule of law
    E) Efficiency

    **Answer:** C) Accountability
    **Explanation:** Accountability is defined as the principle where "Governmental institutions...are accountable to the public" and "officials are answerable for their actions and decisions."

12. **The "Rule of Law" implies that**
    A) the ruler is above the law.
    B) laws are made only by the ruler.
    C) everyone, including the government, is subject to the law.
    D) only citizens must obey the law.
    E) law is a tool for the powerful.

    **Answer:** C) everyone, including the government, is subject to the law.
    **Explanation:** The principle of Rule of Law states that "All individuals, institutions, and entities, including the government itself, are accountable to laws that are publicly promulgated, equally enforced, and independently adjudicated."

13. **A government that attempts to control all aspects of public and private life is best described as**
    A) Democratic
    B) Totalitarian
    C) Monarchical
    D) Oligarchic
    E) Anarchic

    **Answer:** B) Totalitarian
    **Explanation:** Totalitarianism is defined as an "extreme form of authoritarianism where the state attempts to control all aspects of public and private life."

14. **Which of the following is NOT a core principle of good governance?**
    A) Rule by the strongest
    B) Transparency
    C) Participation
    D) Accountability
    E) Equity and Inclusiveness

    **Answer:** A) Rule by the strongest
    **Explanation:** Rule by the strongest is contrary to the principles of good governance, especially the rule of law and equity. The other options are listed as core principles.

15. **A constitutional monarchy is a system where**
    A) the monarch has absolute power.
    B) the monarch's power is limited by a constitution.
    C) there is no monarch.
    D) religious leaders rule.
    E) the military is in control.

    **Answer:** B) the monarch's power is limited by a constitution.
    **Explanation:** The text defines a constitutional monarchy as a system where "the monarch's powers are limited by a constitution and laws," and they often serve as a ceremonial head of state.

---

### **Chapter 2: Political Ideologies and Systems**

16. **Which political ideology emphasizes individual liberty, rights, and the consent of the governed?**
    A) Socialism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Liberalism
    D) Communism
    E) Nationalism

    **Answer:** C) Liberalism
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the core values of liberalism as emphasizing "individual liberty, rights, equality of opportunity, reason, and consent of the governed."

17. **An ideology that values tradition, established institutions, and gradual change is known as**
    A) Liberalism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Anarchism
    D) Socialism
    E) Fascism

    **Answer:** B) Conservatism
    **Explanation:** Conservatism is defined by its emphasis on "tradition, established institutions, social order, authority, hierarchy, and gradual change."

18. **The primary aim of socialism is to promote**
    A) individual profit.
    B) social equality and collective ownership.
    C) a stateless society immediately.
    D) aggressive national expansion.
    E) limited government intervention.

    **Answer:** B) social equality and collective ownership.
    **Explanation:** Socialism's core values include "social equality, community, cooperation, and collective ownership or control of the means of production."

19. **Communism, in its theoretical form, aims for a society that is**
    A) classless and stateless.
    B) ruled by a monarch.
    C) based on a free market.
    D) led by a military junta.
    E) highly individualistic.

    **Answer:** A) classless and stateless.
    **Explanation:** Communism is described as aiming for "a classless, stateless society where the means of production are communally owned."

20. **Nationalism is an ideology that primarily promotes the interests of a**
    A) particular social class.
    B) specific religious group.
    C) particular nation.
    D) single political party.
    E) global community.

    **Answer:** C) a particular nation.
    **Explanation:** Nationalism is defined as an ideology that "promotes the interests of a particular nation (a group of people with a shared identity...)."

21. **In a direct democracy, laws are made by**
    A) elected representatives.
    B) the head of state.
    C) citizens themselves.
    D) a council of elders.
    E) the judiciary.

    **Answer:** C) citizens themselves.
    **Explanation:** Direct democracy is a system where "Citizens participate directly in making laws and policy decisions, without intermediaries or elected representatives."

22. **Most modern large-scale democracies are representative because**
    A) citizens are not intelligent enough to rule.
    B) direct democracy is impractical for large populations.
    C) monarchy is a better system.
    D) political parties do not exist.
    E) it is cheaper to operate.

    **Answer:** B) direct democracy is impractical for large populations.
    **Explanation:** The text notes that direct democracy is "Impractical for large, complex modern states," making representative democracy the more common and practical form.

23. **A key feature of a unitary system of government is that power is**
    A) divided between central and regional governments.
    B) concentrated in the central government.
    C) held by the judiciary.
    D) shared equally among all citizens.
    E) exercised by a monarch.

    **Answer:** B) concentrated in the central government.
    **Explanation:** In a unitary system, "Sovereign power is concentrated in a single, central (national) government," and any regional units are subordinate to it.

24. **In a federal system of government, like Nigeria or the USA, sovereignty is**
    A) held exclusively by the central government.
    B) held exclusively by the state governments.
    C) divided between the central and state governments.
    D) vested in the legislature alone.
    E) non-existent.

    **Answer:** C) divided between the central and state governments.
    **Explanation:** A federal system is one where "Sovereign power is divided and shared between a central (national or federal) government and constituent regional governments."

25. **The defining characteristic of a presidential system of government is**
    A) the fusion of executive and legislative powers.
    B) the clear separation of powers between branches.
    C) the Prime Minister being the head of government.
    D) the Head of State being a monarch.
    E) the absence of a judiciary.

    **Answer:** B) the clear separation of powers between branches.
    **Explanation:** A presidential system is characterized by a "Clear separation of powers between the executive, legislative, and judicial branches."

26. **In a parliamentary system, the head of government is the Prime Minister who is accountable to the**
    A) monarch.
    B) people directly through a separate election.
    C) judiciary.
    D) legislature.
    E) civil service.

    **Answer:** D) legislature.
    **Explanation:** In a parliamentary system, the "Prime Minister (PM) and their cabinet...are drawn from, and are accountable to, the legislature (parliament)."

27. **A political system with three or more parties having a realistic chance of gaining power is a**
    A) one-party system.
    B) two-party system.
    C) multi-party system.
    D) zero-party system.
    E) dominant-party system.

    **Answer:** C) multi-party system.
    **Explanation:** A multi-party system is defined as one where "Three or more political parties have a realistic chance of gaining power."

28. **One of the primary functions of an opposition party in a democracy is to**
    A) overthrow the government by force.
    B) always agree with government policies.
    C) scrutinize government actions and offer alternative policies.
    D) refuse to participate in elections.
    E) control the judiciary.

    **Answer:** C) scrutinize government actions and offer alternative policies.
    **Explanation:** The text states that parties not in power (opposition) "critically examine government actions, hold it accountable, and offer alternative policies."

29. **A system where the executive can be removed from office by a legislative "vote of no confidence" is**
    A) presidential.
    B) authoritarian.
    C) monarchical.
    D) parliamentary.
    E) federal.

    **Answer:** D) parliamentary.
    **Explanation:** A key feature of the parliamentary system is that the government "can be removed by a vote of no confidence" from the legislature.

30. **Which of the following countries is a classic example of a federal system?**
    A) France
    B) United Kingdom
    C) Japan
    D) Nigeria
    E) Ghana

    **Answer:** D) Nigeria
    **Explanation:** The text lists Nigeria, along with the United States, Canada, Germany, etc., as examples of countries with a federal system. The others listed as options are unitary.

---

### **Chapter 3: Constitutions and the Rule of Law**

31. **A constitution is best described as the**
    A) set of rules for a political party.
    B) collection of all ordinary laws.
    C) supreme law of the land establishing the framework of government.
    D) history of a country's rulers.
    E) annual budget of a country.

    **Answer:** C) supreme law of the land establishing the framework of government.
    **Explanation:** A constitution is defined as "a set of fundamental principles...according to which a state...is governed" and is "often considered the supreme law of the land."

32. **A key advantage of a written constitution is its**
    A) flexibility and ease of change.
    B) clarity and accessibility.
    C) reliance on unwritten conventions.
    D) subordination to ordinary laws.
    E) temporary nature.

    **Answer:** B) clarity and accessibility.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Clarity, accessibility, stability" as advantages of a written constitution, providing a clear standard for governance.
