33. **The constitution of the United Kingdom is a prime example of a(n) \_\_\_\_\_ constitution.**
    A) rigid
    B) written
    C) federal
    D) unwritten
    E) republican

    **Answer:** D) unwritten
    **Explanation:** The United Kingdom is cited as the "primary example" of a country with an unwritten (uncodified) constitution, where principles are found in various sources.

34. **A rigid constitution is one that**
    A) can be amended by a simple majority vote.
    B) is very short and easy to read.
    C) is difficult to amend, requiring a special procedure.
    D) applies only to the government.
    E) has never been changed.

    **Answer:** C) is difficult to amend, requiring a special procedure.
    **Explanation:** A rigid constitution is defined as being "Difficult to amend (change). Requires a special, complex procedure for amendment."

35. **The principle of separation of powers is most famously associated with**
    A) Karl Marx
    B) Harold Lasswell
    C) John Locke
    D) Baron de Montesquieu
    E) Adam Smith

    **Answer:** D) Baron de Montesquieu
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the Separation of Powers was "most famously, articulated by...Baron de Montesquieu."

36. **The primary function of the legislature is to**
    A) interpret laws.
    B) execute laws.
    C) make laws.
    D) elect the president.
    E) command the armed forces.

    **Answer:** C) make laws.
    **Explanation:** The legislature is described as the "Law-making Branch" with the "Primary Function: To make laws (statutes) for the society."

37. **The executive branch of government is responsible for**
    A) implementing and enforcing laws.
    B) adjudicating legal disputes.
    C) making constitutional amendments.
    D) selecting its own members from the judiciary.
    E) declaring laws unconstitutional.

    **Answer:** A) implementing and enforcing laws.
    **Explanation:** The primary function of the executive branch is "To implement, execute, and enforce the laws made by the legislature."

38. **The system where each branch of government can limit the powers of the others is known as**
    A) fusion of powers.
    B) rule of law.
    C) checks and balances.
    D) popular sovereignty.
    E) unitary government.

    **Answer:** C) checks and balances.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that under checks and balances, "the branches are also designed to check and balance each other's powers to prevent any one branch from becoming dominant."

39. **The presidential power to reject a bill passed by the legislature is called a**
    A) veto.
    B) pardon.
    C) decree.
    D) filibuster.
    E) statute.

    **Answer:** A) veto.
    **Explanation:** As an example of checks and balances, the text mentions that the "President can veto" laws passed by the legislature.

40. **The section of a constitution that guarantees basic rights and freedoms is often called a**
    A) Preamble.
    B) Bill of Rights.
    C) Schedule.
    D) Manifesto.
    E) Code of Conduct.

    **Answer:** B) Bill of Rights.
    **Explanation:** Most modern constitutions include a "Bill of Rights or Chapter on Fundamental Rights" to guarantee basic freedoms.

41. **The right to a fair hearing, including the presumption of innocence, is a \_\_\_\_\_ right.**
    A) political
    B) social
    C) economic
    D) civil
    E) cultural

    **Answer:** D) civil
    **Explanation:** The "Right to a Fair Hearing (Due Process)" is listed as a common fundamental right, falling under the category of civil rights and liberties.

42. **The power of a court to determine if a law is consistent with the constitution is called**
    A) judicial activism.
    B) judicial review.
    C) legislative oversight.
    D) executive order.
    E) constitutional amendment.

    **Answer:** B) judicial review.
    **Explanation:** Judicial review is defined as "The power of a court...to examine laws...and to determine whether they are constitutional."

43. **A key difference between civil liberties and civil rights is that civil liberties typically**
    A) require government action to provide them.
    B) protect individuals from government interference.
    C) apply only to citizens.
    D) relate to economic issues only.
    E) are granted by the executive.

    **Answer:** B) protect individuals from government interference.
    **Explanation:** Civil liberties are described as "Freedoms that protect individuals from unwarranted government interference (often 'negative rights' - freedom from)."

44. **Which of the following is NOT a source of an unwritten constitution?**
    A) Statutes
    B) Conventions
    C) A single codified document
    D) Judicial decisions
    E) Historical charters

    **Answer:** C) A single codified document
    **Explanation:** The defining feature of an unwritten constitution is that its principles are NOT contained in a single document but are found in various other sources like statutes, conventions, and case law.

45. **Which body is responsible for interpreting the constitution and resolving constitutional disputes?**
    A) The Executive
    B) The Legislature
    C) The Civil Service
    D) The Judiciary
    E) The Electoral Commission

    **Answer:** D) The Judiciary
    **Explanation:** Constitutional interpretation is "Primarily the role of the judiciary (courts), particularly the highest court (e.g., Supreme Court)."

---

### **Chapter 4: Executive Branch and Administrative Structures**

46. **In a presidential system, the President is both Head of State and**
    A) Head of the Legislature.
    B) Head of Government.
    C) Chief Justice.
    D) Speaker of the House.
    E) Head of the Civil Service Commission.

    **Answer:** B) Head of Government.
    **Explanation:** The text states that the President in a presidential system is "Usually both Head of State...and Head of Government."

47. **In a parliamentary system, the Cabinet is typically drawn from the**
    A) judiciary.
    B) civil service.
    C) business community.
    D) legislature.
    E) general public through a separate election.

    **Answer:** D) legislature.
    **Explanation:** In parliamentary systems, "cabinet ministers are typically Members of Parliament (MPs) from the governing party/coalition."

48. **The collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive and senior ministers is the**
    A) Legislature.
    B) Judiciary.
    C) Cabinet.
    D) Civil Service.
    E) National Security Council.

    **Answer:** C) Cabinet.
    **Explanation:** The Cabinet is defined as "A collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive...and senior government ministers."

49. **The principle that all cabinet members must publicly support government decisions is known as**
    A) individual responsibility.
    B) collective responsibility.
    C) separation of powers.
    D) judicial independence.
    E) bureaucratic neutrality.

    **Answer:** B) collective responsibility.
    **Explanation:** Collective Responsibility is the principle where "Cabinet members are expected to publicly support government decisions, even if they privately disagreed."

50. **The operational arm of the executive branch responsible for implementing laws and policies is the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Legislature.
    C) Bureaucracy.
    D) Cabinet.
    E) Political Party.

    **Answer:** C) Bureaucracy.
    **Explanation:** The bureaucracy (or public administration) is described as "The operational arm of the executive branch" responsible for day-to-day implementation.

51. **According to Max Weber's model, an ideal bureaucracy should be characterized by**
    A) political patronage.
    B) merit-based appointment.
    C) personal favoritism.
    D) inconsistent rules.
    E) verbal commands only.

    **Answer:** B) merit-based appointment.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Merit-based Appointment and Promotion" as a key characteristic of Weber's ideal model of bureaucracy.

52. **The largest administrative units in a government, each headed by a minister, are called**
    A) Agencies.
    B) Bureaus.
    C) Councils.
    D) Ministries.
    E) Committees.

    **Answer:** D) Ministries.
    **Explanation:** Ministries (or Departments in some systems) are described as "The largest administrative units, each headed by a Minister."

53. **The main purpose of local government is to**
    A) conduct foreign policy.
    B) command the national army.
    C) bring government closer to the people and address local needs.
    D) print national currency.
    E) amend the national constitution.

    **Answer:** C) bring government closer to the people and address local needs.
    **Explanation:** The text states the purpose of local government is "To bring government closer to the people, allow for local participation...and address local needs."

54. **The transfer of powers and resources to democratically elected local governments is called**
    A) Deconcentration.
    B) Delegation.
    C) Devolution.
    D) Centralization.
    E) Privatization.

    **Answer:** C) Devolution.
    **Explanation:** Devolution is defined as the "strongest form" of decentralization, involving the transfer of "powers, functions, and resources to democratically elected local governments."

55. **Which of the following is a typical function of local government?**
    A) National defense
    B) Waste management and collection
    C) Foreign diplomacy
    D) Central banking
    E) Supreme Court appointments

    **Answer:** B) Waste management and collection
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Waste management and collection" as a typical function of local government, along with primary education, local roads, etc.

56. **The principle that civil servants should serve the government of the day loyally, regardless of its political party, is known as**
    A) political activism.
    B) political neutrality.
    C) political patronage.
    D) political socialization.
    E) political ambition.

    **Answer:** B) political neutrality.
    **Explanation:** A key characteristic of an ideal civil service is "Political Neutrality and Impartiality," meaning they serve the government impartially.

57. **An independent official who investigates citizen complaints about government maladministration is the**
    A) President.
    B) Prime Minister.
    C) Auditor-General.
    D) Ombudsman.
    E) Chief Justice.

    **Answer:** D) Ombudsman.
    **Explanation:** The Ombudsman is defined as "An independent official who investigates complaints from citizens about maladministration or injustice by government agencies."

58. **The Auditor-General is primarily responsible for**
    A) prosecuting criminals.
    B) auditing government accounts and expenditures.
    C) commanding the police force.
    D) appointing ministers.
    E) making laws.

    **Answer:** B) auditing government accounts and expenditures.
    **Explanation:** The Auditor-General is an independent body that "audits government accounts and expenditures to ensure financial propriety and value for money."

59. **Which of the following is NOT a mechanism for ensuring executive accountability?**
    A) Legislative oversight
    B) Judicial review
    C) Regular elections
    D) Freedom of the press
    E) Absolute immunity from prosecution

    **Answer:** E) Absolute immunity from prosecution
    **Explanation:** Absolute immunity would prevent accountability. The other options (oversight, review, elections, free press) are all key mechanisms for holding the executive accountable.

60. **"Red tape" in bureaucracy refers to**
    A) efficient service delivery.
    B) high levels of transparency.
    C) a fast-track system for important issues.
    D) slow, inefficient processes caused by excessive rules.
    E) merit-based promotion.

    **Answer:** D) slow, inefficient processes caused by excessive rules.
    **Explanation:** "Red tape" is a common criticism of bureaucracy, referring to its tendency to be "slow, inefficient...rigid" due to complex rules and procedures.

---

### **Chapter 6: Judiciary and Legal System**

61. **The highest court in most judicial systems is called the**
    A) High Court.
    B) Magistrate Court.
    C) Court of Appeal.
    D) Supreme Court.
    E) Customary Court.

    **Answer:** D) Supreme Court.
    **Explanation:** The Supreme Court is described as the "Apex Court" or "Court of Last Resort," making it the highest court in the judicial hierarchy.

62. **The primary function of a Court of Appeal is to**
    A) conduct new trials with new evidence.
    B) hear cases for the first time.
    C) review decisions of lower courts for errors of law.
    D) handle minor criminal offenses.
    E) adjudicate cases based on local customs.

    **Answer:** C) review decisions of lower courts for errors of law.
    **Explanation:** Courts of Appeal "review the record of the lower court proceedings to determine if errors of law or procedure occurred."

63. **Judicial independence is crucial because it**
    A) allows judges to be influenced by politicians.
    B) ensures judges can apply the law without fear or favor.
    C) makes the judiciary subordinate to the executive.
    D) allows judges to ignore the constitution.
    E) speeds up all court cases.

    **Answer:** B) ensures judges can apply the law without fear or favor.
    **Explanation:** The importance of judicial independence is that it "Ensures judges can apply the law fairly and impartially, without fear or favor."

64. **Security of tenure for judges means they**
    A) are re-elected every year.
    B) can be easily removed for unpopular decisions.
    C) are appointed for a fixed period and cannot be easily dismissed.
    D) are members of the executive branch.
    E) must be approved by the police.

    **Answer:** C) are appointed for a fixed period and cannot be easily dismissed.
    **Explanation:** Security of tenure means judges "should not be easily removed from office," often being appointed until a mandatory retirement age, to protect them from political pressure.

65. **Criminal law deals with offenses against**
    A) private individuals only.
    B) contracts and property.
    C) the state or society as a whole.
    D) international organizations.
    E) family matters.

    **Answer:** C) the state or society as a whole.
    **Explanation:** Criminal law is defined as dealing with "offenses committed against the state or society as a whole (crimes)."

66. **In a criminal case, the standard of proof required for a conviction is**
    A) beyond a reasonable doubt.
    B) on the balance of probabilities.
    C) a gut feeling.
    D) preponderance of the evidence.
    E) absolute certainty.

    **Answer:** A) beyond a reasonable doubt.
    **Explanation:** The text states that in criminal law, guilt must be proven "beyond a reasonable doubt," which is a high standard.

67. **A legal dispute between two individuals over a contract is an example of**
    A) criminal law.
    B) constitutional law.
    C) administrative law.
    D) civil law.
    E) international law.

    **Answer:** D) civil law.
    **Explanation:** Civil law deals with "disputes between private individuals, organizations, or entities," and contract disputes are a classic example.

68. **Sharia law is a legal system derived from**
    A) Roman law.
    B) English common law.
    C) Islamic religious sources.
    D) French civil code.
    E) Traditional African customs.

    **Answer:** C) Islamic religious sources.
    **Explanation:** Sharia law is defined as "A legal system derived from Islamic religious sources, primarily the Quran and the Sunnah."

69. **A process where a neutral third party helps disputing parties reach their own agreement is called**
    A) Litigation.
    B) Arbitration.
    C) Mediation.
    D) Adjudication.
    E) Prosecution.

    **Answer:** C) Mediation.
    **Explanation:** In mediation, "a neutral mediator helps disputing parties reach a mutually acceptable agreement...but does not make a binding decision."

70. **The main difference between mediation and arbitration is that an arbitrator's decision is**
    A) non-binding.
    B) always a compromise.
    C) generally legally binding.
    D) made by the parties themselves.
    E) only an opinion.

    **Answer:** C) generally legally binding.
    **Explanation:** In arbitration, the arbitrator "make[s] a binding decision (an award)," which is generally legally enforceable, unlike in mediation where the mediator facilitates a non-binding agreement.

71. **Which of the following courts typically has original jurisdiction over serious criminal cases like murder?**
    A) Magistrate Court
    B) High Court
    C) Small Claims Court
    D) Customary Court
    E) Court of Appeal

    **Answer:** B) High Court
    **Explanation:** High Courts are described as having jurisdiction over "serious criminal cases (e.g., felonies like murder, robbery, treason)."

72. **Customary law is based on**
    A) the national constitution.
    B) acts of parliament.
    C) long-established traditions and norms of a community.
    D) decisions of the Supreme Court.
    E) international treaties.

    **Answer:** C) long-established traditions and norms of a community.
    **Explanation:** Customary law is defined as being "based on the long-established customs, traditions, and social norms of a particular indigenous community."

73. **Alternative Dispute Resolution (ADR) mechanisms are often preferred to court litigation because they are**
    A) more formal and complex.
    B) always more expensive.
    C) generally faster and less adversarial.
    D) available only for criminal cases.
    E) guaranteed to be public.

    **Answer:** C) generally faster and less adversarial.
    **Explanation:** The text lists advantages of ADR as being "quicker, less expensive, more flexible, and less adversarial than going to court."

74. **In a civil case, the party bringing the lawsuit is called the**
    A) Defendant.
    B) Prosecutor.
    C) Plaintiff.
    D) Judge.
    E) Witness.

    **Answer:** C) Plaintiff.
    **Explanation:** Civil law deals with disputes between a "plaintiff vs. defendant." The plaintiff is the one who initiates the case.

75. **The financial security of judges is important for judicial independence because it**
    A) makes them wealthy.
    B) allows politicians to control their salaries.
    C) prevents them from being pressured through their pay.
    D) requires them to pay for court expenses.
    E) allows them to take bribes.

    **Answer:** C) prevents them from being pressured through their pay.
    **Explanation:** Financial security, where judges' salaries are secure and not subject to arbitrary reduction, is a mechanism to "prevent financial pressure" and ensure independence.

---

### **Chapter 7: Electoral Processes and Systems**

76. **The First-Past-the-Post (FPTP) electoral system is also known as the**
    A) Proportional Representation system.
    B) Two-Round system.
    C) Winner-Takes-All system.
    D) Mixed Member system.
    E) Alternative Vote system.

    **Answer:** C) Winner-Takes-All system.
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the First-Past-the-Post (FPTP) system is also referred to as a "Plurality System or Winner-Takes-All."

77. **A major disadvantage of the FPTP system is that it can**
    A) lead to unstable coalition governments.
    B) give smaller parties fair representation.
    C) result in a party winning a majority of seats without a majority of votes.
    D) be very complex for voters to understand.
    E) ensure every vote counts towards representation.

    **Answer:** C) result in a party winning a majority of seats without a majority of votes.
    **Explanation:** A key disadvantage of FPTP is that it "Often results in disproportionate representation: a party can win a majority of seats without winning a majority of the national vote."

78. **Proportional Representation (PR) systems are designed to**
    A) ensure a single-party government.
    B) allocate seats to parties in proportion to their vote share.
    C) elect one representative per constituency.
    D) make it difficult for small parties to win seats.
    E) simplify the vote counting process.

    **Answer:** B) allocate seats to parties in proportion to their vote share.
    **Explanation:** PR systems "Aim to allocate seats to political parties in direct proportion to their share of the national vote."

79. **A key advantage of the Proportional Representation system is**
    A) its simplicity.
    B) the creation of stable, single-party governments.
    C) the strong link between a representative and a specific constituency.
    D) fairer representation for smaller parties and minority groups.
    E) the elimination of political parties.

    **Answer:** D) fairer representation for smaller parties and minority groups.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Fairer representation of parties' share of the vote" and "Better representation for smaller parties and minority groups" as key advantages of PR.

80. **The process by which eligible citizens are enrolled on an official list to vote is called**
    A) Voter campaigning.
    B) Voter registration.
    C) Voter education.
    D) Voter monitoring.
    E) Voter collation.

    **Answer:** B) Voter registration.
    **Explanation:** Voter registration is defined as "the process by which eligible citizens are enrolled on an official list (voters' register or electoral roll)."

81. **The independent body responsible for organizing and supervising elections in a country is the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Executive.
    C) Legislature.
    D) Electoral Management Body (EMB).
    E) National Security Council.

    **Answer:** D) Electoral Management Body (EMB).
    **Explanation:** The Electoral Management Body (EMB), such as an Electoral Commission, is the "independent or autonomous body...responsible for organizing, conducting, and supervising elections."

82. **A major barrier to women's participation in politics mentioned in the text is**
    A) lack of interest in public affairs.
    B) constitutional prohibition.
    C) socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes.
    D) the absence of political parties.
    E) low population of women.

    **Answer:** C) socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes that assign women to domestic roles" as a key barrier to women's political participation.

83. **A two-round electoral system is used to ensure the winner achieves**
    A) a plurality of votes.
    B) a fixed number of votes.
    C) an absolute majority of votes.
    D) the support of all parties.
    E) the approval of the incumbent.

    **Answer:** C) an absolute majority of votes.
    **Explanation:** A majority system like the two-round system ensures that a candidate "must win an absolute majority (more than 50%) of votes to be elected," often by holding a runoff if no one achieves it in the first round.

84. **Which of the following is NOT a function of an Electoral Management Body?**
    A) Voter registration
    B) Delimitation of constituencies
    C) Announcing official results
    D) Formulating party manifestos
    E) Civic and voter education

    **Answer:** D) Formulating party manifestos
    **Explanation:** Formulating manifestos is a function of political parties. The EMB is responsible for the administration of the election process itself.

85. **The observation of an election by independent domestic and international groups is called**
    A) Election rigging.
    B) Election campaigning.
    C) Election monitoring.
    D) Election petition.
    E) Election financing.

    **Answer:** C) Election monitoring.
    **Explanation:** Election monitoring (or observation) is defined as "The observation of an election process by independent domestic and/or international observers to assess its fairness."

86. **Reserved seats or party list quotas are strategies used to enhance the political participation of**
    A) the elderly.
    B) the military.
    C) women.
    D) the judiciary.
    E) civil servants.

    **Answer:** C) women.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Quotas (reserved seats or party list quotas) for women in legislatures" as a strategy to enhance women's political participation.

87. **The secrecy of the ballot is essential in an election to**
    A) make counting difficult.
    B) ensure voters can make free choices without intimidation.
    C) help the ruling party win.
    D) allow for multiple voting.
    E) identify who voted for whom.

    **Answer:** B) ensure voters can make free choices without intimidation.
    **Explanation:** The principle of a secret ballot is fundamental to democratic elections as it protects voters from coercion and allows them to express their true preference.

88. **A system that combines elements of both FPTP and PR is known as a**
    A) Unitary system.
    B) Direct system.
    C) Mixed electoral system.
    D) Unicameral system.
    E) Plurality system.

    **Answer:** C) Mixed electoral system.
    **Explanation:** Mixed Electoral Systems are defined as those that "Combine elements of both plurality/majority systems (like FPTP) and proportional representation systems."

89. **A major barrier to youth participation in politics is often**
    A) lack of physical strength.
    B) constitutional bans on youth voting.
    C) the perception of politics as being dominated by older generations.
    D) the absence of social media.
    E) youth having no opinions.

    **Answer:** C) the perception of politics as being dominated by older generations.
    **Explanation:** The text lists the "Perception of politics as corrupt or dominated by older generations" as a significant barrier for youth.

90. **In an election, a political campaign is primarily designed to**
    A) count votes accurately.
    B) persuade voters to support a candidate or party.
    C) register eligible voters.
    D) monitor the fairness of the poll.
    E) adjudicate electoral disputes.

    **Answer:** B) persuade voters to support a candidate or party.
    **Explanation:** Political campaigning is defined as "The process by which political parties and candidates try to persuade voters to support them in an election."

---

### **Chapter 8: Political Parties and Interest Groups**

91. **A primary difference between a political party and an interest group is that a political party**
    A) seeks to influence policy without controlling government.
    B) is not interested in elections.
    C) seeks to win elections and control government.
    D) focuses only on a single issue.
    E) is usually smaller and less organized.

    **Answer:** C) seeks to win elections and control government.
    **Explanation:** Political parties "seek to win elections and control government," whereas interest groups primarily aim to influence policy from the outside.

92. **Which of the following is a key function of political parties?**
    A) Adjudicating legal cases
    B) Commanding the armed forces
    C) Recruiting and nominating candidates for office
    D) Maintaining political neutrality
    E) Collecting taxes

    **Answer:** C) Recruiting and nominating candidates for office
    **Explanation:** "Recruit and Nominate Candidates" is listed as a primary function of political parties.

93. **A document outlining a political party's policies and programs is its**
    A) Constitution.
    B) Budget.
    C) Platform or Manifesto.
    D) Court ruling.
    E) Civil service code.

    **Answer:** C) Platform or Manifesto.
    **Explanation:** Parties "Formulate Policies and Programs (Party Platform/Manifesto)" to present their goals to the electorate.

94. **An interest group representing the Nigerian Medical Association or a Bar Association is an example of a(n)**
    A) Business group.
    B) Labor union.
    C) Professional association.
    D) Single-issue group.
    E) Ethnic group.

    **Answer:** C) Professional association.
    **Explanation:** Professional associations are defined as groups that "Represent members of specific professions (e.g., medical associations, bar associations for lawyers)."

95. **Lobbying is a technique used by interest groups to**
    A) directly run for office.
    B) influence policymakers.
    C) count votes in an election.
    D) provide social welfare to the poor.
    E) protest violently.

    **Answer:** B) influence policymakers.
    **Explanation:** Interest groups (or pressure groups) use lobbying techniques to "influence public policy and government decisions." Direct lobbying involves direct contact with policymakers.

96. **The media's role in scrutinizing government actions and exposing corruption is known as its**
    A) entertainment role.
    B) advertising role.
    C) watchdog role.
    D) socialization role.
    E) mobilization role.

    **Answer:** C) watchdog role.
    **Explanation:** The media's "Watchdog Role" involves "Investigating and exposing corruption, mismanagement, or abuses of power by government officials."

97. **The "third sector" of society, distinct from government and business, is called**
    A) Civil society.
    B) The judiciary.
    C) The electorate.
    D) The bureaucracy.
    E) The cabinet.

    **Answer:** A) Civil society.
    **Explanation:** Civil society is defined as "the 'third sector' of society, distinct from government (state) and business (market)," comprising various non-governmental organizations.

98. **Grassroots lobbying involves**
    A) secretly meeting with high-level officials.
    B) mobilizing the public and group members to contact policymakers.
    C) filing lawsuits in the Supreme Court.
    D) donating large sums of money to campaigns.
    E) writing academic articles.

    **Answer:** B) mobilizing the public and group members to contact policymakers.
    **Explanation:** Grassroots lobbying is an indirect technique that involves "Mobilizing their members and the public to contact policymakers."

99. **Laws that regulate the sources and amounts of funding for political campaigns are known as**
    A) Electoral system laws.
    B) Campaign finance regulations.
    C) Party registration laws.
    D) Media ethics codes.
    E) Lobbying disclosure acts.

    **Answer:** B) Campaign finance regulations.
    **Explanation:** Campaign finance regulation refers to laws designed to "regulate campaign finance, which may include...limits on contributions...disclosure of donors."

100. **Spreading false rumors and engaging in character assassination during an election are examples of**
    A) effective campaign strategy.
    B) voter education.
    C) unethical campaign practices.
    D) interest articulation.
    E) policy formulation.

    **Answer:** C) unethical campaign practices.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "spreading false rumors, character assassination" as unethical practices that violate principles of honesty and fairness in elections.

101. **Which of the following is NOT a type of economic interest group?**
    A) Labor unions
    B) Business groups
    C) Agricultural groups
    D) Human rights organizations
    E) Professional associations

    **Answer:** D) Human rights organizations
    **Explanation:** Human rights organizations are classified as social or public interest groups, focusing on broad societal values rather than the specific economic interests of their members.

102. **A Political Action Committee (PAC) is primarily involved in**
    A) formulating public policy.
    B) providing legal aid.
    C) grassroots mobilization.
    D) contributing financially to political campaigns.
    E) monitoring elections.

    **Answer:** D) contributing financially to political campaigns.
    **Explanation:** Electioneering, including the use of PACs, is a method used by interest groups for "contributing to campaigns (where legally permitted)."

103. **The media's ability to influence which issues are seen as important by the public is its \_\_\_\_\_ function.**
    A) agenda-setting
    B) watchdog
    C) entertainment
    D) economic
    E) procedural

    **Answer:** A) agenda-setting
    **Explanation:** The agenda-setting function of the media refers to its role in "Influencing which issues are perceived as important by the public and policymakers."

104. **A key ethical principle for political campaigns is**
    A) winning at all costs.
    B) focusing only on an opponent's personal life.
    C) transparency in funding.
    D) discouraging voter turnout.
    E) using state resources for private campaigns.

    **Answer:** C) transparency in funding.
    **Explanation:** Transparency, including "Openness about funding sources," is listed as a key ethical principle in elections.

105. **An interest group focused solely on environmental protection is best described as a**
    A) labor union.
    B) professional association.
    C) business group.
    D) single-issue group.
    E) political party.

    **Answer:** D) single-issue group.
    **Explanation:** Single-issue groups "Focus intensely on one specific issue," and environmental protection is a classic example. It also falls under the broader category of a public interest group.

---

### **Chapter 9: Policy‐Making Process and Implementation**

106. **The first stage in the public policy cycle is**
    A) Policy evaluation.
    B) Policy implementation.
    C) Agenda setting.
    D) Policy adoption.
    E) Budget allocation.

    **Answer:** C) Agenda setting.
    **Explanation:** The policy cycle begins with agenda setting, which is "The process by which certain problems or issues come to the attention of policymakers."

107. **Policy formulation is the stage where**
    A) a law is formally passed.
    B) potential solutions and alternatives are developed.
    C) the success of a policy is measured.
    D) a policy is put into action.
    E) a problem is first identified.

    **Answer:** B) potential solutions and alternatives are developed.
    **Explanation:** Policy formulation is defined as "The development of potential solutions or alternative courses of action (policy options)."

108. **When a legislature passes a law, it is engaging in which stage of the policy cycle?**
    A) Policy formulation
    B) Policy adoption
    C) Policy implementation
    D) Agenda setting
    E) Policy evaluation

    **Answer:** B) Policy adoption
    **Explanation:** Policy adoption is the stage where a policy is "formally chosen and authorized," and the "Passage of a law by the legislature" is a key mechanism for this.

109. **The primary responsibility for policy implementation lies with the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Electorate.
    C) Media.
    D) Bureaucracy.
    E) Interest groups.

    **Answer:** D) Bureaucracy.
    **Explanation:** The bureaucracy (civil service, government agencies) is described as the "Primary implementers" of public policy.

110. **Which of the following is a major challenge to effective policy implementation?**
    A) Clear policy objectives
    B) Strong political will
    C) High levels of public support
    D) Insufficient resources
    E) Excellent coordination

    **Answer:** D) Insufficient resources
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Insufficient Resources: Lack of adequate funding, personnel, or technical capacity" as a key challenge to policy implementation.

111. **The systematic assessment of a policy's effectiveness and impact is called**
    A) Policy formulation.
    B) Policy adoption.
    C) Policy evaluation.
    D) Agenda setting.
    E) Lobbying.

    **Answer:** C) Policy evaluation.
    **Explanation:** Policy evaluation is defined as "The systematic assessment of the effectiveness, efficiency, and impact of a public policy."

112. **A major crisis like a natural disaster or economic recession often serves as a**
    A) policy outcome.
    B) focusing event for agenda setting.
    C) method of policy evaluation.
    D) tool for implementation.
    E) final stage of the policy cycle.

    **Answer:** B) a focusing event for agenda setting.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "Crises and Focusing Events" can "thrust issues onto the agenda," making them a priority for government action.

113. **Cost-benefit analysis is a tool used primarily during**
    A) policy evaluation and formulation.
    B) agenda setting.
    C) political campaigning.
    D) judicial review.
    E) voting.

    **Answer:** A) policy evaluation and formulation.
    **Explanation:** Cost-benefit analysis is used to assess the feasibility of different options during formulation and to evaluate the economic efficiency of an implemented policy.

114. **When a government agency creates specific rules to carry out a broad law passed by the legislature, it is engaged in**
    A) agenda setting.
    B) policy implementation.
    C) election monitoring.
    D) judicial adjudication.
    E) constitutional amendment.

    **Answer:** B) policy implementation.
    **Explanation:** "Developing Rules, Regulations, and Guidelines" is listed as a key activity in the policy implementation stage, translating broad policy into operational procedures.

115. **The feedback loop in the policy cycle connects policy evaluation to**
    A) the end of the process.
    B) judicial punishment.
    C) future agenda setting and policy formulation.
    D) the private sector only.
    E) international diplomacy.

    **Answer:** C) future agenda setting and policy formulation.
    **Explanation:** The text describes the policy process as a cycle where "evaluation and feedback lead to policy review, potential reformulation of problems or solutions, and new rounds of decision-making."

---

### **Chapter 10: Human Rights, Civil Liberties, and Social Justice**

116. **"Freedom from" external interference, such as censorship or arbitrary arrest, is known as**
    A) Positive liberty.
    B) Negative liberty.
    C) Equality of outcome.
    D) Distributive justice.
    E) Social welfare.

    **Answer:** B) Negative liberty.
    **Explanation:** Negative liberty is defined as "Freedom from external interference, constraints, or oppression."

117. **The idea that all individuals should have an equal chance to succeed, regardless of their background, is**
    A) Equality of outcome.
    B) Equality of opportunity.
    C) Formal equality.
    D) Retributive justice.
    E) Positive liberty.

    **Answer:** B) Equality of opportunity.
    **Explanation:** Equality of opportunity is the principle that "All individuals should have an equal chance to succeed in life, regardless of their background."

118. **The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) was adopted by the**
    A) African Union.
    B) European Union.
    C) United Nations General Assembly.
    D) World Bank.
    E) League of Nations.

    **Answer:** C) United Nations General Assembly.
    **Explanation:** The text clearly states that the UDHR was "Adopted by the United Nations General Assembly on December 10, 1948."

119. **A unique feature of the African Charter on Human and Peoples' Rights is its recognition of**
    A) only economic rights.
    B) only individual rights.
    C) individual rights, peoples' rights, and individual duties.
    D) the right to own weapons.
    E) the supremacy of customary law over all other laws.

    **Answer:** C) individual rights, peoples' rights, and individual duties.
    **Explanation:** The African Charter is described as unique because it "recognizes not only individual rights but also peoples' rights...and individual duties."

120. **Which of the following is a justifiable limitation on freedom of speech?**
    A) Criticism of the government
    B) Unpopular opinions
    C) Incitement to violence
    D) Religious expression
    E) Academic debate

    **Answer:** C) Incitement to violence
    **Explanation:** Freedom of speech is not absolute and can be limited for reasons like national security or public order, with "incitement to violence" being a classic example.

121. **The right to form and join groups like political parties and trade unions is the freedom of**
    A) speech.
    B) religion.
    C) assembly.
    D) association.
    E) movement.

    **Answer:** D) association.
    **Explanation:** Freedom of association is defined as "The right of individuals to form and join groups or organizations."

122. **Amnesty International and Human Rights Watch are examples of**
    A) government agencies.
    B) international financial institutions.
    C) political parties.
    D) non-governmental organizations (NGOs).
    E) intergovernmental organizations.

    **Answer:** D) non-governmental organizations (NGOs).
    **Explanation:** These are cited as examples of international human rights NGOs that operate independently of government.

123. **A National Human Rights Institution (NHRI) is a(n)**
    A) private company focused on profit.
    B) international court for all crimes.
    C) independent public body established by a state to protect human rights.
    D) political party focused on human rights issues.
    E) armed rebel group.

    **Answer:** C) independent public body established by a state to protect human rights.
    **Explanation:** NHRIs are defined as "Independent public bodies established by government...with a mandate to promote and protect human rights within a country."

124. **The concept of social justice is primarily concerned with**
    A) punishing criminals severely.
    B) fairness and equity in the distribution of resources and opportunities.
    C) ensuring maximum profit for businesses.
    D) the freedom of individuals from all government control.
    E) maintaining traditional social hierarchies.

    **Answer:** B) fairness and equity in the distribution of resources and opportunities.
    **Explanation:** Social justice aims for a society where "all individuals and groups have equal access to rights, opportunities, and resources, and are treated with dignity and fairness."

125. **The International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights (ICCPR) is a**
    A) non-binding declaration.
    B) legally binding treaty.
    C) regional African charter.
    D) national constitution.
    E) political party manifesto.

    **Answer:** B) legally binding treaty.
    **Explanation:** The ICCPR is described as a "legally binding treaty that translate[s] UDHR principles into enforceable obligations for states that ratify" it.

---

### **Chapter 11: Theories of International Relations**

126. **Which IR theory views the international system as anarchic and states as being primarily motivated by the pursuit of power and security?**
    A) Liberalism
    B) Constructivism
    C) Realism
    D) Marxism
    E) Feminism

    **Answer:** C) Realism
    **Explanation:** Realism's core assumptions are that the international system is anarchic and that states' primary goal is survival, which they pursue by maximizing power.

127. **Liberalism in IR emphasizes the importance of \_\_\_\_\_ in promoting peace.**
    A) balance of power.
    B) military strength.
    C) international institutions, democracy, and economic interdependence.
    D) class struggle.
    E) national interest defined as power.

    **Answer:** C) international institutions, democracy, and economic interdependence.
    **Explanation:** Liberalism focuses on how international law, organizations, trade (interdependence), and democratic values can foster cooperation and peace.

128. **The idea that "anarchy is what states make of it" is central to which IR theory?**
    A) Realism
    B) Liberalism
    C) Constructivism
    D) Marxism
    E) Post-modernism

    **Answer:** C) Constructivism
    **Explanation:** The text directly quotes Alexander Wendt's famous constructivist phrase, "Anarchy is what states make of it," to highlight the theory's focus on socially constructed ideas and norms.

129. **Marxist IR theory focuses on**
    A) the balance of military power.
    B) shared norms and identities.
    C) global capitalist exploitation and class struggle.
    D) the role of international law.
    E) the peaceful nature of democracies.

    **Answer:** C) global capitalist exploitation and class struggle.
    **Explanation:** Marxism is defined by its focus on "economic and material aspects...particularly class struggle and capitalist exploitation on a global scale."

130. **The ability of a state to influence others through the appeal of its culture and values is known as**
    A) Hard power.
    B) Smart power.
    C) Economic power.
    D) Soft power.
    E) Military power.

    **Answer:** D) Soft power.
    **Explanation:** Joseph Nye's concept of soft power is defined as the "Ability to attract and co-opt rather than coerce; based on appeal of culture, political values, foreign policies."

131. **The principle of sovereignty implies that a state**
    A) is subject to the authority of a higher power.
    B) has supreme authority over its own internal affairs.
    C) must follow the domestic laws of other states.
    D) has no defined territory.
    E) can be run by an international organization.

    **Answer:** B) has supreme authority over its own internal affairs.
    **Explanation:** Sovereignty is defined as the "supreme and independent authority of a state over its territory and population, free from external interference in its internal affairs."

122. **A state's goals and objectives in its interactions with other states is its**
    A) National interest.
    B) Constitution.
    C) Social contract.
    D) Public policy.
    E) Diplomatic immunity.

    **Answer:** A) National interest.
    **Explanation:** National interest is defined as "The goals, objectives, and ambitions of a state in its interactions with other states" which guides its foreign policy.

133. **The practice of conducting negotiations and managing relations between states is called**
    A) War.
    B) Diplomacy.
    C) Espionage.
    D) Sanctions.
    E) Intervention.

    **Answer:** B) Diplomacy.
    **Explanation:** Diplomacy is defined as "The art and practice of conducting negotiations and managing relations between states."

134. **NATO is an example of a(n)**
    A) international financial institution.
    B) defensive alliance.
    C) global government.
    D) single-issue interest group.
    E) regional trade bloc.

    **Answer:** B) defensive alliance.
    **Explanation:** NATO is cited as an example of a defensive alliance, where "Members pledge to come to each other's aid if one is attacked."

135. **The increasing interconnectedness of societies worldwide, driven by technology and trade, is known as**
    A) Nationalism.
    B) Isolationism.
    C) Globalization.
    D) Regionalism.
    E) Colonialism.

    **Answer:** C) Globalization.
    **Explanation:** Globalization is defined as the "process characterized by increasing interconnectedness and interdependence between countries and societies worldwide."

136. **Feminist IR theory primarily critiques traditional theories for**
    A) being too focused on economics.
    B) ignoring the role of gender in shaping international relations.
    C) being too optimistic about peace.
    D) overemphasizing international law.
    E) neglecting the state as an actor.

    **Answer:** B) ignoring the role of gender in shaping international relations.
    **Explanation:** A core assumption of Feminist IR is that traditional theories "have often ignored or marginalized women's experiences...and have been based on masculine assumptions."

137. **A situation where states form coalitions to prevent any single state from becoming too powerful is a**
    A) collective security system.
    B) world government.
    C) balance of power.
    D) global empire.
    E) unipolar system.

    **Answer:** C) balance of power.
    **Explanation:** The balance of power concept describes a situation where states "strive to ensure that no single state or bloc becomes overwhelmingly powerful." Forming alliances (external balancing) is a key mechanism.

138. **The principle of non-intervention is a key aspect of**
    A) Globalization.
    B) Sovereignty.
    C) Humanitarianism.
    D) Internationalism.
    E) Dependency.

    **Answer:** B) Sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** The text lists non-intervention ("Other states should not interfere in the domestic affairs of a sovereign state") as a key aspect of Westphalian sovereignty.

139. **According to realist theory, the most important national interest is**
    A) promoting human rights.
    B) economic prosperity.
    C) survival and security.
    D) spreading democratic values.
    E) environmental protection.

    **Answer:** C) survival and security.
    **Explanation:** The text notes that "Survival and Security" is the "primary interest for Realists."

140. **A major political implication of globalization is that it**
    A) strengthens state sovereignty.
    B) challenges state sovereignty.
    C) eliminates international organizations.
    D) stops all international trade.
    E) promotes isolationism.

    **Answer:** B) challenges state sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** One of the key political implications of globalization discussed is that it "Challenges to State Sovereignty" through the influence of IOs, international law, and transnational actors.

---

### **Chapter 12: International Organizations and Treaties**

141. **The principal organ of the UN responsible for maintaining international peace and security is the**
    A) General Assembly.
    B) Security Council.
    C) International Court of Justice.
    D) Secretariat.
    E) ECOSOC.

    **Answer:** B) Security Council.
    **Explanation:** The UN Security Council has the "Primary responsibility for maintaining international peace and security."

142. **The veto power in the UN Security Council is held exclusively by the**
    A) 10 non-permanent members.
    B) Secretary-General.
    C) 5 permanent members.
    D) President of the General Assembly.
    E) Judges of the ICJ.

    **Answer:** C) 5 permanent members.
    **Explanation:** The five permanent members (P5: China, France, Russia, UK, US) have veto power, meaning a negative vote from any of them can block a resolution.

143. **The UN General Assembly operates on the principle of**
    A) weighted voting based on financial contribution.
    B) one state, one vote.
    C) permanent member veto power.
    D) qualified majority voting.
    E) consensus only.

    **Answer:** B) one state, one vote.
    **Explanation:** The General Assembly is where "All member states have equal representation (one state, one vote)."

144. **Which regional organization aims to promote economic integration and has a peacekeeping force in West Africa?**
    A) European Union (EU)
    B) African Union (AU)
    C) Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN)
    D) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS)
E) North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO)

    **Answer:** D) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS)
    **Explanation:** ECOWAS is the regional organization for West Africa, noted for its focus on economic integration and its history of peacekeeping (e.g., ECOMOG).

145. **The process by which a state gives its formal approval to a treaty, making it legally binding, is called**
    A) Signature.
    B) Negotiation.
    C) Adoption.
    D) Ratification.
    E) Proclamation.

    **Answer:** D) Ratification.
    **Explanation:** Ratification is the "formal approval by a state according to its constitutional procedures, making it legally binding on that state."

146. **The Geneva Conventions are international treaties primarily concerned with**
    A) international trade.
    B) environmental protection.
    C) the laws of war and protection of civilians.
    D) the non-proliferation of nuclear weapons.
    E) the law of the sea.

    **Answer:** C) the laws of war and protection of civilians.
    **Explanation:** The Geneva Conventions are a key set of treaties in international humanitarian law, dealing with the treatment of non-combatants and prisoners during armed conflict.

147. **The international norm that states have a responsibility to protect their populations from atrocities is known as**
    A) Pacta sunt servanda.
    B) Responsibility to Protect (R2P).
    C) The Paris Agreement.
    D) The UN Charter.
    E) Humanitarian neutrality.

    **Answer:** B) Responsibility to Protect (R2P).
    **Explanation:** R2P is the "emerging international norm...asserting that...States have a primary responsibility to protect their own populations from genocide, war crimes, ethnic cleansing, and crimes against humanity."

148. **UN peacekeeping operations are deployed with the**
    A) consent of the host state.
    B) sole authority of the Secretary-General.
    C) order of the International Court of Justice.
    D) approval of all UN member states.
    E) funding of the World Bank only.

    **Answer:** A) consent of the host state.
    **Explanation:** "Consent of parties" is listed as a core principle of UN peacekeeping.

149. **The African Union (AU) is the successor to the**
    A) League of Nations.
    B) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS).
    C) Organization of African Unity (OAU).
    D) Non-Aligned Movement.
    E) Commonwealth of Nations.

    **Answer:** C) Organization of African Unity (OAU).
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the AU, established in 2002, is the "successor to the Organization of African Unity - OAU, founded 1963."

150. **The principal judicial organ of the United Nations is the**
    A) International Criminal Court (ICC).
    B) European Court of Human Rights.
    C) African Court on Human and Peoples' Rights.
    D) International Court of Justice (ICJ).
    E) UN Security Council.

    **Answer:** D) International Court of Justice (ICJ).
    **Explanation:** The ICJ (or World Court) is defined as "The principal judicial organ of the UN."

151. **A legally binding written agreement between states governed by international law is a(n)**
    A) Resolution.
    B) Declaration.
    C) Treaty.
    D) Communiqué.
    E) Sanction.

    **Answer:** C) Treaty.
    **Explanation:** A treaty is defined as a "Formal, legally binding written agreement between states." Resolutions and declarations are often non-binding.

152. **The Paris Agreement is a major international treaty that deals with**
    A) Human rights.
    B) Trade liberalization.
    C) Arms control.
    D) Climate change.
    E) Internet governance.

    **Answer:** D) Climate change.
    **Explanation:** The text lists the Paris Agreement under the UNFCCC as a key environmental treaty aiming to "limit global warming."

153. **Post-conflict efforts to rebuild institutions and prevent the recurrence of violence are called**
    A) Peacekeeping.
    B) Peacemaking.
    C) Peacebuilding.
    D) Humanitarian intervention.
    E) Sanctions.

    **Answer:** C) Peacebuilding.
    **Explanation:** Peacebuilding is defined as "Post-conflict efforts to rebuild institutions, address root causes of conflict, promote reconciliation, and prevent recurrence of violence."

154. **Which of the following is NOT a principal organ of the UN?**
    A) General Assembly
    B) Security Council
    C) World Health Organization (WHO)
    D) ECOSOC
    E) Secretariat

    **Answer:** C) World Health Organization (WHO)
    **Explanation:** The WHO is a very important UN Specialized Agency, but it is not one of the six principal organs established by the UN Charter. The others listed are principal organs.

155. **The European Union (EU) is best described as a**
    A) military alliance.
    B) loose association for cultural exchange.
    C) regional organization with a deep level of economic and political integration.
    D) global financial institution.
    E) human rights NGO.

    **Answer:** C) a regional organization with a deep level of economic and political integration.
    **Explanation:** The EU is described as a "unique economic and political partnership" with a "deep level of integration," including a single market and supranational institutions.

---

### **Chapter 13: Concepts of Development**

156. **The concept of development that focuses primarily on the increase in a country's economic output (GDP) is**
    A) Human development.
    B) Sustainable development.
    C) Economic growth.
    D) Political development.
    E) Social development.

    **Answer:** C) Economic growth.
    **Explanation:** Economic growth is defined as focusing "primarily on the increase in a country's output of goods and services, typically measured by...GDP."

157. **The Human Development Index (HDI) does NOT include which of the following indicators?**
    A) Life expectancy at birth
    B) Mean years of schooling
    C) Gross National Income (GNI) per capita
    D) Political freedom and human rights
    E) Expected years of schooling

    **Answer:** D) Political freedom and human rights
    **Explanation:** The HDI combines three dimensions: health (life expectancy), education (schooling years), and standard of living (GNI per capita). While human development as a concept includes freedoms, they are not part of the formal HDI calculation.

158. **Sustainable development aims to meet the needs of the present without**
    A) compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
    B) any economic growth.
    C) involving the private sector.
    D) seeking international aid.
    E) addressing social inequality.

    **Answer:** A) compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
    **Explanation:** This is the classic definition of sustainable development from the Brundtland Commission, as cited in the text.

159. **The GINI coefficient is a measure of**
    A) economic growth.
    B) human development.
    C) income or wealth inequality.
    D) absolute poverty.
    E) infrastructure quality.

    **Answer:** C) income or wealth inequality.
    **Explanation:** The GINI coefficient is defined as "A measure of income inequality or wealth inequality within a nation."

160. **A GINI coefficient of 0 represents**
    A) perfect inequality.
    B) perfect equality.
    C) high levels of poverty.
    D) rapid economic growth.
    E) a stable economy.

    **Answer:** B) perfect equality.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that a GINI of 0 signifies "Perfect equality (everyone has the same income/wealth)."

161. **The primary mandate of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) is to**
    A) fund long-term infrastructure projects.
    B) ensure the stability of the international monetary system.
    C) promote free trade exclusively.
    D) provide humanitarian aid during disasters.
    E) eradicate specific diseases.

    **Answer:** B) ensure the stability of the international monetary system.
    **Explanation:** The IMF's primary mandate is "To ensure the stability of the international monetary system," primarily by helping countries with balance of payments problems.

162. **The World Bank's main focus is on**
    A) short-term emergency loans.
    B) global military security.
    C) long-term economic development and poverty reduction.
    D) managing global exchange rates.
    E) regulating international corporations.

    **Answer:** C) long-term economic development and poverty reduction.
    **Explanation:** The World Bank's focus is on "long-term economic development and poverty reduction in developing countries" through loans and grants for projects.

163. **The African Development Bank (AfDB) is a**
    A) global institution for all countries.
    B) private commercial bank.
    C) regional multilateral development finance institution for Africa.
    D) human rights organization.
    E) political union of African states.

    **Answer:** C) regional multilateral development finance institution for Africa.
    **Explanation:** The AfDB is defined as a "regional multilateral development finance institution...to promote the economic development and social progress of African countries."

164. **Policies that a country must implement to receive a loan from the IMF or World Bank are known as**
    A) Grants.
    B) Conditionalities.
    C) Tariffs.
    D) Quotas.
    E) Subsidies.

    **Answer:** B) Conditionalities.
    **Explanation:** Loans from IFIs often come with "conditionalities (policy reforms the country must undertake)," historically known as Structural Adjustment Programs (SAPs).

165. **Which of the following is an example of an infrastructure deficit?**
    A) High literacy rate
    B) Low unemployment
    C) A stable government
    D) Poor road networks and unreliable electricity
    E) High GDP per capita

    **Answer:** D) Poor road networks and unreliable electricity
    **Explanation:** Infrastructure deficit refers to the lack of adequate physical infrastructure, with "Poor transportation networks (roads...)" and "Inadequate energy supply (electricity)" being prime examples.

166. **The Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) measures poverty based on**
    A) income alone.
    B) a person's social class.
    C) deprivations in health, education, and living standards.
    D) the national unemployment rate.
    E) a country's GINI coefficient.

    **Answer:** C) deprivations in health, education, and living standards.
    **Explanation:** The MPI is described as measuring poverty "based on deprivations in multiple dimensions (health, education, living standards) rather than just income."

167. **Which concept of development puts the expansion of people's capabilities and freedoms at its center?**
    A) Economic growth
    B) Human development
    C) Industrial development
    D) Political development
    E) Agrarian development

    **Answer:** B) Human development
    **Explanation:** Human development "focuses on expanding people's choices, capabilities, and freedoms, and improving their well-being."

168. **Which of the following is NOT one of the three core dimensions of sustainable development?**
    A) Economic sustainability
    B) Social sustainability
    C) Environmental sustainability
    D) Military sustainability
    E) None of the above

    **Answer:** D) Military sustainability
    **Explanation:** The three integrated dimensions or pillars of sustainable development are economic, social, and environmental.

169. **Underemployment refers to a situation where people are**
    A) actively seeking work but cannot find any.
    B) working in jobs below their skill level or for fewer hours than they want.
    C) retired from the workforce.
    D) not looking for a job.
    E) working in the public sector.

    **Answer:** B) working in jobs below their skill level or for fewer hours than they want.
    **Explanation:** Underemployment includes "working part-time when they want full-time work, or working in jobs below their skill level."

170. **The Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) provide a global framework for development to be achieved by**
    A) 2025.
    B) 2030.
    C) 2040.
    D) 2050.
    E) 2063.

    **Answer:** B) 2030.
    **Explanation:** The text states the SDGs "provide a global framework for achieving sustainable development by 2030."

---

### **Chapter 14: Public Administration and Leadership**

171. **A common criticism of bureaucracy, referring to its focus on rules over goals, is**
    A) Goal achievement.
    B) Goal displacement.
    C) Goal setting.
    D) Goal orientation.
    E) Goal alignment.

    **Answer:** B) Goal displacement.
    **Explanation:** Goal displacement is a listed criticism of bureaucracy, described as "Focusing on rules and procedures for their own sake rather than achieving the original policy goals."

172. **The leadership theory that focuses on an exchange relationship using rewards and punishments is**
    A) Transformational.
    B) Transactional.
    C) Servant.
    D) Trait.
    E) Situational.

    **Answer:** B) Transactional.
    **Explanation:** Transactional leadership "Focuses on the exchange relationship between leaders and followers...using rewards...and punishments."

173. **A leader who inspires followers with a compelling vision and encourages creativity is best described as**
    A) Transactional.
    B) Laissez-faire.
    C) Autocratic.
    D) Transformational.
    E) Bureaucratic.

    **Answer:** D) Transformational.
    **Explanation:** Transformational leadership involves "Inspirational Motivation (Articulates a compelling vision)" and "Intellectual Stimulation (encourages creativity)."

174. **The core principle of Servant Leadership is**
    A) putting the leader's needs first.
    B) a focus on transactions and exchanges.
    C) putting the well-being of followers and the community first.
    D) maintaining power through coercion.
    E) adherence to rigid rules.

    **Answer:** C) putting the well-being of followers and the community first.
    **Explanation:** Servant leadership "Focuses primarily on the growth and well-being of people and the communities...puts the needs of others first."

175. **Which of the following is a core ethical value in public service?**
    A) Self-interest
    B) Partisan advantage
    C) Integrity
    D) Secrecy
    E) Nepotism

    **Answer:** C) Integrity
    **Explanation:** Integrity, defined as honesty and incorruptibility, is listed as a core ethical value for public servants. The other options are examples of unethical behavior.

176. **The abuse of public office for private gain is known as**
    A) Philanthropy.
    B) Public service.
    C) Accountability.
    D) Corruption.
    E) Transparency.

    **Answer:** D) Corruption.
    **Explanation:** Corruption is defined as "The abuse of entrusted power or public office for private gain."

177. **Showing undue favor to relatives in public appointments is an example of**
    A) Embezzlement.
    B) Bribery.
    C) Nepotism.
    D) Fraud.
    E) Extortion.

    **Answer:** C) Nepotism.
    **Explanation:** Nepotism is a form of corruption defined as "Showing undue favor to relatives or friends in appointments or contracts."

178. **The use of ICT by government to provide services and improve efficiency is called**
    A) E-commerce.
    B) E-learning.
    C) E-government.
    D) E-mail.
    E) E-procurement.

    **Answer:** C) E-government.
    **Explanation:** E-government is defined as "The use of Information and Communication Technologies (ICTs) by government agencies to provide information and services."

179. **A major challenge for the implementation of e-government is the**
    A) Digital divide.
    B) High level of transparency it creates.
    C) Increased efficiency.
    D) Reduction in corruption.
    E) Over-supply of ICT professionals.

    **Answer:** A) Digital divide.
    **Explanation:** The "Digital divide (unequal access to ICTs)" is listed as a key challenge for e-government, as it can exclude citizens without internet access or digital literacy.

180. **New Public Management (NPM) is an administrative reform trend that seeks to**
    A) increase the size of the bureaucracy.
    B) introduce private sector management techniques into the public sector.
    C) centralize all decision-making.
    D) eliminate performance measurement.
    E) reduce transparency and accountability.

    **Answer:** B) introduce private sector management techniques into the public sector.
    **Explanation:** New Public Management (NPM) is described as "Introducing market-based principles and private sector management techniques into public sector."

181. **Protecting individuals who report corruption is a key feature of**
    A) Asset declaration laws.
    B) Whistleblower protection laws.
    C) Freedom of Information acts.
    D) Codes of conduct.
    E) Anti-corruption agencies.

    **Answer:** B) Whistleblower protection laws.
    **Explanation:** Whistleblower protection is an anti-corruption measure designed to protect "individuals who report corruption."

182. **A system of setting goals, monitoring progress, and evaluating outcomes in public agencies is called**
    A) Performance management.
    B) Public relations.
    C) Crisis management.
    D) Financial accounting.
    E) Bureaucratic recruitment.

    **Answer:** A) Performance management.
    **Explanation:** Performance management is defined as "A systematic process of planning, monitoring, evaluating, and improving the performance of government organizations."

183. **The ethical principle of making decisions based on merit, without bias or favoritism, is**
    A) Integrity.
    B) Honesty.
    C) Diligence.
    D) Objectivity.
    E) Confidentiality.

    **Answer:** D) Objectivity.
    **Explanation:** Objectivity (or Impartiality) is defined as "Making decisions based on merit, evidence, and law, without bias, favoritism, or prejudice."

184. **Which leadership theory argues that the most effective style depends on the context and followers?**
    A) Trait Theory
    B) Transactional Theory
    C) Contingency Theory
    D) Behavioral Theory
    E) Servant Leadership

    **Answer:** C) Contingency Theory
    **Explanation:** Contingency (or Situational) Theories "Argue that effective leadership depends on the situation...Leaders need to adapt their style to fit the specific circumstances."

185. **An independent Anti-Corruption Agency (ACA) is designed to be**
    A) under the direct control of the President.
    B) a branch of the military.
    C) free from political influence in its investigations.
    D) a committee within the legislature.
    E) a department of the Ministry of Finance.

    **Answer:** C) free from political influence in its investigations.
    **Explanation:** The effectiveness of ACAs relies on their independence, allowing them to investigate and prosecute corruption without political interference.

---

### **Chapter 15: Structure and Functions of Local Government**

186. **The legal existence and powers of local government are typically defined in the**
    A) national budget.
    B) party manifesto.
    C) national constitution and local government acts.
    D) annual presidential address.
    E) civil service rules.

    **Answer:** C) national constitution and local government acts.
    **Explanation:** The text states that the framework for local government is "usually defined and established by...The National Constitution" and "National or State/Regional Legislation (Local Government Acts/Laws)."

187. **The principle that public functions should be handled by the lowest competent level of government is called**
    A) Centralization.
    B) Subsidiarity.
    C) Sovereignty.
    D) Federalism.
    E) Unitarism.

    **Answer:** B) Subsidiarity.
    **Explanation:** Subsidiarity is defined as "the principle that public functions should be exercised by the lowest level of government competent to perform them effectively."

188. **The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level is the**
    A) Traditional council of chiefs.
    B) Local council.
    C) Office of the Mayor.
    D) Local judiciary.
    E) Community development association.

    **Answer:** B) Local council.
    **Explanation:** The local council is described as "The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level."

189. **In many African countries, the role of traditional authorities in local governance often includes**
    A) commanding the local police.
    B) collecting national income tax.
    C) conducting foreign relations.
    D) resolving disputes using customary methods.
    E) passing the national budget.

    **Answer:** D) resolving disputes using customary methods.
    **Explanation:** "Dispute resolution (using customary methods)" is listed as a key potential role for traditional authorities in local communities.

190. **A major source of locally generated revenue for local governments is**
    A) Grants from the central government.
    B) Loans from the World Bank.
    C) Property rates and taxes.
    D) Foreign aid.
    E) Revenue from oil exports.

    **Answer:** C) Property rates and taxes.
    **Explanation:** Property rates/taxes are a classic example of "Locally Generated Revenue (Internal Sources)" for local governments. Grants are an external source.

191. **A grant from the central government that must be used for a specific purpose, like primary education, is a(n)**
    A) General purpose grant.
    B) Specific purpose (conditional) grant.
    C) Equalization grant.
    D) Block grant.
    E) Unconditional grant.

    **Answer:** B) Specific purpose (conditional) grant.
    **Explanation:** Specific purpose grants are defined as those that "Must be used for particular sectors or programs...often with conditions attached."

192. **The financial plan of a local government, outlining expected revenues and expenditures, is the**
    A) Manifesto.
    B) Constitution.
    C) Audit report.
    D) Budget.
    E) Development plan.

    **Answer:** D) Budget.
    **Explanation:** A budget is defined as "A financial plan for a fiscal year, outlining expected revenues and proposed expenditures."

193. **Spending on the construction of a new local road or school is an example of**
    A) Recurrent expenditure.
    B) Capital expenditure.
    C) Debt servicing.
    D) Administrative cost.
    E) Consumption expenditure.

    **Answer:** B) Capital expenditure.
    **Explanation:** Capital (or development) expenditure is defined as "Investment in long-term assets (e.g., construction of schools, roads, clinics)."

194. **Community participation in local governance is important because it**
    A) increases government secrecy.
    B) ensures policies are relevant and promotes local ownership.
    C) makes decision-making faster.
    D) reduces the need for local elections.
    E) centralizes power in the hands of the chairperson.

    **Answer:** B) ensures policies are relevant and promotes local ownership.
    **Explanation:** The importance of community participation includes ensuring "local policies and projects are relevant to community needs" and promoting "local ownership and sustainability."

195. **Rural development is a process aimed at improving the well-being of people in rural areas and primarily involves**
    A) only agricultural development.
    B) only building large factories.
    C) a multifaceted approach including economic, social, and infrastructural improvements.
    D) moving all rural people to cities.
    E) focusing exclusively on traditional customs.

    **Answer:** C) a multifaceted approach including economic, social, and infrastructural improvements.
    **Explanation:** Rural development is described as multifaceted, including agriculture but also economic diversification, infrastructure, and social development (health, education).

196. **The head of the executive arm of a local government is often called the**
    A) Councillor.
    B) Town Clerk.
    C) Chairperson or Mayor.
    D) Chief Judge.
    E) Traditional Ruler.

    **Answer:** C) Chairperson or Mayor.
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the head of the local executive as the "Chairperson/Mayor/Council Leader."

197. **The final approval of the local government budget is typically done by the**
    A) Chairperson/Mayor.
    B) Central government's finance minister.
    C) Local council.
    D) Traditional ruler.
    E) Local government staff.

    **Answer:** C) Local council.
    **Explanation:** The budget cycle described indicates that the draft budget is submitted to the "local council for review, debate, modification, and final approval."

198. **A key difference between a local government council and a Community-Based Organization (CBO) is that the council**
    A) is informal.
    B) has legally established governmental powers.
    C) is a voluntary group.
    D) focuses on a single project.
    E) is not involved in service delivery.

    **Answer:** B) has legally established governmental powers.
    **Explanation:** A local government council is a formal, statutory body with powers defined by law (e.g., to make by-laws, levy taxes), whereas a CBO is a voluntary civil society group.

199. **The permanent officials who carry out the day-to-day work of a local government are its**
    A) Councillors.
    B) Administration/Staff.
    C) Mayor and Deputy Mayor.
    D) Traditional Chiefs.
    E) Party agents.

    **Answer:** B) Administration/Staff.
    **Explanation:** The local government administration or staff are the "permanent officials and employees who carry out the administrative and operational functions."

200. **The process by which ordinary citizens organize themselves to take collective action on local issues is**
    A) Centralization.
    B) Grassroots mobilization.
    C) Nationalization.
    D) Executive decree.
    E) Judicial review.

    **Answer:** B) Grassroots mobilization.
    **Explanation:** Grassroots mobilization is defined as the "process by which ordinary citizens or local community groups organize themselves to take collective action to address common problems."

---

### **Comprehensive Review Questions (Covering Multiple Chapters)**

201. **A federal constitution is a necessary feature of a state with a**
    A) parliamentary system of government.
    B) federal system of government.
    C) unitary system of government.
    D) monarchical system of government.
    E) one-party system.

    **Answer:** B) federal system of government.
    **Explanation:** A federal system, by definition, requires a constitution that divides powers between central and regional governments. (Ch 2 & 3)

202. **The concept of checks and balances is a practical application of the principle of**
    A) popular sovereignty.
    B) the rule of law.
    C) separation of powers.
    D) judicial independence.
    E) collective responsibility.

    **Answer:** C) separation of powers.
    **Explanation:** Checks and balances is the mechanism used to enforce the separation of powers, preventing any single branch from becoming too dominant. (Ch 3)

203. **Which of the following pairs of concepts are necessarily in opposition?**
    A) Democracy and Federalism
    B) Authoritarianism and Rule of Law
    C) Presidentialism and Republicanism
    D) Unitarism and Parliamentary System
    E) Monarchy and Sovereignty

    **Answer:** B) Authoritarianism and Rule of Law
    **Explanation:** Authoritarianism concentrates power and places the state above the law, while the Rule of Law subjects everyone, including the state, to the law. They are fundamentally incompatible. (Ch 1 & 3)

204. **The "watchdog" function of the media is most essential for ensuring**
    A) government accountability.
    B) national unity.
    C) economic growth.
    D) party discipline.
    E) judicial independence.

    **Answer:** A) government accountability.
    **Explanation:** By scrutinizing the government and exposing wrongdoing, the media plays a crucial role in holding public officials accountable to the people. (Ch 8 & 14)

205. **An election conducted using the Proportional Representation (PR) system is likely to result in a**
    A) two-party system.
    B) one-party dominant system.
    C) multi-party system and coalition government.
    D) system with no political parties.
    E) presidential system.

    **Answer:** C) multi-party system and coalition government.
    **Explanation:** PR systems give smaller parties a better chance of winning seats, which often leads to multi-party systems where no single party has a majority, necessitating coalition governments. (Ch 2 & 7)
