206. **The International Court of Justice (ICJ) primarily settles legal disputes between**
    A) individuals.
    B) corporations.
    C) states.
    D) non-governmental organizations.
    E) provinces within a single country.

    **Answer:** C) states.
    **Explanation:** The ICJ's function is to "settle legal disputes submitted to it by states" and give advisory opinions to UN organs. (Ch 6 & 12)

207. **Which political ideology would most strongly advocate for a significant role for the state in redistributing wealth and providing extensive social welfare?**
    A) Classical Liberalism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Socialism
    D) Anarchism
    E) Libertarianism

    **Answer:** C) Socialism
    **Explanation:** Socialism advocates for a significant state role in "redistributing wealth, providing social welfare...and managing key industries to benefit society as a whole." (Ch 2)

208. **The body of professional, non-political government officials employed on a permanent basis is the**
    A) Cabinet.
    B) Legislature.
    C) Judiciary.
    D) Civil Service.
    E) Electorate.

    **Answer:** D) Civil Service.
    **Explanation:** The Civil Service is defined as the body of "professional, non-political government officials...employed on a permanent basis." (Ch 4 & 14)

209. **The Responsibility to Protect (R2P) challenges the traditional notion of**
    A) human rights.
    B) state sovereignty.
    C) balance of power.
    D) free trade.
    E) diplomacy.

    **Answer:** B) state sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** R2P posits that the international community can intervene in a state's affairs to prevent atrocities, which can conflict with the principle of non-intervention inherent in state sovereignty. (Ch 11 & 12)

210. **The development of a national health insurance policy is an example of which stage of the policy cycle?**
    A) Agenda Setting
    B) Policy Formulation and Adoption
    C) Policy Termination
    D) Grassroots Mobilization
    E) Electoral Campaigning

    **Answer:** B) Policy Formulation and Adoption
    **Explanation:** Designing and formally choosing a national health insurance scheme is a clear example of formulating policy options and adopting one as the official course of action. (Ch 9)

211. **The power of a president to appoint cabinet members who are then confirmed by the legislature is an example of**
    A) fusion of powers.
    B) checks and balances.
    C) direct democracy.
    D) unitary government.
    E) judicial review.

    **Answer:** B) checks and balances.
    **Explanation:** This is a classic example of checks and balances, where the executive's power to appoint is checked by the legislature's power to confirm. (Ch 3)

212. **Which of the following is NOT a source of revenue for local governments?**
    A) Property taxes
    B) Grants from central government
    C) Profits from national oil sales
    D) Fees for market stalls
    E) Business licenses

    **Answer:** C) Profits from national oil sales
    **Explanation:** Revenue from major national resources like oil is typically collected and managed by the central/federal government, not directly by local governments. (Ch 15)

213. **A key difference between a Prime Minister and a President is that the Prime Minister is**
    A) directly elected by the entire country in a separate election.
    B) the head of state in a monarchy.
    C) chosen from and accountable to the legislature.
    D) not the head of government.
    E) able to serve for life.

    **Answer:** C) chosen from and accountable to the legislature.
    **Explanation:** In a parliamentary system, the PM is the leader of the majority party in parliament and can be removed by a vote of no confidence, ensuring accountability to the legislature. (Ch 2 & 4)

214. **The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) is a foundational document for**
    A) international trade law.
    B) international human rights law.
    C) global environmental policy.
    D) the laws of war only.
    E) maritime law.

    **Answer:** B) international human rights law.
    **Explanation:** The UDHR is the landmark document that forms the basis for numerous legally binding human rights treaties and conventions. (Ch 10 & 12)

215. **A government that focuses on increasing GDP per capita but ignores income inequality and environmental damage is prioritizing**
    A) sustainable development.
    B) human development.
    C) economic growth.
    D) social justice.
    E) good governance.

    **Answer:** C) economic growth.
    **Explanation:** This scenario describes a narrow focus on economic growth, which is defined by increases in GDP, while neglecting the broader aspects of human and sustainable development. (Ch 13)

216. **In international relations, forming an alliance to counter a rising power is an example of**
    A) internal balancing.
    B) external balancing.
    C) soft power.
    D) diplomacy.
    E) collective security.

    **Answer:** B) external balancing.
    **Explanation:** The concept of balance of power involves two main mechanisms: internal balancing (building up one's own strength) and external balancing (forming alliances with others). (Ch 11)

217. **Which of the following is an example of a civil liberty?**
    A) The right to education
    B) Freedom from torture
    C) The right to healthcare
    D) The right to social security
    E) The right to work

    **Answer:** B) Freedom from torture
    **Explanation:** Freedom from torture is a classic civil liberty (a negative right) protecting individuals from government action. The other options are examples of economic and social rights (positive rights) that often require government action to provide. (Ch 3 & 10)

218. **The most significant challenge posed by the "digital divide" relates to the implementation of**
    A) fiscal policy.
    B) e-government.
    C) foreign policy.
    D) judicial review.
    E) national defense.

    **Answer:** B) e-government.
    **Explanation:** The digital divide, or unequal access to technology, directly hinders the goal of e-government to provide services to all citizens electronically. (Ch 14)

219. **A country with an unwritten, flexible constitution and a parliamentary system of government is**
    A) The United States.
    B) Nigeria.
    C) The United Kingdom.
    D) France.
    E) Germany.

    **Answer:** C) The United Kingdom.
    **Explanation:** The UK is the primary example used in the text for having an unwritten, flexible constitution and a parliamentary system. (Ch 2 & 3)

220. **The process of participatory budgeting is a mechanism to enhance**
    A) central government control.
    B) judicial independence.
    C) community participation in local governance.
    D) the power of the executive.
    E) the speed of policy implementation.

    **Answer:** C) community participation in local governance.
    **Explanation:** Participatory budgeting is a specific tool that allows citizens to have a direct say in how public money is spent at the local level, thus increasing participation. (Ch 15)

221. **Which of these institutions is NOT part of the executive branch?**
    A) The Cabinet
    B) The Civil Service
    C) The Supreme Court
    D) Ministries
    E) The Presidency

    **Answer:** C) The Supreme Court
    **Explanation:** The Supreme Court is the head of the judicial branch, which is separate from the executive branch. The other options are all components of the executive. (Ch 3 & 4)

222. **The "primacy of public interest" is a core ethical principle guiding the conduct of**
    A) private entrepreneurs.
    B) foreign diplomats.
    C) public administrators.
    D) international journalists.
    E) corporate lobbyists.

    **Answer:** C) public administrators.
    **Explanation:** A core ethical value for public servants is acting in the public interest, placing it above personal gain or other considerations. (Ch 14)

223. **The ECOWAS, EU, and ASEAN are all examples of**
    A) global military alliances.
    B) international financial institutions.
    C) regional international organizations.
    D) UN specialized agencies.
    E) human rights NGOs.

    **Answer:** C) regional international organizations.
    **Explanation:** These are all organizations whose members come from a specific geographic region, formed to promote cooperation and integration. (Ch 12)

224. **In a court of law, a tort is a wrongful act that results in injury to another person, for which the injured party may seek compensation. This falls under**
    A) criminal law.
    B) constitutional law.
    C) administrative law.
    D) civil law.
    E) international law.

    **Answer:** D) civil law.
    **Explanation:** Personal injury claims (torts) are a classic example of civil law, involving disputes between private parties where the remedy is usually monetary compensation (damages). (Ch 6)

225. **The leader who acts as a role model and inspires followers to achieve extraordinary outcomes is demonstrating \_\_\_\_\_ leadership.**
    A) autocratic
    B) transactional
    C) laissez-faire
    D) transformational
    E) bureaucratic

    **Answer:** D) transformational
    **Explanation:** Two key components of transformational leadership are "Idealized Influence" (acting as a role model) and "Inspirational Motivation" (inspiring followers). (Ch 14)

226. **Which of the following would a realist scholar of IR consider most important?**
    A) The moral values of a state's leader
    B) The distribution of military power in the international system
    C) The resolutions passed by the UN General Assembly
    D) The level of trade between two countries
    E) The shared cultural identity of a region

    **Answer:** B) The distribution of military power in the international system
    **Explanation:** Realism prioritizes power, especially military power, as the key variable in explaining state behavior and international outcomes. (Ch 11)

227. **A major advantage of a federal system over a unitary system is its ability to**
    A) make decisions more quickly.
    B) ensure uniformity of all laws.
    C) manage diversity and give autonomy to regions.
    D) reduce the cost of government.
    E) eliminate conflicts between levels of government.

    **Answer:** C) manage diversity and give autonomy to regions.
    **Explanation:** A key advantage of federalism is that it "Allows for regional diversity and autonomy" and is "suitable for large and diverse countries." (Ch 2)

228. **An ombudsman and an auditor-general are both examples of**
    A) executive agencies.
    B) legislative committees.
    C) judicial courts.
    D) external oversight and accountability bodies.
    E) informal civil society groups.

    **Answer:** D) external oversight and accountability bodies.
    **Explanation:** Both are listed as independent, external bodies designed to hold the government accountable for administrative fairness (Ombudsman) and financial propriety (Auditor-General). (Ch 4)

229. **The Bretton Woods Conference of 1944 led to the creation of the**
    A) United Nations and League of Nations.
    B) World Bank and International Monetary Fund.
    C) African Union and ECOWAS.
    D) European Union and NATO.
    E) World Trade Organization and GATT.

    **Answer:** B) World Bank and International Monetary Fund.
    **Explanation:** The text states that both the IMF and the World Bank Group were established in 1944 at the Bretton Woods Conference. (Ch 13)

230. **Which of the following is NOT a fundamental right typically found in a constitution?**
    A) Freedom of speech
    B) Right to life
    C) Right to a fair hearing
    D) Right to hold public office without qualifications
    E) Freedom of religion

    **Answer:** D) Right to hold public office without qualifications
    **Explanation:** While citizens have the right to participate in government, the right to hold public office is always subject to meeting certain qualifications (e.g., age, citizenship, education). (Ch 3)

231. **The main purpose of a political party's manifesto is to**
    A) list its registered members.
    B) present its policies and programs to the electorate.
    C) provide a legal framework for its existence.
    D) challenge election results in court.
    E) select candidates through primaries.

    **Answer:** B) present its policies and programs to the electorate.
    **Explanation:** A party platform or manifesto is developed to "address societal problems and guide government action if elected," and is presented to voters during campaigns. (Ch 2 & 8)

232. **A bicameral legislature is one that has**
    A) one chamber or house.
    B) two chambers or houses.
    C) three chambers or houses.
    D) no power to make laws.
    E) members who are all appointed.

    **Answer:** B) two chambers or houses.
    **Explanation:** The term "bicameral" literally means "two-chambered" (e.g., a Senate and a House of Representatives). This term is standard political science knowledge, implied by the discussion of legislatures. (Ch 3)

233. **A key function of a constitution is to**
    A) list all citizens by name.
    B) define and limit the powers of government.
    C) serve as the annual budget.
    D) act as a criminal code only.
    E) provide a history of the nation's culture.

    **Answer:** B) define and limit the powers of government.
    **Explanation:** A constitution "Defines the structure and powers of the main organs of government" and regulates their relationships, thereby limiting their powers. (Ch 3)

234. **If a country's legislature can amend the constitution through the same process as passing an ordinary law, the constitution is said to be**
    A) rigid.
    B) written.
    C) flexible.
    D) federal.
    E) uncodified.

    **Answer:** C) flexible.
    **Explanation:** A flexible constitution is defined as one that "Can be amended relatively easily, often by the same process as ordinary laws." (Ch 3)

235. **The existence of interest groups is a key feature of a \_\_\_\_\_ democracy.**
    A) totalitarian
    B) authoritarian
    C) pluralist
    D) direct
    E) socialist

    **Answer:** C) pluralist
    **Explanation:** Pluralism is a theory of democracy where power is seen as dispersed among various competing groups. Interest groups are the primary example of these groups, and their existence is vital for a pluralist system. (Ch 8)

236. **Which of these best illustrates the concept of political socialization?**
    A) A court interpreting a law
    B) A family teaching a child about national identity and the importance of voting
    C) A government agency implementing a new tax policy
    D) A central bank setting interest rates
    E) A country signing an international treaty

    **Answer:** B) A family teaching a child about national identity and the importance of voting
    **Explanation:** Political socialization is the process through which individuals acquire political values and beliefs. This happens through agents like family, schools, and media. The example in option B is a classic illustration. (Ch 1)

237. **The primary purpose of a code of conduct for public officials is to**
    A) increase their salaries.
    B) guide ethical behavior and prevent corruption.
    C) give them immunity from prosecution.
    D) define the structure of government.
    E) outline foreign policy goals.

    **Answer:** B) guide ethical behavior and prevent corruption.
    **Explanation:** Codes of conduct are an anti-corruption and accountability mechanism that establishes ethical standards for public officials. (Ch 4 & 14)

238. **The 'Harm Principle', often associated with John Stuart Mill, states that the only legitimate reason to restrict an individual's liberty is to**
    A) promote economic equality.
    B) uphold traditional values.
    C) prevent harm to others.
    D) increase the power of the state.
    E) ensure conformity.

    **Answer:** C) prevent harm to others.
    **Explanation:** This is a core concept of liberalism, stating that liberty is paramount unless its exercise causes harm to other people. It underpins the idea of justifiable limitations on rights. (Ch 2 & 10)

239. **In a confederal system, ultimate sovereignty rests with the**
    A) central government.
    B) constituent states.
    C) judiciary.
    D) people directly.
    E) international community.

    **Answer:** B) constituent states.
    **Explanation:** A confederal system is a "league of independent states" where the "States retain ultimate sovereignty." The central government is weak and derives its power from the states. (Ch 2)

240. **A government's decision to privatize state-owned enterprises is a form of**
    A) Social Policy.
    B) Foreign Policy.
    C) Economic Policy.
    D) Defense Policy.
    E) Cultural Policy.

    **Answer:** C) Economic Policy.
    **Explanation:** Privatization is a major economic reform policy, falling under the government's function of economic management and development. (Ch 1 & 9)

241. **The 'Iron Triangle' is a concept used to describe the powerful policy-making relationship between**
    A) the president, the prime minister, and the monarch.
    B) the legislature, the executive, and the judiciary.
    C) legislative committees, bureaucratic agencies, and interest groups.
    D) the media, the public, and political parties.
    E) the IMF, the World Bank, and the WTO.

    **Answer:** C) legislative committees, bureaucratic agencies, and interest groups.
    **Explanation:** Though not explicitly in the text, this is a standard concept in public policy that explains how these three actors often collaborate to create and implement policies in a specific area, to their mutual benefit. It's a classic WAEC-level concept. (Ch 8 & 9)

242. **The idea that democracies are highly unlikely to go to war with one another is known as**
    A) Balance of Power Theory.
    B) Democratic Peace Theory.
    C) Dependency Theory.
    D) Game Theory.
    E) World Systems Theory.

    **Answer:** B) Democratic Peace Theory.
    **Explanation:** This is a core tenet of the liberal school of thought in International Relations, as mentioned in the text's focus for Liberalism. (Ch 11)

243. **A unicameral legislature is one in which**
    A) there are two legislative chambers.
    B) there is only one legislative chamber.
    C) the members are not elected.
    D) the executive is also a member.
    E) decisions are always unanimous.

    **Answer:** B) there is only one legislative chamber.
    **Explanation:** "Uni" means one, so a unicameral legislature has a single house or chamber. (Implied in Ch 3)

244. **Which of the following would be the most direct form of citizen participation in national law-making?**
    A) Voting in a presidential election
    B) Participating in a referendum
    C) Joining a political party
    D) Lobbying a representative
    E) Writing a letter to a newspaper

    **Answer:** B) Participating in a referendum
    **Explanation:** A referendum allows citizens to vote directly on a specific proposed law, making it a mechanism of direct democracy and the most direct form of participation in law-making from the options. (Ch 2)

245. **The civil service is expected to provide the government with**
    A) political leadership.
    B) financial campaign contributions.
    C) continuity and expert advice.
    D) electoral victory.
    E) judicial rulings.

    **Answer:** C) continuity and expert advice.
    **Explanation:** The permanent and professional nature of the civil service ensures that there is continuity in administration despite changes in political government, and it provides the specialized knowledge needed for policy-making. (Ch 4 & 14)

246. **Which of the following is an example of a public good?**
    A) A private car
    B) A personal computer
    C) National defense
    D) A ticket to a football match
    E) A loaf of bread

    **Answer:** C) National defense
    **Explanation:** A public good is non-excludable (you can't stop someone from benefiting) and non-rivalrous (one person's use doesn't diminish another's). National defense is a classic example provided by the government for the benefit of all society. (Ch 1)

247. **The process of gerrymandering involves**
    A) manipulating electoral district boundaries to favor a political party.
    B) funding election campaigns through illegal means.
    C) suppressing the votes of a particular group.
    D) counting votes dishonestly.
    E) holding elections more frequently than required.

    **Answer:** A) manipulating electoral district boundaries to favor a political party.
    **Explanation:** Gerrymandering is a specific anti-democratic practice related to the delimitation of constituencies, a function of the EMB. It's a standard concept relevant to the electoral process. (Ch 7)

248. **In a parliamentary system, a minority government is one that**
    A) represents an ethnic minority.
    B) is formed by a party that has fewer than 50% of the seats but has enough support to govern.
    C) has been in power for a very short time.
    D) is formed by a coalition of all parties.
    E) has no opposition.

    **Answer:** B) is formed by a party that has fewer than 50% of the seats but has enough support to govern.
    **Explanation:** This is a common outcome in parliamentary systems, especially with multiple parties. The government does not have an absolute majority on its own and may rely on the support of smaller parties on a case-by-case basis. (Ch 2)

249. **The official responsible for the day-to-day administration of a government ministry and who is a career civil servant is the**
    A) Minister.
    B) President.
    C) Permanent Secretary.
    D) Councillor.
    E) Judge.

    **Answer:** C) Permanent Secretary.
    **Explanation:** The text describes a ministry as being headed by a Minister (a political appointee) and a senior civil servant, such as a Permanent Secretary or Director-General, who is a career official. (Ch 4)

250. **The main objective of a pressure group is to**
    A) contest and win elections.
    B) form a government.
    C) influence government policy in favor of its members.
    D) appoint judges to the Supreme Court.
    E) provide an alternative government.

    **Answer:** C) influence government policy in favor of its members.
    **Explanation:** Pressure groups (or interest groups) are defined by their goal of influencing public policy on issues relevant to their members, without seeking to gain control of government themselves. (Ch 8)Of course. Here are 250 WAEC-standard multiple-choice questions with answers and detailed explanations, derived from the foundational textbook text you provided. The questions cover all relevant chapters (1-15) and are designed to test recall, understanding, and application of the concepts.

### **Chapter 1: Meaning and Scope of Government and Governance**

1.  **Which term refers to the formal institutions and processes through which a state is ruled?**
    A) Governance
    B) Politics
    C) Government
    D) Society
    E) Administration

    **Answer:** C) Government
    **Explanation:** The text defines government as "the formal institutions, structures, and processes through which a state or a community is ruled." Governance is the broader process, politics is the struggle for power, society is the community, and administration is a function of government.

2.  **The concept of "who gets what, when, and how" is famously associated with the definition of**
    A) Governance
    B) Politics
    C) Government
    D) The State
    E) Authority

    **Answer:** B) Politics
    **Explanation:** The text directly quotes Harold Lasswell's famous description of politics as "'who gets what, when, and how'", highlighting its focus on the allocation of scarce resources.

3.  **Governance is a broader concept than government because it includes**
    A) only the legislature and judiciary.
    B) only the head of state.
    C) both formal institutions and informal actors like civil society.
    D) only the activities of political parties.
    E) only the enforcement of laws by the police.

    **Answer:** C) both formal institutions and informal actors like civil society.
    **Explanation:** Governance encompasses not just the formal institutions of government but also "the various actors (e.g., civil society organizations, private sector, international bodies, citizens) and informal mechanisms" that influence decision-making.

4.  **The monopoly on the legitimate use of force is a key characteristic of**
    A) Politics
    B) Government
    C) Governance
    D) Civil Society
    E) The Electorate

    **Answer:** B) Government
    **Explanation:** The text lists "The legitimate authority to use force or coercion (e.g., through police, military)" as a key characteristic of government.

5.  **Which of the following is NOT a primary function of government?**
    A) Maintenance of law and order
    B) Provision of public services
    C) Economic management
    D) Formation of private corporations
    E) National defense

    **Answer:** D) Formation of private corporations
    **Explanation:** While government regulates the economy and may own state enterprises, its primary functions are security, service provision, and economic management. The formation of private corporations is an activity of the private sector.

6.  **Building roads, funding schools, and providing healthcare are examples of which function of government?**
    A) Maintenance of law and order
    B) Economic management
    C) Political socialization
    D) Provision of public services
    E) Conflict resolution

    **Answer:** D) Provision of public services
    **Explanation:** The text lists infrastructure, education, and healthcare as prime examples of public and social services provided by the government.

7.  **The management of government revenue and expenditure to influence the economy is known as**
    A) Monetary policy
    B) Trade policy
    C) Fiscal policy
    D) Judicial review
    E) Political policy

    **Answer:** C) Fiscal policy
    **Explanation:** The text defines fiscal policy as "Managing government revenue (taxation) and expenditure (public spending) to influence the economy." Monetary policy deals with money supply and interest rates.

8.  **A system of government where supreme power is vested in the people and exercised through representation is called**
    A) Monarchy
    B) Oligarchy
    C) Authoritarianism
    D) Democracy
    E) Theocracy

    **Answer:** D) Democracy
    **Explanation:** Democracy is defined as a system where "supreme power is vested in the people and exercised by them directly or indirectly through a system of representation."

9.  **Which form of government is characterized by the rule of a single individual who often seizes power by force?**
    A) Constitutional Monarchy
    B) Dictatorship
    C) Oligarchy
    D) Representative Democracy
    E) Anarchy

    **Answer:** B) Dictatorship
    **Explanation:** A dictatorship, a subtype of authoritarianism, is defined as "Rule by a single individual (dictator) who has absolute power, often obtained and maintained by force."

10. **A government ruled by a small, wealthy elite is best described as a(n)**
    A) Plutocracy
    B) Democracy
    C) Theocracy
    D) Monarchy
    E) Anarchy

    **Answer:** A) Plutocracy
    **Explanation:** The text notes that an oligarchy is "rule by the few," and a specific form of it, plutocracy, is "rule by the wealthy."

11. **The principle of good governance that ensures government officials are answerable for their actions is**
    A) Participation
    B) Transparency
    C) Accountability
    D) Rule of law
    E) Efficiency

    **Answer:** C) Accountability
    **Explanation:** Accountability is defined as the principle where "Governmental institutions...are accountable to the public" and "officials are answerable for their actions and decisions."

12. **The "Rule of Law" implies that**
    A) the ruler is above the law.
    B) laws are made only by the ruler.
    C) everyone, including the government, is subject to the law.
    D) only citizens must obey the law.
    E) law is a tool for the powerful.

    **Answer:** C) everyone, including the government, is subject to the law.
    **Explanation:** The principle of Rule of Law states that "All individuals, institutions, and entities, including the government itself, are accountable to laws that are publicly promulgated, equally enforced, and independently adjudicated."

13. **A government that attempts to control all aspects of public and private life is best described as**
    A) Democratic
    B) Totalitarian
    C) Monarchical
    D) Oligarchic
    E) Anarchic

    **Answer:** B) Totalitarian
    **Explanation:** Totalitarianism is defined as an "extreme form of authoritarianism where the state attempts to control all aspects of public and private life."

14. **Which of the following is NOT a core principle of good governance?**
    A) Rule by the strongest
    B) Transparency
    C) Participation
    D) Accountability
    E) Equity and Inclusiveness

    **Answer:** A) Rule by the strongest
    **Explanation:** Rule by the strongest is contrary to the principles of good governance, especially the rule of law and equity. The other options are listed as core principles.

15. **A constitutional monarchy is a system where**
    A) the monarch has absolute power.
    B) the monarch's power is limited by a constitution.
    C) there is no monarch.
    D) religious leaders rule.
    E) the military is in control.

    **Answer:** B) the monarch's power is limited by a constitution.
    **Explanation:** The text defines a constitutional monarchy as a system where "the monarch's powers are limited by a constitution and laws," and they often serve as a ceremonial head of state.

---

### **Chapter 2: Political Ideologies and Systems**

16. **Which political ideology emphasizes individual liberty, rights, and the consent of the governed?**
    A) Socialism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Liberalism
    D) Communism
    E) Nationalism

    **Answer:** C) Liberalism
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the core values of liberalism as emphasizing "individual liberty, rights, equality of opportunity, reason, and consent of the governed."

17. **An ideology that values tradition, established institutions, and gradual change is known as**
    A) Liberalism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Anarchism
    D) Socialism
    E) Fascism

    **Answer:** B) Conservatism
    **Explanation:** Conservatism is defined by its emphasis on "tradition, established institutions, social order, authority, hierarchy, and gradual change."

18. **The primary aim of socialism is to promote**
    A) individual profit.
    B) social equality and collective ownership.
    C) a stateless society immediately.
    D) aggressive national expansion.
    E) limited government intervention.

    **Answer:** B) social equality and collective ownership.
    **Explanation:** Socialism's core values include "social equality, community, cooperation, and collective ownership or control of the means of production."

19. **Communism, in its theoretical form, aims for a society that is**
    A) classless and stateless.
    B) ruled by a monarch.
    C) based on a free market.
    D) led by a military junta.
    E) highly individualistic.

    **Answer:** A) classless and stateless.
    **Explanation:** Communism is described as aiming for "a classless, stateless society where the means of production are communally owned."

20. **Nationalism is an ideology that primarily promotes the interests of a**
    A) particular social class.
    B) specific religious group.
    C) particular nation.
    D) single political party.
    E) global community.

    **Answer:** C) a particular nation.
    **Explanation:** Nationalism is defined as an ideology that "promotes the interests of a particular nation (a group of people with a shared identity...)."

21. **In a direct democracy, laws are made by**
    A) elected representatives.
    B) the head of state.
    C) citizens themselves.
    D) a council of elders.
    E) the judiciary.

    **Answer:** C) citizens themselves.
    **Explanation:** Direct democracy is a system where "Citizens participate directly in making laws and policy decisions, without intermediaries or elected representatives."

22. **Most modern large-scale democracies are representative because**
    A) citizens are not intelligent enough to rule.
    B) direct democracy is impractical for large populations.
    C) monarchy is a better system.
    D) political parties do not exist.
    E) it is cheaper to operate.

    **Answer:** B) direct democracy is impractical for large populations.
    **Explanation:** The text notes that direct democracy is "Impractical for large, complex modern states," making representative democracy the more common and practical form.

23. **A key feature of a unitary system of government is that power is**
    A) divided between central and regional governments.
    B) concentrated in the central government.
    C) held by the judiciary.
    D) shared equally among all citizens.
    E) exercised by a monarch.

    **Answer:** B) concentrated in the central government.
    **Explanation:** In a unitary system, "Sovereign power is concentrated in a single, central (national) government," and any regional units are subordinate to it.

24. **In a federal system of government, like Nigeria or the USA, sovereignty is**
    A) held exclusively by the central government.
    B) held exclusively by the state governments.
    C) divided between the central and state governments.
    D) vested in the legislature alone.
    E) non-existent.

    **Answer:** C) divided between the central and state governments.
    **Explanation:** A federal system is one where "Sovereign power is divided and shared between a central (national or federal) government and constituent regional governments."

25. **The defining characteristic of a presidential system of government is**
    A) the fusion of executive and legislative powers.
    B) the clear separation of powers between branches.
    C) the Prime Minister being the head of government.
    D) the Head of State being a monarch.
    E) the absence of a judiciary.

    **Answer:** B) the clear separation of powers between branches.
    **Explanation:** A presidential system is characterized by a "Clear separation of powers between the executive, legislative, and judicial branches."

26. **In a parliamentary system, the head of government is the Prime Minister who is accountable to the**
    A) monarch.
    B) people directly through a separate election.
    C) judiciary.
    D) legislature.
    E) civil service.

    **Answer:** D) legislature.
    **Explanation:** In a parliamentary system, the "Prime Minister (PM) and their cabinet...are drawn from, and are accountable to, the legislature (parliament)."

27. **A political system with three or more parties having a realistic chance of gaining power is a**
    A) one-party system.
    B) two-party system.
    C) multi-party system.
    D) zero-party system.
    E) dominant-party system.

    **Answer:** C) multi-party system.
    **Explanation:** A multi-party system is defined as one where "Three or more political parties have a realistic chance of gaining power."

28. **One of the primary functions of an opposition party in a democracy is to**
    A) overthrow the government by force.
    B) always agree with government policies.
    C) scrutinize government actions and offer alternative policies.
    D) refuse to participate in elections.
    E) control the judiciary.

    **Answer:** C) scrutinize government actions and offer alternative policies.
    **Explanation:** The text states that parties not in power (opposition) "critically examine government actions, hold it accountable, and offer alternative policies."

29. **A system where the executive can be removed from office by a legislative "vote of no confidence" is**
    A) presidential.
    B) authoritarian.
    C) monarchical.
    D) parliamentary.
    E) federal.

    **Answer:** D) parliamentary.
    **Explanation:** A key feature of the parliamentary system is that the government "can be removed by a vote of no confidence" from the legislature.

30. **Which of the following countries is a classic example of a federal system?**
    A) France
    B) United Kingdom
    C) Japan
    D) Nigeria
    E) Ghana

    **Answer:** D) Nigeria
    **Explanation:** The text lists Nigeria, along with the United States, Canada, Germany, etc., as examples of countries with a federal system. The others listed as options are unitary.

---

### **Chapter 3: Constitutions and the Rule of Law**

31. **A constitution is best described as the**
    A) set of rules for a political party.
    B) collection of all ordinary laws.
    C) supreme law of the land establishing the framework of government.
    D) history of a country's rulers.
    E) annual budget of a country.

    **Answer:** C) supreme law of the land establishing the framework of government.
    **Explanation:** A constitution is defined as "a set of fundamental principles...according to which a state...is governed" and is "often considered the supreme law of the land."

32. **A key advantage of a written constitution is its**
    A) flexibility and ease of change.
    B) clarity and accessibility.
    C) reliance on unwritten conventions.
    D) subordination to ordinary laws.
    E) temporary nature.

    **Answer:** B) clarity and accessibility.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Clarity, accessibility, stability" as advantages of a written constitution, providing a clear standard for governance.

33. **The constitution of the United Kingdom is a prime example of a(n) \_\_\_\_\_ constitution.**
    A) rigid
    B) written
    C) federal
    D) unwritten
    E) republican

    **Answer:** D) unwritten
    **Explanation:** The United Kingdom is cited as the "primary example" of a country with an unwritten (uncodified) constitution, where principles are found in various sources.

34. **A rigid constitution is one that**
    A) can be amended by a simple majority vote.
    B) is very short and easy to read.
    C) is difficult to amend, requiring a special procedure.
    D) applies only to the government.
    E) has never been changed.

    **Answer:** C) is difficult to amend, requiring a special procedure.
    **Explanation:** A rigid constitution is defined as being "Difficult to amend (change). Requires a special, complex procedure for amendment."

35. **The principle of separation of powers is most famously associated with**
    A) Karl Marx
    B) Harold Lasswell
    C) John Locke
    D) Baron de Montesquieu
    E) Adam Smith

    **Answer:** D) Baron de Montesquieu
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the Separation of Powers was "most famously, articulated by...Baron de Montesquieu."

36. **The primary function of the legislature is to**
    A) interpret laws.
    B) execute laws.
    C) make laws.
    D) elect the president.
    E) command the armed forces.

    **Answer:** C) make laws.
    **Explanation:** The legislature is described as the "Law-making Branch" with the "Primary Function: To make laws (statutes) for the society."

37. **The executive branch of government is responsible for**
    A) implementing and enforcing laws.
    B) adjudicating legal disputes.
    C) making constitutional amendments.
    D) selecting its own members from the judiciary.
    E) declaring laws unconstitutional.

    **Answer:** A) implementing and enforcing laws.
    **Explanation:** The primary function of the executive branch is "To implement, execute, and enforce the laws made by the legislature."

38. **The system where each branch of government can limit the powers of the others is known as**
    A) fusion of powers.
    B) rule of law.
    C) checks and balances.
    D) popular sovereignty.
    E) unitary government.

    **Answer:** C) checks and balances.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that under checks and balances, "the branches are also designed to check and balance each other's powers to prevent any one branch from becoming dominant."

39. **The presidential power to reject a bill passed by the legislature is called a**
    A) veto.
    B) pardon.
    C) decree.
    D) filibuster.
    E) statute.

    **Answer:** A) veto.
    **Explanation:** As an example of checks and balances, the text mentions that the "President can veto" laws passed by the legislature.

40. **The section of a constitution that guarantees basic rights and freedoms is often called a**
    A) Preamble.
    B) Bill of Rights.
    C) Schedule.
    D) Manifesto.
    E) Code of Conduct.

    **Answer:** B) Bill of Rights.
    **Explanation:** Most modern constitutions include a "Bill of Rights or Chapter on Fundamental Rights" to guarantee basic freedoms.

41. **The right to a fair hearing, including the presumption of innocence, is a \_\_\_\_\_ right.**
    A) political
    B) social
    C) economic
    D) civil
    E) cultural

    **Answer:** D) civil
    **Explanation:** The "Right to a Fair Hearing (Due Process)" is listed as a common fundamental right, falling under the category of civil rights and liberties.

42. **The power of a court to determine if a law is consistent with the constitution is called**
    A) judicial activism.
    B) judicial review.
    C) legislative oversight.
    D) executive order.
    E) constitutional amendment.

    **Answer:** B) judicial review.
    **Explanation:** Judicial review is defined as "The power of a court...to examine laws...and to determine whether they are constitutional."

43. **A key difference between civil liberties and civil rights is that civil liberties typically**
    A) require government action to provide them.
    B) protect individuals from government interference.
    C) apply only to citizens.
    D) relate to economic issues only.
    E) are granted by the executive.

    **Answer:** B) protect individuals from government interference.
    **Explanation:** Civil liberties are described as "Freedoms that protect individuals from unwarranted government interference (often 'negative rights' - freedom from)."

44. **Which of the following is NOT a source of an unwritten constitution?**
    A) Statutes
    B) Conventions
    C) A single codified document
    D) Judicial decisions
    E) Historical charters

    **Answer:** C) A single codified document
    **Explanation:** The defining feature of an unwritten constitution is that its principles are NOT contained in a single document but are found in various other sources like statutes, conventions, and case law.

45. **Which body is responsible for interpreting the constitution and resolving constitutional disputes?**
    A) The Executive
    B) The Legislature
    C) The Civil Service
    D) The Judiciary
    E) The Electoral Commission

    **Answer:** D) The Judiciary
    **Explanation:** Constitutional interpretation is "Primarily the role of the judiciary (courts), particularly the highest court (e.g., Supreme Court)."

---

### **Chapter 4: Executive Branch and Administrative Structures**

46. **In a presidential system, the President is both Head of State and**
    A) Head of the Legislature.
    B) Head of Government.
    C) Chief Justice.
    D) Speaker of the House.
    E) Head of the Civil Service Commission.

    **Answer:** B) Head of Government.
    **Explanation:** The text states that the President in a presidential system is "Usually both Head of State...and Head of Government."

47. **In a parliamentary system, the Cabinet is typically drawn from the**
    A) judiciary.
    B) civil service.
    C) business community.
    D) legislature.
    E) general public through a separate election.

    **Answer:** D) legislature.
    **Explanation:** In parliamentary systems, "cabinet ministers are typically Members of Parliament (MPs) from the governing party/coalition."

48. **The collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive and senior ministers is the**
    A) Legislature.
    B) Judiciary.
    C) Cabinet.
    D) Civil Service.
    E) National Security Council.

    **Answer:** C) Cabinet.
    **Explanation:** The Cabinet is defined as "A collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive...and senior government ministers."

49. **The principle that all cabinet members must publicly support government decisions is known as**
    A) individual responsibility.
    B) collective responsibility.
    C) separation of powers.
    D) judicial independence.
    E) bureaucratic neutrality.

    **Answer:** B) collective responsibility.
    **Explanation:** Collective Responsibility is the principle where "Cabinet members are expected to publicly support government decisions, even if they privately disagreed."

50. **The operational arm of the executive branch responsible for implementing laws and policies is the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Legislature.
    C) Bureaucracy.
    D) Cabinet.
    E) Political Party.

    **Answer:** C) Bureaucracy.
    **Explanation:** The bureaucracy (or public administration) is described as "The operational arm of the executive branch" responsible for day-to-day implementation.

51. **According to Max Weber's model, an ideal bureaucracy should be characterized by**
    A) political patronage.
    B) merit-based appointment.
    C) personal favoritism.
    D) inconsistent rules.
    E) verbal commands only.

    **Answer:** B) merit-based appointment.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Merit-based Appointment and Promotion" as a key characteristic of Weber's ideal model of bureaucracy.

52. **The largest administrative units in a government, each headed by a minister, are called**
    A) Agencies.
    B) Bureaus.
    C) Councils.
    D) Ministries.
    E) Committees.

    **Answer:** D) Ministries.
    **Explanation:** Ministries (or Departments in some systems) are described as "The largest administrative units, each headed by a Minister."

53. **The main purpose of local government is to**
    A) conduct foreign policy.
    B) command the national army.
    C) bring government closer to the people and address local needs.
    D) print national currency.
    E) amend the national constitution.

    **Answer:** C) bring government closer to the people and address local needs.
    **Explanation:** The text states the purpose of local government is "To bring government closer to the people, allow for local participation...and address local needs."

54. **The transfer of powers and resources to democratically elected local governments is called**
    A) Deconcentration.
    B) Delegation.
    C) Devolution.
    D) Centralization.
    E) Privatization.

    **Answer:** C) Devolution.
    **Explanation:** Devolution is defined as the "strongest form" of decentralization, involving the transfer of "powers, functions, and resources to democratically elected local governments."

55. **Which of the following is a typical function of local government?**
    A) National defense
    B) Waste management and collection
    C) Foreign diplomacy
    D) Central banking
    E) Supreme Court appointments

    **Answer:** B) Waste management and collection
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Waste management and collection" as a typical function of local government, along with primary education, local roads, etc.

56. **The principle that civil servants should serve the government of the day loyally, regardless of its political party, is known as**
    A) political activism.
    B) political neutrality.
    C) political patronage.
    D) political socialization.
    E) political ambition.

    **Answer:** B) political neutrality.
    **Explanation:** A key characteristic of an ideal civil service is "Political Neutrality and Impartiality," meaning they serve the government impartially.

57. **An independent official who investigates citizen complaints about government maladministration is the**
    A) President.
    B) Prime Minister.
    C) Auditor-General.
    D) Ombudsman.
    E) Chief Justice.

    **Answer:** D) Ombudsman.
    **Explanation:** The Ombudsman is defined as "An independent official who investigates complaints from citizens about maladministration or injustice by government agencies."

58. **The Auditor-General is primarily responsible for**
    A) prosecuting criminals.
    B) auditing government accounts and expenditures.
    C) commanding the police force.
    D) appointing ministers.
    E) making laws.

    **Answer:** B) auditing government accounts and expenditures.
    **Explanation:** The Auditor-General is an independent body that "audits government accounts and expenditures to ensure financial propriety and value for money."

59. **Which of the following is NOT a mechanism for ensuring executive accountability?**
    A) Legislative oversight
    B) Judicial review
    C) Regular elections
    D) Freedom of the press
    E) Absolute immunity from prosecution

    **Answer:** E) Absolute immunity from prosecution
    **Explanation:** Absolute immunity would prevent accountability. The other options (oversight, review, elections, free press) are all key mechanisms for holding the executive accountable.

60. **"Red tape" in bureaucracy refers to**
    A) efficient service delivery.
    B) high levels of transparency.
    C) a fast-track system for important issues.
    D) slow, inefficient processes caused by excessive rules.
    E) merit-based promotion.

    **Answer:** D) slow, inefficient processes caused by excessive rules.
    **Explanation:** "Red tape" is a common criticism of bureaucracy, referring to its tendency to be "slow, inefficient...rigid" due to complex rules and procedures.

---

### **Chapter 6: Judiciary and Legal System**

61. **The highest court in most judicial systems is called the**
    A) High Court.
    B) Magistrate Court.
    C) Court of Appeal.
    D) Supreme Court.
    E) Customary Court.

    **Answer:** D) Supreme Court.
    **Explanation:** The Supreme Court is described as the "Apex Court" or "Court of Last Resort," making it the highest court in the judicial hierarchy.

62. **The primary function of a Court of Appeal is to**
    A) conduct new trials with new evidence.
    B) hear cases for the first time.
    C) review decisions of lower courts for errors of law.
    D) handle minor criminal offenses.
    E) adjudicate cases based on local customs.

    **Answer:** C) review decisions of lower courts for errors of law.
    **Explanation:** Courts of Appeal "review the record of the lower court proceedings to determine if errors of law or procedure occurred."

63. **Judicial independence is crucial because it**
    A) allows judges to be influenced by politicians.
    B) ensures judges can apply the law without fear or favor.
    C) makes the judiciary subordinate to the executive.
    D) allows judges to ignore the constitution.
    E) speeds up all court cases.

    **Answer:** B) ensures judges can apply the law without fear or favor.
    **Explanation:** The importance of judicial independence is that it "Ensures judges can apply the law fairly and impartially, without fear or favor."

64. **Security of tenure for judges means they**
    A) are re-elected every year.
    B) can be easily removed for unpopular decisions.
    C) are appointed for a fixed period and cannot be easily dismissed.
    D) are members of the executive branch.
    E) must be approved by the police.

    **Answer:** C) are appointed for a fixed period and cannot be easily dismissed.
    **Explanation:** Security of tenure means judges "should not be easily removed from office," often being appointed until a mandatory retirement age, to protect them from political pressure.

65. **Criminal law deals with offenses against**
    A) private individuals only.
    B) contracts and property.
    C) the state or society as a whole.
    D) international organizations.
    E) family matters.

    **Answer:** C) the state or society as a whole.
    **Explanation:** Criminal law is defined as dealing with "offenses committed against the state or society as a whole (crimes)."

66. **In a criminal case, the standard of proof required for a conviction is**
    A) beyond a reasonable doubt.
    B) on the balance of probabilities.
    C) a gut feeling.
    D) preponderance of the evidence.
    E) absolute certainty.

    **Answer:** A) beyond a reasonable doubt.
    **Explanation:** The text states that in criminal law, guilt must be proven "beyond a reasonable doubt," which is a high standard.

67. **A legal dispute between two individuals over a contract is an example of**
    A) criminal law.
    B) constitutional law.
    C) administrative law.
    D) civil law.
    E) international law.

    **Answer:** D) civil law.
    **Explanation:** Civil law deals with "disputes between private individuals, organizations, or entities," and contract disputes are a classic example.

68. **Sharia law is a legal system derived from**
    A) Roman law.
    B) English common law.
    C) Islamic religious sources.
    D) French civil code.
    E) Traditional African customs.

    **Answer:** C) Islamic religious sources.
    **Explanation:** Sharia law is defined as "A legal system derived from Islamic religious sources, primarily the Quran and the Sunnah."

69. **A process where a neutral third party helps disputing parties reach their own agreement is called**
    A) Litigation.
    B) Arbitration.
    C) Mediation.
    D) Adjudication.
    E) Prosecution.

    **Answer:** C) Mediation.
    **Explanation:** In mediation, "a neutral mediator helps disputing parties reach a mutually acceptable agreement...but does not make a binding decision."

70. **The main difference between mediation and arbitration is that an arbitrator's decision is**
    A) non-binding.
    B) always a compromise.
    C) generally legally binding.
    D) made by the parties themselves.
    E) only an opinion.

    **Answer:** C) generally legally binding.
    **Explanation:** In arbitration, the arbitrator "make[s] a binding decision (an award)," which is generally legally enforceable, unlike in mediation where the mediator facilitates a non-binding agreement.

71. **Which of the following courts typically has original jurisdiction over serious criminal cases like murder?**
    A) Magistrate Court
    B) High Court
    C) Small Claims Court
    D) Customary Court
    E) Court of Appeal

    **Answer:** B) High Court
    **Explanation:** High Courts are described as having jurisdiction over "serious criminal cases (e.g., felonies like murder, robbery, treason)."

72. **Customary law is based on**
    A) the national constitution.
    B) acts of parliament.
    C) long-established traditions and norms of a community.
    D) decisions of the Supreme Court.
    E) international treaties.

    **Answer:** C) long-established traditions and norms of a community.
    **Explanation:** Customary law is defined as being "based on the long-established customs, traditions, and social norms of a particular indigenous community."

73. **Alternative Dispute Resolution (ADR) mechanisms are often preferred to court litigation because they are**
    A) more formal and complex.
    B) always more expensive.
    C) generally faster and less adversarial.
    D) available only for criminal cases.
    E) guaranteed to be public.

    **Answer:** C) generally faster and less adversarial.
    **Explanation:** The text lists advantages of ADR as being "quicker, less expensive, more flexible, and less adversarial than going to court."

74. **In a civil case, the party bringing the lawsuit is called the**
    A) Defendant.
    B) Prosecutor.
    C) Plaintiff.
    D) Judge.
    E) Witness.

    **Answer:** C) Plaintiff.
    **Explanation:** Civil law deals with disputes between a "plaintiff vs. defendant." The plaintiff is the one who initiates the case.

75. **The financial security of judges is important for judicial independence because it**
    A) makes them wealthy.
    B) allows politicians to control their salaries.
    C) prevents them from being pressured through their pay.
    D) requires them to pay for court expenses.
    E) allows them to take bribes.

    **Answer:** C) prevents them from being pressured through their pay.
    **Explanation:** Financial security, where judges' salaries are secure and not subject to arbitrary reduction, is a mechanism to "prevent financial pressure" and ensure independence.

---

### **Chapter 7: Electoral Processes and Systems**

76. **The First-Past-the-Post (FPTP) electoral system is also known as the**
    A) Proportional Representation system.
    B) Two-Round system.
    C) Winner-Takes-All system.
    D) Mixed Member system.
    E) Alternative Vote system.

    **Answer:** C) Winner-Takes-All system.
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the First-Past-the-Post (FPTP) system is also referred to as a "Plurality System or Winner-Takes-All."

77. **A major disadvantage of the FPTP system is that it can**
    A) lead to unstable coalition governments.
    B) give smaller parties fair representation.
    C) result in a party winning a majority of seats without a majority of votes.
    D) be very complex for voters to understand.
    E) ensure every vote counts towards representation.

    **Answer:** C) result in a party winning a majority of seats without a majority of votes.
    **Explanation:** A key disadvantage of FPTP is that it "Often results in disproportionate representation: a party can win a majority of seats without winning a majority of the national vote."

78. **Proportional Representation (PR) systems are designed to**
    A) ensure a single-party government.
    B) allocate seats to parties in proportion to their vote share.
    C) elect one representative per constituency.
    D) make it difficult for small parties to win seats.
    E) simplify the vote counting process.

    **Answer:** B) allocate seats to parties in proportion to their vote share.
    **Explanation:** PR systems "Aim to allocate seats to political parties in direct proportion to their share of the national vote."

79. **A key advantage of the Proportional Representation system is**
    A) its simplicity.
    B) the creation of stable, single-party governments.
    C) the strong link between a representative and a specific constituency.
    D) fairer representation for smaller parties and minority groups.
    E) the elimination of political parties.

    **Answer:** D) fairer representation for smaller parties and minority groups.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Fairer representation of parties' share of the vote" and "Better representation for smaller parties and minority groups" as key advantages of PR.

80. **The process by which eligible citizens are enrolled on an official list to vote is called**
    A) Voter campaigning.
    B) Voter registration.
    C) Voter education.
    D) Voter monitoring.
    E) Voter collation.

    **Answer:** B) Voter registration.
    **Explanation:** Voter registration is defined as "the process by which eligible citizens are enrolled on an official list (voters' register or electoral roll)."

81. **The independent body responsible for organizing and supervising elections in a country is the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Executive.
    C) Legislature.
    D) Electoral Management Body (EMB).
    E) National Security Council.

    **Answer:** D) Electoral Management Body (EMB).
    **Explanation:** The Electoral Management Body (EMB), such as an Electoral Commission, is the "independent or autonomous body...responsible for organizing, conducting, and supervising elections."

82. **A major barrier to women's participation in politics mentioned in the text is**
    A) lack of interest in public affairs.
    B) constitutional prohibition.
    C) socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes.
    D) the absence of political parties.
    E) low population of women.

    **Answer:** C) socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Socio-cultural norms and patriarchal attitudes that assign women to domestic roles" as a key barrier to women's political participation.

83. **A two-round electoral system is used to ensure the winner achieves**
    A) a plurality of votes.
    B) a fixed number of votes.
    C) an absolute majority of votes.
    D) the support of all parties.
    E) the approval of the incumbent.

    **Answer:** C) an absolute majority of votes.
    **Explanation:** A majority system like the two-round system ensures that a candidate "must win an absolute majority (more than 50%) of votes to be elected," often by holding a runoff if no one achieves it in the first round.

84. **Which of the following is NOT a function of an Electoral Management Body?**
    A) Voter registration
    B) Delimitation of constituencies
    C) Announcing official results
    D) Formulating party manifestos
    E) Civic and voter education

    **Answer:** D) Formulating party manifestos
    **Explanation:** Formulating manifestos is a function of political parties. The EMB is responsible for the administration of the election process itself.

85. **The observation of an election by independent domestic and international groups is called**
    A) Election rigging.
    B) Election campaigning.
    C) Election monitoring.
    D) Election petition.
    E) Election financing.

    **Answer:** C) Election monitoring.
    **Explanation:** Election monitoring (or observation) is defined as "The observation of an election process by independent domestic and/or international observers to assess its fairness."

86. **Reserved seats or party list quotas are strategies used to enhance the political participation of**
    A) the elderly.
    B) the military.
    C) women.
    D) the judiciary.
    E) civil servants.

    **Answer:** C) women.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Quotas (reserved seats or party list quotas) for women in legislatures" as a strategy to enhance women's political participation.

87. **The secrecy of the ballot is essential in an election to**
    A) make counting difficult.
    B) ensure voters can make free choices without intimidation.
    C) help the ruling party win.
    D) allow for multiple voting.
    E) identify who voted for whom.

    **Answer:** B) ensure voters can make free choices without intimidation.
    **Explanation:** The principle of a secret ballot is fundamental to democratic elections as it protects voters from coercion and allows them to express their true preference.

88. **A system that combines elements of both FPTP and PR is known as a**
    A) Unitary system.
    B) Direct system.
    C) Mixed electoral system.
    D) Unicameral system.
    E) Plurality system.

    **Answer:** C) Mixed electoral system.
    **Explanation:** Mixed Electoral Systems are defined as those that "Combine elements of both plurality/majority systems (like FPTP) and proportional representation systems."

89. **A major barrier to youth participation in politics is often**
    A) lack of physical strength.
    B) constitutional bans on youth voting.
    C) the perception of politics as being dominated by older generations.
    D) the absence of social media.
    E) youth having no opinions.

    **Answer:** C) the perception of politics as being dominated by older generations.
    **Explanation:** The text lists the "Perception of politics as corrupt or dominated by older generations" as a significant barrier for youth.

90. **In an election, a political campaign is primarily designed to**
    A) count votes accurately.
    B) persuade voters to support a candidate or party.
    C) register eligible voters.
    D) monitor the fairness of the poll.
    E) adjudicate electoral disputes.

    **Answer:** B) persuade voters to support a candidate or party.
    **Explanation:** Political campaigning is defined as "The process by which political parties and candidates try to persuade voters to support them in an election."

---

### **Chapter 8: Political Parties and Interest Groups**

91. **A primary difference between a political party and an interest group is that a political party**
    A) seeks to influence policy without controlling government.
    B) is not interested in elections.
    C) seeks to win elections and control government.
    D) focuses only on a single issue.
    E) is usually smaller and less organized.

    **Answer:** C) seeks to win elections and control government.
    **Explanation:** Political parties "seek to win elections and control government," whereas interest groups primarily aim to influence policy from the outside.

92. **Which of the following is a key function of political parties?**
    A) Adjudicating legal cases
    B) Commanding the armed forces
    C) Recruiting and nominating candidates for office
    D) Maintaining political neutrality
    E) Collecting taxes

    **Answer:** C) Recruiting and nominating candidates for office
    **Explanation:** "Recruit and Nominate Candidates" is listed as a primary function of political parties.

93. **A document outlining a political party's policies and programs is its**
    A) Constitution.
    B) Budget.
    C) Platform or Manifesto.
    D) Court ruling.
    E) Civil service code.

    **Answer:** C) Platform or Manifesto.
    **Explanation:** Parties "Formulate Policies and Programs (Party Platform/Manifesto)" to present their goals to the electorate.

94. **An interest group representing the Nigerian Medical Association or a Bar Association is an example of a(n)**
    A) Business group.
    B) Labor union.
    C) Professional association.
    D) Single-issue group.
    E) Ethnic group.

    **Answer:** C) Professional association.
    **Explanation:** Professional associations are defined as groups that "Represent members of specific professions (e.g., medical associations, bar associations for lawyers)."

95. **Lobbying is a technique used by interest groups to**
    A) directly run for office.
    B) influence policymakers.
    C) count votes in an election.
    D) provide social welfare to the poor.
    E) protest violently.

    **Answer:** B) influence policymakers.
    **Explanation:** Interest groups (or pressure groups) use lobbying techniques to "influence public policy and government decisions." Direct lobbying involves direct contact with policymakers.

96. **The media's role in scrutinizing government actions and exposing corruption is known as its**
    A) entertainment role.
    B) advertising role.
    C) watchdog role.
    D) socialization role.
    E) mobilization role.

    **Answer:** C) watchdog role.
    **Explanation:** The media's "Watchdog Role" involves "Investigating and exposing corruption, mismanagement, or abuses of power by government officials."

97. **The "third sector" of society, distinct from government and business, is called**
    A) Civil society.
    B) The judiciary.
    C) The electorate.
    D) The bureaucracy.
    E) The cabinet.

    **Answer:** A) Civil society.
    **Explanation:** Civil society is defined as "the 'third sector' of society, distinct from government (state) and business (market)," comprising various non-governmental organizations.

98. **Grassroots lobbying involves**
    A) secretly meeting with high-level officials.
    B) mobilizing the public and group members to contact policymakers.
    C) filing lawsuits in the Supreme Court.
    D) donating large sums of money to campaigns.
    E) writing academic articles.

    **Answer:** B) mobilizing the public and group members to contact policymakers.
    **Explanation:** Grassroots lobbying is an indirect technique that involves "Mobilizing their members and the public to contact policymakers."

99. **Laws that regulate the sources and amounts of funding for political campaigns are known as**
    A) Electoral system laws.
    B) Campaign finance regulations.
    C) Party registration laws.
    D) Media ethics codes.
    E) Lobbying disclosure acts.

    **Answer:** B) Campaign finance regulations.
    **Explanation:** Campaign finance regulation refers to laws designed to "regulate campaign finance, which may include...limits on contributions...disclosure of donors."

100. **Spreading false rumors and engaging in character assassination during an election are examples of**
    A) effective campaign strategy.
    B) voter education.
    C) unethical campaign practices.
    D) interest articulation.
    E) policy formulation.

    **Answer:** C) unethical campaign practices.
    **Explanation:** The text lists "spreading false rumors, character assassination" as unethical practices that violate principles of honesty and fairness in elections.

101. **Which of the following is NOT a type of economic interest group?**
    A) Labor unions
    B) Business groups
    C) Agricultural groups
    D) Human rights organizations
    E) Professional associations

    **Answer:** D) Human rights organizations
    **Explanation:** Human rights organizations are classified as social or public interest groups, focusing on broad societal values rather than the specific economic interests of their members.

102. **A Political Action Committee (PAC) is primarily involved in**
    A) formulating public policy.
    B) providing legal aid.
    C) grassroots mobilization.
    D) contributing financially to political campaigns.
    E) monitoring elections.

    **Answer:** D) contributing financially to political campaigns.
    **Explanation:** Electioneering, including the use of PACs, is a method used by interest groups for "contributing to campaigns (where legally permitted)."

103. **The media's ability to influence which issues are seen as important by the public is its \_\_\_\_\_ function.**
    A) agenda-setting
    B) watchdog
    C) entertainment
    D) economic
    E) procedural

    **Answer:** A) agenda-setting
    **Explanation:** The agenda-setting function of the media refers to its role in "Influencing which issues are perceived as important by the public and policymakers."

104. **A key ethical principle for political campaigns is**
    A) winning at all costs.
    B) focusing only on an opponent's personal life.
    C) transparency in funding.
    D) discouraging voter turnout.
    E) using state resources for private campaigns.

    **Answer:** C) transparency in funding.
    **Explanation:** Transparency, including "Openness about funding sources," is listed as a key ethical principle in elections.

105. **An interest group focused solely on environmental protection is best described as a**
    A) labor union.
    B) professional association.
    C) business group.
    D) single-issue group.
    E) political party.

    **Answer:** D) single-issue group.
    **Explanation:** Single-issue groups "Focus intensely on one specific issue," and environmental protection is a classic example. It also falls under the broader category of a public interest group.

---

### **Chapter 9: Policy‐Making Process and Implementation**

106. **The first stage in the public policy cycle is**
    A) Policy evaluation.
    B) Policy implementation.
    C) Agenda setting.
    D) Policy adoption.
    E) Budget allocation.

    **Answer:** C) Agenda setting.
    **Explanation:** The policy cycle begins with agenda setting, which is "The process by which certain problems or issues come to the attention of policymakers."

107. **Policy formulation is the stage where**
    A) a law is formally passed.
    B) potential solutions and alternatives are developed.
    C) the success of a policy is measured.
    D) a policy is put into action.
    E) a problem is first identified.

    **Answer:** B) potential solutions and alternatives are developed.
    **Explanation:** Policy formulation is defined as "The development of potential solutions or alternative courses of action (policy options)."

108. **When a legislature passes a law, it is engaging in which stage of the policy cycle?**
    A) Policy formulation
    B) Policy adoption
    C) Policy implementation
    D) Agenda setting
    E) Policy evaluation

    **Answer:** B) Policy adoption
    **Explanation:** Policy adoption is the stage where a policy is "formally chosen and authorized," and the "Passage of a law by the legislature" is a key mechanism for this.

109. **The primary responsibility for policy implementation lies with the**
    A) Judiciary.
    B) Electorate.
    C) Media.
    D) Bureaucracy.
    E) Interest groups.

    **Answer:** D) Bureaucracy.
    **Explanation:** The bureaucracy (civil service, government agencies) is described as the "Primary implementers" of public policy.

110. **Which of the following is a major challenge to effective policy implementation?**
    A) Clear policy objectives
    B) Strong political will
    C) High levels of public support
    D) Insufficient resources
    E) Excellent coordination

    **Answer:** D) Insufficient resources
    **Explanation:** The text lists "Insufficient Resources: Lack of adequate funding, personnel, or technical capacity" as a key challenge to policy implementation.

111. **The systematic assessment of a policy's effectiveness and impact is called**
    A) Policy formulation.
    B) Policy adoption.
    C) Policy evaluation.
    D) Agenda setting.
    E) Lobbying.

    **Answer:** C) Policy evaluation.
    **Explanation:** Policy evaluation is defined as "The systematic assessment of the effectiveness, efficiency, and impact of a public policy."

112. **A major crisis like a natural disaster or economic recession often serves as a**
    A) policy outcome.
    B) focusing event for agenda setting.
    C) method of policy evaluation.
    D) tool for implementation.
    E) final stage of the policy cycle.

    **Answer:** B) a focusing event for agenda setting.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that "Crises and Focusing Events" can "thrust issues onto the agenda," making them a priority for government action.

113. **Cost-benefit analysis is a tool used primarily during**
    A) policy evaluation and formulation.
    B) agenda setting.
    C) political campaigning.
    D) judicial review.
    E) voting.

    **Answer:** A) policy evaluation and formulation.
    **Explanation:** Cost-benefit analysis is used to assess the feasibility of different options during formulation and to evaluate the economic efficiency of an implemented policy.

114. **When a government agency creates specific rules to carry out a broad law passed by the legislature, it is engaged in**
    A) agenda setting.
    B) policy implementation.
    C) election monitoring.
    D) judicial adjudication.
    E) constitutional amendment.

    **Answer:** B) policy implementation.
    **Explanation:** "Developing Rules, Regulations, and Guidelines" is listed as a key activity in the policy implementation stage, translating broad policy into operational procedures.

115. **The feedback loop in the policy cycle connects policy evaluation to**
    A) the end of the process.
    B) judicial punishment.
    C) future agenda setting and policy formulation.
    D) the private sector only.
    E) international diplomacy.

    **Answer:** C) future agenda setting and policy formulation.
    **Explanation:** The text describes the policy process as a cycle where "evaluation and feedback lead to policy review, potential reformulation of problems or solutions, and new rounds of decision-making."

---

### **Chapter 10: Human Rights, Civil Liberties, and Social Justice**

116. **"Freedom from" external interference, such as censorship or arbitrary arrest, is known as**
    A) Positive liberty.
    B) Negative liberty.
    C) Equality of outcome.
    D) Distributive justice.
    E) Social welfare.

    **Answer:** B) Negative liberty.
    **Explanation:** Negative liberty is defined as "Freedom from external interference, constraints, or oppression."

117. **The idea that all individuals should have an equal chance to succeed, regardless of their background, is**
    A) Equality of outcome.
    B) Equality of opportunity.
    C) Formal equality.
    D) Retributive justice.
    E) Positive liberty.

    **Answer:** B) Equality of opportunity.
    **Explanation:** Equality of opportunity is the principle that "All individuals should have an equal chance to succeed in life, regardless of their background."

118. **The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) was adopted by the**
    A) African Union.
    B) European Union.
    C) United Nations General Assembly.
    D) World Bank.
    E) League of Nations.

    **Answer:** C) United Nations General Assembly.
    **Explanation:** The text clearly states that the UDHR was "Adopted by the United Nations General Assembly on December 10, 1948."

119. **A unique feature of the African Charter on Human and Peoples' Rights is its recognition of**
    A) only economic rights.
    B) only individual rights.
    C) individual rights, peoples' rights, and individual duties.
    D) the right to own weapons.
    E) the supremacy of customary law over all other laws.

    **Answer:** C) individual rights, peoples' rights, and individual duties.
    **Explanation:** The African Charter is described as unique because it "recognizes not only individual rights but also peoples' rights...and individual duties."

120. **Which of the following is a justifiable limitation on freedom of speech?**
    A) Criticism of the government
    B) Unpopular opinions
    C) Incitement to violence
    D) Religious expression
    E) Academic debate

    **Answer:** C) Incitement to violence
    **Explanation:** Freedom of speech is not absolute and can be limited for reasons like national security or public order, with "incitement to violence" being a classic example.

121. **The right to form and join groups like political parties and trade unions is the freedom of**
    A) speech.
    B) religion.
    C) assembly.
    D) association.
    E) movement.

    **Answer:** D) association.
    **Explanation:** Freedom of association is defined as "The right of individuals to form and join groups or organizations."

122. **Amnesty International and Human Rights Watch are examples of**
    A) government agencies.
    B) international financial institutions.
    C) political parties.
    D) non-governmental organizations (NGOs).
    E) intergovernmental organizations.

    **Answer:** D) non-governmental organizations (NGOs).
    **Explanation:** These are cited as examples of international human rights NGOs that operate independently of government.

123. **A National Human Rights Institution (NHRI) is a(n)**
    A) private company focused on profit.
    B) international court for all crimes.
    C) independent public body established by a state to protect human rights.
    D) political party focused on human rights issues.
    E) armed rebel group.

    **Answer:** C) independent public body established by a state to protect human rights.
    **Explanation:** NHRIs are defined as "Independent public bodies established by government...with a mandate to promote and protect human rights within a country."

124. **The concept of social justice is primarily concerned with**
    A) punishing criminals severely.
    B) fairness and equity in the distribution of resources and opportunities.
    C) ensuring maximum profit for businesses.
    D) the freedom of individuals from all government control.
    E) maintaining traditional social hierarchies.

    **Answer:** B) fairness and equity in the distribution of resources and opportunities.
