    **Explanation:** Diplomacy is defined as "The art and practice of conducting negotiations and managing relations between states."

134. **NATO is an example of a(n)**
    A) international financial institution.
    B) defensive alliance.
    C) global government.
    D) single-issue interest group.
    E) regional trade bloc.

    **Answer:** B) defensive alliance.
    **Explanation:** NATO is cited as an example of a defensive alliance, where "Members pledge to come to each other's aid if one is attacked."

135. **The increasing interconnectedness of societies worldwide, driven by technology and trade, is known as**
    A) Nationalism.
    B) Isolationism.
    C) Globalization.
    D) Regionalism.
    E) Colonialism.

    **Answer:** C) Globalization.
    **Explanation:** Globalization is defined as the "process characterized by increasing interconnectedness and interdependence between countries and societies worldwide."

136. **Feminist IR theory primarily critiques traditional theories for**
    A) being too focused on economics.
    B) ignoring the role of gender in shaping international relations.
    C) being too optimistic about peace.
    D) overemphasizing international law.
    E) neglecting the state as an actor.

    **Answer:** B) ignoring the role of gender in shaping international relations.
    **Explanation:** A core assumption of Feminist IR is that traditional theories "have often ignored or marginalized women's experiences...and have been based on masculine assumptions."

137. **A situation where states form coalitions to prevent any single state from becoming too powerful is a**
    A) collective security system.
    B) world government.
    C) balance of power.
    D) global empire.
    E) unipolar system.

    **Answer:** C) balance of power.
    **Explanation:** The balance of power concept describes a situation where states "strive to ensure that no single state or bloc becomes overwhelmingly powerful." Forming alliances (external balancing) is a key mechanism.

138. **The principle of non-intervention is a key aspect of**
    A) Globalization.
    B) Sovereignty.
    C) Humanitarianism.
    D) Internationalism.
    E) Dependency.

    **Answer:** B) Sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** The text lists non-intervention ("Other states should not interfere in the domestic affairs of a sovereign state") as a key aspect of Westphalian sovereignty.

139. **According to realist theory, the most important national interest is**
    A) promoting human rights.
    B) economic prosperity.
    C) survival and security.
    D) spreading democratic values.
    E) environmental protection.

    **Answer:** C) survival and security.
    **Explanation:** The text notes that "Survival and Security" is the "primary interest for Realists."

140. **A major political implication of globalization is that it**
    A) strengthens state sovereignty.
    B) challenges state sovereignty.
    C) eliminates international organizations.
    D) stops all international trade.
    E) promotes isolationism.

    **Answer:** B) challenges state sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** One of the key political implications of globalization discussed is that it "Challenges to State Sovereignty" through the influence of IOs, international law, and transnational actors.

---

### **Chapter 12: International Organizations and Treaties**

141. **The principal organ of the UN responsible for maintaining international peace and security is the**
    A) General Assembly.
    B) Security Council.
    C) International Court of Justice.
    D) Secretariat.
    E) ECOSOC.

    **Answer:** B) Security Council.
    **Explanation:** The UN Security Council has the "Primary responsibility for maintaining international peace and security."

142. **The veto power in the UN Security Council is held exclusively by the**
    A) 10 non-permanent members.
    B) Secretary-General.
    C) 5 permanent members.
    D) President of the General Assembly.
    E) Judges of the ICJ.

    **Answer:** C) 5 permanent members.
    **Explanation:** The five permanent members (P5: China, France, Russia, UK, US) have veto power, meaning a negative vote from any of them can block a resolution.

143. **The UN General Assembly operates on the principle of**
    A) weighted voting based on financial contribution.
    B) one state, one vote.
    C) permanent member veto power.
    D) qualified majority voting.
    E) consensus only.

    **Answer:** B) one state, one vote.
    **Explanation:** The General Assembly is where "All member states have equal representation (one state, one vote)."

144. **Which regional organization aims to promote economic integration and has a peacekeeping force in West Africa?**
    A) European Union (EU)
    B) African Union (AU)
    C) Association of Southeast Asian Nations (ASEAN)
    D) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS)
E) North Atlantic Treaty Organization (NATO)

    **Answer:** D) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS)
    **Explanation:** ECOWAS is the regional organization for West Africa, noted for its focus on economic integration and its history of peacekeeping (e.g., ECOMOG).

145. **The process by which a state gives its formal approval to a treaty, making it legally binding, is called**
    A) Signature.
    B) Negotiation.
    C) Adoption.
    D) Ratification.
    E) Proclamation.

    **Answer:** D) Ratification.
    **Explanation:** Ratification is the "formal approval by a state according to its constitutional procedures, making it legally binding on that state."

146. **The Geneva Conventions are international treaties primarily concerned with**
    A) international trade.
    B) environmental protection.
    C) the laws of war and protection of civilians.
    D) the non-proliferation of nuclear weapons.
    E) the law of the sea.

    **Answer:** C) the laws of war and protection of civilians.
    **Explanation:** The Geneva Conventions are a key set of treaties in international humanitarian law, dealing with the treatment of non-combatants and prisoners during armed conflict.

147. **The international norm that states have a responsibility to protect their populations from atrocities is known as**
    A) Pacta sunt servanda.
    B) Responsibility to Protect (R2P).
    C) The Paris Agreement.
    D) The UN Charter.
    E) Humanitarian neutrality.

    **Answer:** B) Responsibility to Protect (R2P).
    **Explanation:** R2P is the "emerging international norm...asserting that...States have a primary responsibility to protect their own populations from genocide, war crimes, ethnic cleansing, and crimes against humanity."

148. **UN peacekeeping operations are deployed with the**
    A) consent of the host state.
    B) sole authority of the Secretary-General.
    C) order of the International Court of Justice.
    D) approval of all UN member states.
    E) funding of the World Bank only.

    **Answer:** A) consent of the host state.
    **Explanation:** "Consent of parties" is listed as a core principle of UN peacekeeping.

149. **The African Union (AU) is the successor to the**
    A) League of Nations.
    B) Economic Community of West African States (ECOWAS).
    C) Organization of African Unity (OAU).
    D) Non-Aligned Movement.
    E) Commonwealth of Nations.

    **Answer:** C) Organization of African Unity (OAU).
    **Explanation:** The text explicitly states that the AU, established in 2002, is the "successor to the Organization of African Unity - OAU, founded 1963."

150. **The principal judicial organ of the United Nations is the**
    A) International Criminal Court (ICC).
    B) European Court of Human Rights.
    C) African Court on Human and Peoples' Rights.
    D) International Court of Justice (ICJ).
    E) UN Security Council.

    **Answer:** D) International Court of Justice (ICJ).
    **Explanation:** The ICJ (or World Court) is defined as "The principal judicial organ of the UN."

151. **A legally binding written agreement between states governed by international law is a(n)**
    A) Resolution.
    B) Declaration.
    C) Treaty.
    D) Communiqué.
    E) Sanction.

    **Answer:** C) Treaty.
    **Explanation:** A treaty is defined as a "Formal, legally binding written agreement between states." Resolutions and declarations are often non-binding.

152. **The Paris Agreement is a major international treaty that deals with**
    A) Human rights.
    B) Trade liberalization.
    C) Arms control.
    D) Climate change.
    E) Internet governance.

    **Answer:** D) Climate change.
    **Explanation:** The text lists the Paris Agreement under the UNFCCC as a key environmental treaty aiming to "limit global warming."

153. **Post-conflict efforts to rebuild institutions and prevent the recurrence of violence are called**
    A) Peacekeeping.
    B) Peacemaking.
    C) Peacebuilding.
    D) Humanitarian intervention.
    E) Sanctions.

    **Answer:** C) Peacebuilding.
    **Explanation:** Peacebuilding is defined as "Post-conflict efforts to rebuild institutions, address root causes of conflict, promote reconciliation, and prevent recurrence of violence."

154. **Which of the following is NOT a principal organ of the UN?**
    A) General Assembly
    B) Security Council
    C) World Health Organization (WHO)
    D) ECOSOC
    E) Secretariat

    **Answer:** C) World Health Organization (WHO)
    **Explanation:** The WHO is a very important UN Specialized Agency, but it is not one of the six principal organs established by the UN Charter. The others listed are principal organs.

155. **The European Union (EU) is best described as a**
    A) military alliance.
    B) loose association for cultural exchange.
    C) regional organization with a deep level of economic and political integration.
    D) global financial institution.
    E) human rights NGO.

    **Answer:** C) a regional organization with a deep level of economic and political integration.
    **Explanation:** The EU is described as a "unique economic and political partnership" with a "deep level of integration," including a single market and supranational institutions.

---

### **Chapter 13: Concepts of Development**

156. **The concept of development that focuses primarily on the increase in a country's economic output (GDP) is**
    A) Human development.
    B) Sustainable development.
    C) Economic growth.
    D) Political development.
    E) Social development.

    **Answer:** C) Economic growth.
    **Explanation:** Economic growth is defined as focusing "primarily on the increase in a country's output of goods and services, typically measured by...GDP."

157. **The Human Development Index (HDI) does NOT include which of the following indicators?**
    A) Life expectancy at birth
    B) Mean years of schooling
    C) Gross National Income (GNI) per capita
    D) Political freedom and human rights
    E) Expected years of schooling

    **Answer:** D) Political freedom and human rights
    **Explanation:** The HDI combines three dimensions: health (life expectancy), education (schooling years), and standard of living (GNI per capita). While human development as a concept includes freedoms, they are not part of the formal HDI calculation.

158. **Sustainable development aims to meet the needs of the present without**
    A) compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
    B) any economic growth.
    C) involving the private sector.
    D) seeking international aid.
    E) addressing social inequality.

    **Answer:** A) compromising the ability of future generations to meet their own needs.
    **Explanation:** This is the classic definition of sustainable development from the Brundtland Commission, as cited in the text.

159. **The GINI coefficient is a measure of**
    A) economic growth.
    B) human development.
    C) income or wealth inequality.
    D) absolute poverty.
    E) infrastructure quality.

    **Answer:** C) income or wealth inequality.
    **Explanation:** The GINI coefficient is defined as "A measure of income inequality or wealth inequality within a nation."

160. **A GINI coefficient of 0 represents**
    A) perfect inequality.
    B) perfect equality.
    C) high levels of poverty.
    D) rapid economic growth.
    E) a stable economy.

    **Answer:** B) perfect equality.
    **Explanation:** The text explains that a GINI of 0 signifies "Perfect equality (everyone has the same income/wealth)."

161. **The primary mandate of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) is to**
    A) fund long-term infrastructure projects.
    B) ensure the stability of the international monetary system.
    C) promote free trade exclusively.
    D) provide humanitarian aid during disasters.
    E) eradicate specific diseases.

    **Answer:** B) ensure the stability of the international monetary system.
    **Explanation:** The IMF's primary mandate is "To ensure the stability of the international monetary system," primarily by helping countries with balance of payments problems.

162. **The World Bank's main focus is on**
    A) short-term emergency loans.
    B) global military security.
    C) long-term economic development and poverty reduction.
    D) managing global exchange rates.
    E) regulating international corporations.

    **Answer:** C) long-term economic development and poverty reduction.
    **Explanation:** The World Bank's focus is on "long-term economic development and poverty reduction in developing countries" through loans and grants for projects.

163. **The African Development Bank (AfDB) is a**
    A) global institution for all countries.
    B) private commercial bank.
    C) regional multilateral development finance institution for Africa.
    D) human rights organization.
    E) political union of African states.

    **Answer:** C) regional multilateral development finance institution for Africa.
    **Explanation:** The AfDB is defined as a "regional multilateral development finance institution...to promote the economic development and social progress of African countries."

164. **Policies that a country must implement to receive a loan from the IMF or World Bank are known as**
    A) Grants.
    B) Conditionalities.
    C) Tariffs.
    D) Quotas.
    E) Subsidies.

    **Answer:** B) Conditionalities.
    **Explanation:** Loans from IFIs often come with "conditionalities (policy reforms the country must undertake)," historically known as Structural Adjustment Programs (SAPs).

165. **Which of the following is an example of an infrastructure deficit?**
    A) High literacy rate
    B) Low unemployment
    C) A stable government
    D) Poor road networks and unreliable electricity
    E) High GDP per capita

    **Answer:** D) Poor road networks and unreliable electricity
    **Explanation:** Infrastructure deficit refers to the lack of adequate physical infrastructure, with "Poor transportation networks (roads...)" and "Inadequate energy supply (electricity)" being prime examples.

166. **The Multidimensional Poverty Index (MPI) measures poverty based on**
    A) income alone.
    B) a person's social class.
    C) deprivations in health, education, and living standards.
    D) the national unemployment rate.
    E) a country's GINI coefficient.

    **Answer:** C) deprivations in health, education, and living standards.
    **Explanation:** The MPI is described as measuring poverty "based on deprivations in multiple dimensions (health, education, living standards) rather than just income."

167. **Which concept of development puts the expansion of people's capabilities and freedoms at its center?**
    A) Economic growth
    B) Human development
    C) Industrial development
    D) Political development
    E) Agrarian development

    **Answer:** B) Human development
    **Explanation:** Human development "focuses on expanding people's choices, capabilities, and freedoms, and improving their well-being."

168. **Which of the following is NOT one of the three core dimensions of sustainable development?**
    A) Economic sustainability
    B) Social sustainability
    C) Environmental sustainability
    D) Military sustainability
    E) None of the above

    **Answer:** D) Military sustainability
    **Explanation:** The three integrated dimensions or pillars of sustainable development are economic, social, and environmental.

169. **Underemployment refers to a situation where people are**
    A) actively seeking work but cannot find any.
    B) working in jobs below their skill level or for fewer hours than they want.
    C) retired from the workforce.
    D) not looking for a job.
    E) working in the public sector.

    **Answer:** B) working in jobs below their skill level or for fewer hours than they want.
    **Explanation:** Underemployment includes "working part-time when they want full-time work, or working in jobs below their skill level."

170. **The Sustainable Development Goals (SDGs) provide a global framework for development to be achieved by**
    A) 2025.
    B) 2030.
    C) 2040.
    D) 2050.
    E) 2063.

    **Answer:** B) 2030.
    **Explanation:** The text states the SDGs "provide a global framework for achieving sustainable development by 2030."

---

### **Chapter 14: Public Administration and Leadership**

171. **A common criticism of bureaucracy, referring to its focus on rules over goals, is**
    A) Goal achievement.
    B) Goal displacement.
    C) Goal setting.
    D) Goal orientation.
    E) Goal alignment.

    **Answer:** B) Goal displacement.
    **Explanation:** Goal displacement is a listed criticism of bureaucracy, described as "Focusing on rules and procedures for their own sake rather than achieving the original policy goals."

172. **The leadership theory that focuses on an exchange relationship using rewards and punishments is**
    A) Transformational.
    B) Transactional.
    C) Servant.
    D) Trait.
    E) Situational.

    **Answer:** B) Transactional.
    **Explanation:** Transactional leadership "Focuses on the exchange relationship between leaders and followers...using rewards...and punishments."

173. **A leader who inspires followers with a compelling vision and encourages creativity is best described as**
    A) Transactional.
    B) Laissez-faire.
    C) Autocratic.
    D) Transformational.
    E) Bureaucratic.

    **Answer:** D) Transformational.
    **Explanation:** Transformational leadership involves "Inspirational Motivation (Articulates a compelling vision)" and "Intellectual Stimulation (encourages creativity)."

174. **The core principle of Servant Leadership is**
    A) putting the leader's needs first.
    B) a focus on transactions and exchanges.
    C) putting the well-being of followers and the community first.
    D) maintaining power through coercion.
    E) adherence to rigid rules.

    **Answer:** C) putting the well-being of followers and the community first.
    **Explanation:** Servant leadership "Focuses primarily on the growth and well-being of people and the communities...puts the needs of others first."

175. **Which of the following is a core ethical value in public service?**
    A) Self-interest
    B) Partisan advantage
    C) Integrity
    D) Secrecy
    E) Nepotism

    **Answer:** C) Integrity
    **Explanation:** Integrity, defined as honesty and incorruptibility, is listed as a core ethical value for public servants. The other options are examples of unethical behavior.

176. **The abuse of public office for private gain is known as**
    A) Philanthropy.
    B) Public service.
    C) Accountability.
    D) Corruption.
    E) Transparency.

    **Answer:** D) Corruption.
    **Explanation:** Corruption is defined as "The abuse of entrusted power or public office for private gain."

177. **Showing undue favor to relatives in public appointments is an example of**
    A) Embezzlement.
    B) Bribery.
    C) Nepotism.
    D) Fraud.
    E) Extortion.

    **Answer:** C) Nepotism.
    **Explanation:** Nepotism is a form of corruption defined as "Showing undue favor to relatives or friends in appointments or contracts."

178. **The use of ICT by government to provide services and improve efficiency is called**
    A) E-commerce.
    B) E-learning.
    C) E-government.
    D) E-mail.
    E) E-procurement.

    **Answer:** C) E-government.
    **Explanation:** E-government is defined as "The use of Information and Communication Technologies (ICTs) by government agencies to provide information and services."

179. **A major challenge for the implementation of e-government is the**
    A) Digital divide.
    B) High level of transparency it creates.
    C) Increased efficiency.
    D) Reduction in corruption.
    E) Over-supply of ICT professionals.

    **Answer:** A) Digital divide.
    **Explanation:** The "Digital divide (unequal access to ICTs)" is listed as a key challenge for e-government, as it can exclude citizens without internet access or digital literacy.

180. **New Public Management (NPM) is an administrative reform trend that seeks to**
    A) increase the size of the bureaucracy.
    B) introduce private sector management techniques into the public sector.
    C) centralize all decision-making.
    D) eliminate performance measurement.
    E) reduce transparency and accountability.

    **Answer:** B) introduce private sector management techniques into the public sector.
    **Explanation:** New Public Management (NPM) is described as "Introducing market-based principles and private sector management techniques into public sector."

181. **Protecting individuals who report corruption is a key feature of**
    A) Asset declaration laws.
    B) Whistleblower protection laws.
    C) Freedom of Information acts.
    D) Codes of conduct.
    E) Anti-corruption agencies.

    **Answer:** B) Whistleblower protection laws.
    **Explanation:** Whistleblower protection is an anti-corruption measure designed to protect "individuals who report corruption."

182. **A system of setting goals, monitoring progress, and evaluating outcomes in public agencies is called**
    A) Performance management.
    B) Public relations.
    C) Crisis management.
    D) Financial accounting.
    E) Bureaucratic recruitment.

    **Answer:** A) Performance management.
    **Explanation:** Performance management is defined as "A systematic process of planning, monitoring, evaluating, and improving the performance of government organizations."

183. **The ethical principle of making decisions based on merit, without bias or favoritism, is**
    A) Integrity.
    B) Honesty.
    C) Diligence.
    D) Objectivity.
    E) Confidentiality.

    **Answer:** D) Objectivity.
    **Explanation:** Objectivity (or Impartiality) is defined as "Making decisions based on merit, evidence, and law, without bias, favoritism, or prejudice."

184. **Which leadership theory argues that the most effective style depends on the context and followers?**
    A) Trait Theory
    B) Transactional Theory
    C) Contingency Theory
    D) Behavioral Theory
    E) Servant Leadership

    **Answer:** C) Contingency Theory
    **Explanation:** Contingency (or Situational) Theories "Argue that effective leadership depends on the situation...Leaders need to adapt their style to fit the specific circumstances."

185. **An independent Anti-Corruption Agency (ACA) is designed to be**
    A) under the direct control of the President.
    B) a branch of the military.
    C) free from political influence in its investigations.
    D) a committee within the legislature.
    E) a department of the Ministry of Finance.

    **Answer:** C) free from political influence in its investigations.
    **Explanation:** The effectiveness of ACAs relies on their independence, allowing them to investigate and prosecute corruption without political interference.

---

### **Chapter 15: Structure and Functions of Local Government**

186. **The legal existence and powers of local government are typically defined in the**
    A) national budget.
    B) party manifesto.
    C) national constitution and local government acts.
    D) annual presidential address.
    E) civil service rules.

    **Answer:** C) national constitution and local government acts.
    **Explanation:** The text states that the framework for local government is "usually defined and established by...The National Constitution" and "National or State/Regional Legislation (Local Government Acts/Laws)."

187. **The principle that public functions should be handled by the lowest competent level of government is called**
    A) Centralization.
    B) Subsidiarity.
    C) Sovereignty.
    D) Federalism.
    E) Unitarism.

    **Answer:** B) Subsidiarity.
    **Explanation:** Subsidiarity is defined as "the principle that public functions should be exercised by the lowest level of government competent to perform them effectively."

188. **The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level is the**
    A) Traditional council of chiefs.
    B) Local council.
    C) Office of the Mayor.
    D) Local judiciary.
    E) Community development association.

    **Answer:** B) Local council.
    **Explanation:** The local council is described as "The primary legislative and policy-making body at the local level."

189. **In many African countries, the role of traditional authorities in local governance often includes**
    A) commanding the local police.
    B) collecting national income tax.
    C) conducting foreign relations.
    D) resolving disputes using customary methods.
    E) passing the national budget.

    **Answer:** D) resolving disputes using customary methods.
    **Explanation:** "Dispute resolution (using customary methods)" is listed as a key potential role for traditional authorities in local communities.

190. **A major source of locally generated revenue for local governments is**
    A) Grants from the central government.
    B) Loans from the World Bank.
    C) Property rates and taxes.
    D) Foreign aid.
    E) Revenue from oil exports.

    **Answer:** C) Property rates and taxes.
    **Explanation:** Property rates/taxes are a classic example of "Locally Generated Revenue (Internal Sources)" for local governments. Grants are an external source.

191. **A grant from the central government that must be used for a specific purpose, like primary education, is a(n)**
    A) General purpose grant.
    B) Specific purpose (conditional) grant.
    C) Equalization grant.
    D) Block grant.
    E) Unconditional grant.

    **Answer:** B) Specific purpose (conditional) grant.
    **Explanation:** Specific purpose grants are defined as those that "Must be used for particular sectors or programs...often with conditions attached."

192. **The financial plan of a local government, outlining expected revenues and expenditures, is the**
    A) Manifesto.
    B) Constitution.
    C) Audit report.
    D) Budget.
    E) Development plan.

    **Answer:** D) Budget.
    **Explanation:** A budget is defined as "A financial plan for a fiscal year, outlining expected revenues and proposed expenditures."

193. **Spending on the construction of a new local road or school is an example of**
    A) Recurrent expenditure.
    B) Capital expenditure.
    C) Debt servicing.
    D) Administrative cost.
    E) Consumption expenditure.

    **Answer:** B) Capital expenditure.
    **Explanation:** Capital (or development) expenditure is defined as "Investment in long-term assets (e.g., construction of schools, roads, clinics)."

194. **Community participation in local governance is important because it**
    A) increases government secrecy.
    B) ensures policies are relevant and promotes local ownership.
    C) makes decision-making faster.
    D) reduces the need for local elections.
    E) centralizes power in the hands of the chairperson.

    **Answer:** B) ensures policies are relevant and promotes local ownership.
    **Explanation:** The importance of community participation includes ensuring "local policies and projects are relevant to community needs" and promoting "local ownership and sustainability."

195. **Rural development is a process aimed at improving the well-being of people in rural areas and primarily involves**
    A) only agricultural development.
    B) only building large factories.
    C) a multifaceted approach including economic, social, and infrastructural improvements.
    D) moving all rural people to cities.
    E) focusing exclusively on traditional customs.

    **Answer:** C) a multifaceted approach including economic, social, and infrastructural improvements.
    **Explanation:** Rural development is described as multifaceted, including agriculture but also economic diversification, infrastructure, and social development (health, education).

196. **The head of the executive arm of a local government is often called the**
    A) Councillor.
    B) Town Clerk.
    C) Chairperson or Mayor.
    D) Chief Judge.
    E) Traditional Ruler.

    **Answer:** C) Chairperson or Mayor.
    **Explanation:** The text identifies the head of the local executive as the "Chairperson/Mayor/Council Leader."

197. **The final approval of the local government budget is typically done by the**
    A) Chairperson/Mayor.
    B) Central government's finance minister.
    C) Local council.
    D) Traditional ruler.
    E) Local government staff.

    **Answer:** C) Local council.
    **Explanation:** The budget cycle described indicates that the draft budget is submitted to the "local council for review, debate, modification, and final approval."

198. **A key difference between a local government council and a Community-Based Organization (CBO) is that the council**
    A) is informal.
    B) has legally established governmental powers.
    C) is a voluntary group.
    D) focuses on a single project.
    E) is not involved in service delivery.

    **Answer:** B) has legally established governmental powers.
    **Explanation:** A local government council is a formal, statutory body with powers defined by law (e.g., to make by-laws, levy taxes), whereas a CBO is a voluntary civil society group.

199. **The permanent officials who carry out the day-to-day work of a local government are its**
    A) Councillors.
    B) Administration/Staff.
    C) Mayor and Deputy Mayor.
    D) Traditional Chiefs.
    E) Party agents.

    **Answer:** B) Administration/Staff.
    **Explanation:** The local government administration or staff are the "permanent officials and employees who carry out the administrative and operational functions."

200. **The process by which ordinary citizens organize themselves to take collective action on local issues is**
    A) Centralization.
    B) Grassroots mobilization.
    C) Nationalization.
    D) Executive decree.
    E) Judicial review.

    **Answer:** B) Grassroots mobilization.
    **Explanation:** Grassroots mobilization is defined as the "process by which ordinary citizens or local community groups organize themselves to take collective action to address common problems."

---

### **Comprehensive Review Questions (Covering Multiple Chapters)**

201. **A federal constitution is a necessary feature of a state with a**
    A) parliamentary system of government.
    B) federal system of government.
    C) unitary system of government.
    D) monarchical system of government.
    E) one-party system.

    **Answer:** B) federal system of government.
    **Explanation:** A federal system, by definition, requires a constitution that divides powers between central and regional governments. (Ch 2 & 3)

202. **The concept of checks and balances is a practical application of the principle of**
    A) popular sovereignty.
    B) the rule of law.
    C) separation of powers.
    D) judicial independence.
    E) collective responsibility.

    **Answer:** C) separation of powers.
    **Explanation:** Checks and balances is the mechanism used to enforce the separation of powers, preventing any single branch from becoming too dominant. (Ch 3)

203. **Which of the following pairs of concepts are necessarily in opposition?**
    A) Democracy and Federalism
    B) Authoritarianism and Rule of Law
    C) Presidentialism and Republicanism
    D) Unitarism and Parliamentary System
    E) Monarchy and Sovereignty

    **Answer:** B) Authoritarianism and Rule of Law
    **Explanation:** Authoritarianism concentrates power and places the state above the law, while the Rule of Law subjects everyone, including the state, to the law. They are fundamentally incompatible. (Ch 1 & 3)

204. **The "watchdog" function of the media is most essential for ensuring**
    A) government accountability.
    B) national unity.
    C) economic growth.
    D) party discipline.
    E) judicial independence.

    **Answer:** A) government accountability.
    **Explanation:** By scrutinizing the government and exposing wrongdoing, the media plays a crucial role in holding public officials accountable to the people. (Ch 8 & 14)

205. **An election conducted using the Proportional Representation (PR) system is likely to result in a**
    A) two-party system.
    B) one-party dominant system.
    C) multi-party system and coalition government.
    D) system with no political parties.
    E) presidential system.

    **Answer:** C) multi-party system and coalition government.
    **Explanation:** PR systems give smaller parties a better chance of winning seats, which often leads to multi-party systems where no single party has a majority, necessitating coalition governments. (Ch 2 & 7)

206. **The International Court of Justice (ICJ) primarily settles legal disputes between**
    A) individuals.
    B) corporations.
    C) states.
    D) non-governmental organizations.
    E) provinces within a single country.

    **Answer:** C) states.
    **Explanation:** The ICJ's function is to "settle legal disputes submitted to it by states" and give advisory opinions to UN organs. (Ch 6 & 12)

207. **Which political ideology would most strongly advocate for a significant role for the state in redistributing wealth and providing extensive social welfare?**
    A) Classical Liberalism
    B) Conservatism
    C) Socialism
    D) Anarchism
    E) Libertarianism

    **Answer:** C) Socialism
    **Explanation:** Socialism advocates for a significant state role in "redistributing wealth, providing social welfare...and managing key industries to benefit society as a whole." (Ch 2)

208. **The body of professional, non-political government officials employed on a permanent basis is the**
    A) Cabinet.
    B) Legislature.
    C) Judiciary.
    D) Civil Service.
    E) Electorate.

    **Answer:** D) Civil Service.
    **Explanation:** The Civil Service is defined as the body of "professional, non-political government officials...employed on a permanent basis." (Ch 4 & 14)

209. **The Responsibility to Protect (R2P) challenges the traditional notion of**
    A) human rights.
    B) state sovereignty.
    C) balance of power.
    D) free trade.
    E) diplomacy.

    **Answer:** B) state sovereignty.
    **Explanation:** R2P posits that the international community can intervene in a state's affairs to prevent atrocities, which can conflict with the principle of non-intervention inherent in state sovereignty. (Ch 11 & 12)

210. **The development of a national health insurance policy is an example of which stage of the policy cycle?**
    A) Agenda Setting
    B) Policy Formulation and Adoption
    C) Policy Termination
    D) Grassroots Mobilization
    E) Electoral Campaigning

    **Answer:** B) Policy Formulation and Adoption
    **Explanation:** Designing and formally choosing a national health insurance scheme is a clear example of formulating policy options and adopting one as the official course of action. (Ch 9)

211. **The power of a president to appoint cabinet members who are then confirmed by the legislature is an example of**
    A) fusion of powers.
    B) checks and balances.
    C) direct democracy.
    D) unitary government.
    E) judicial review.

    **Answer:** B) checks and balances.
    **Explanation:** This is a classic example of checks and balances, where the executive's power to appoint is checked by the legislature's power to confirm. (Ch 3)

212. **Which of the following is NOT a source of revenue for local governments?**
    A) Property taxes
    B) Grants from central government
    C) Profits from national oil sales
    D) Fees for market stalls
    E) Business licenses

    **Answer:** C) Profits from national oil sales
    **Explanation:** Revenue from major national resources like oil is typically collected and managed by the central/federal government, not directly by local governments. (Ch 15)

213. **A key difference between a Prime Minister and a President is that the Prime Minister is**
    A) directly elected by the entire country in a separate election.
    B) the head of state in a monarchy.
    C) chosen from and accountable to the legislature.
    D) not the head of government.
    E) able to serve for life.

    **Answer:** C) chosen from and accountable to the legislature.
    **Explanation:** In a parliamentary system, the PM is the leader of the majority party in parliament and can be removed by a vote of no confidence, ensuring accountability to the legislature. (Ch 2 & 4)

214. **The Universal Declaration of Human Rights (UDHR) is a foundational document for**
    A) international trade law.
    B) international human rights law.
    C) global environmental policy.
    D) the laws of war only.
    E) maritime law.

    **Answer:** B) international human rights law.
    **Explanation:** The UDHR is the landmark document that forms the basis for numerous legally binding human rights treaties and conventions. (Ch 10 & 12)

215. **A government that focuses on increasing GDP per capita but ignores income inequality and environmental damage is prioritizing**
    A) sustainable development.
    B) human development.
    C) economic growth.
    D) social justice.
    E) good governance.

    **Answer:** C) economic growth.
    **Explanation:** This scenario describes a narrow focus on economic growth, which is defined by increases in GDP, while neglecting the broader aspects of human and sustainable development. (Ch 13)

216. **In international relations, forming an alliance to counter a rising power is an example of**
    A) internal balancing.
    B) external balancing.
    C) soft power.
    D) diplomacy.
    E) collective security.

    **Answer:** B) external balancing.
    **Explanation:** The concept of balance of power involves two main mechanisms: internal balancing (building up one's own strength) and external balancing (forming alliances with others). (Ch 11)

217. **Which of the following is an example of a civil liberty?**
    A) The right to education
    B) Freedom from torture
    C) The right to healthcare
    D) The right to social security
    E) The right to work

    **Answer:** B) Freedom from torture
    **Explanation:** Freedom from torture is a classic civil liberty (a negative right) protecting individuals from government action. The other options are examples of economic and social rights (positive rights) that often require government action to provide. (Ch 3 & 10)

218. **The most significant challenge posed by the "digital divide" relates to the implementation of**
    A) fiscal policy.
    B) e-government.
    C) foreign policy.
    D) judicial review.
    E) national defense.

    **Answer:** B) e-government.
    **Explanation:** The digital divide, or unequal access to technology, directly hinders the goal of e-government to provide services to all citizens electronically. (Ch 14)

219. **A country with an unwritten, flexible constitution and a parliamentary system of government is**
    A) The United States.
    B) Nigeria.
    C) The United Kingdom.
    D) France.
    E) Germany.

    **Answer:** C) The United Kingdom.
    **Explanation:** The UK is the primary example used in the text for having an unwritten, flexible constitution and a parliamentary system. (Ch 2 & 3)

220. **The process of participatory budgeting is a mechanism to enhance**
    A) central government control.
    B) judicial independence.
    C) community participation in local governance.
    D) the power of the executive.
    E) the speed of policy implementation.

    **Answer:** C) community participation in local governance.
    **Explanation:** Participatory budgeting is a specific tool that allows citizens to have a direct say in how public money is spent at the local level, thus increasing participation. (Ch 15)

221. **Which of these institutions is NOT part of the executive branch?**
    A) The Cabinet
    B) The Civil Service
    C) The Supreme Court
    D) Ministries
    E) The Presidency

    **Answer:** C) The Supreme Court
    **Explanation:** The Supreme Court is the head of the judicial branch, which is separate from the executive branch. The other options are all components of the executive. (Ch 3 & 4)

222. **The "primacy of public interest" is a core ethical principle guiding the conduct of**
    A) private entrepreneurs.
    B) foreign diplomats.
    C) public administrators.
    D) international journalists.
    E) corporate lobbyists.

    **Answer:** C) public administrators.
    **Explanation:** A core ethical value for public servants is acting in the public interest, placing it above personal gain or other considerations. (Ch 14)

223. **The ECOWAS, EU, and ASEAN are all examples of**
    A) global military alliances.
    B) international financial institutions.
    C) regional international organizations.
    D) UN specialized agencies.
    E) human rights NGOs.

    **Answer:** C) regional international organizations.
    **Explanation:** These are all organizations whose members come from a specific geographic region, formed to promote cooperation and integration. (Ch 12)

224. **In a court of law, a tort is a wrongful act that results in injury to another person, for which the injured party may seek compensation. This falls under**
    A) criminal law.
    B) constitutional law.
    C) administrative law.
    D) civil law.
    E) international law.

    **Answer:** D) civil law.
    **Explanation:** Personal injury claims (torts) are a classic example of civil law, involving disputes between private parties where the remedy is usually monetary compensation (damages). (Ch 6)

225. **The leader who acts as a role model and inspires followers to achieve extraordinary outcomes is demonstrating \_\_\_\_\_ leadership.**
    A) autocratic
    B) transactional
    C) laissez-faire
    D) transformational
    E) bureaucratic

    **Answer:** D) transformational
    **Explanation:** Two key components of transformational leadership are "Idealized Influence" (acting as a role model) and "Inspirational Motivation" (inspiring followers). (Ch 14)

226. **Which of the following would a realist scholar of IR consider most important?**
    A) The moral values of a state's leader
    B) The distribution of military power in the international system
    C) The resolutions passed by the UN General Assembly
    D) The level of trade between two countries
    E) The shared cultural identity of a region

    **Answer:** B) The distribution of military power in the international system
    **Explanation:** Realism prioritizes power, especially military power, as the key variable in explaining state behavior and international outcomes. (Ch 11)

227. **A major advantage of a federal system over a unitary system is its ability to**
    A) make decisions more quickly.
    B) ensure uniformity of all laws.
    C) manage diversity and give autonomy to regions.
    D) reduce the cost of government.
    E) eliminate conflicts between levels of government.

    **Answer:** C) manage diversity and give autonomy to regions.
    **Explanation:** A key advantage of federalism is that it "Allows for regional diversity and autonomy" and is "suitable for large and diverse countries." (Ch 2)

228. **An ombudsman and an auditor-general are both examples of**
    A) executive agencies.
    B) legislative committees.
    C) judicial courts.
    D) external oversight and accountability bodies.
    E) informal civil society groups.

    **Answer:** D) external oversight and accountability bodies.
    **Explanation:** Both are listed as independent, external bodies designed to hold the government accountable for administrative fairness (Ombudsman) and financial propriety (Auditor-General). (Ch 4)

229. **The Bretton Woods Conference of 1944 led to the creation of the**
    A) United Nations and League of Nations.
    B) World Bank and International Monetary Fund.
    C) African Union and ECOWAS.
    D) European Union and NATO.
    E) World Trade Organization and GATT.

    **Answer:** B) World Bank and International Monetary Fund.
    **Explanation:** The text states that both the IMF and the World Bank Group were established in 1944 at the Bretton Woods Conference. (Ch 13)

230. **Which of the following is NOT a fundamental right typically found in a constitution?**
    A) Freedom of speech
    B) Right to life
    C) Right to a fair hearing
    D) Right to hold public office without qualifications
    E) Freedom of religion

    **Answer:** D) Right to hold public office without qualifications
    **Explanation:** While citizens have the right to participate in government, the right to hold public office is always subject to meeting certain qualifications (e.g., age, citizenship, education). (Ch 3)

231. **The main purpose of a political party's manifesto is to**
    A) list its registered members.
    B) present its policies and programs to the electorate.
    C) provide a legal framework for its existence.
    D) challenge election results in court.
    E) select candidates through primaries.

    **Answer:** B) present its policies and programs to the electorate.
    **Explanation:** A party platform or manifesto is developed to "address societal problems and guide government action if elected," and is presented to voters during campaigns. (Ch 2 & 8)

232. **A bicameral legislature is one that has**
    A) one chamber or house.
    B) two chambers or houses.
    C) three chambers or houses.
    D) no power to make laws.
    E) members who are all appointed.

    **Answer:** B) two chambers or houses.
    **Explanation:** The term "bicameral" literally means "two-chambered" (e.g., a Senate and a House of Representatives). This term is standard political science knowledge, implied by the discussion of legislatures. (Ch 3)

233. **A key function of a constitution is to**
    A) list all citizens by name.
    B) define and limit the powers of government.
    C) serve as the annual budget.
    D) act as a criminal code only.
    E) provide a history of the nation's culture.

    **Answer:** B) define and limit the powers of government.
    **Explanation:** A constitution "Defines the structure and powers of the main organs of government" and regulates their relationships, thereby limiting their powers. (Ch 3)

234. **If a country's legislature can amend the constitution through the same process as passing an ordinary law, the constitution is said to be**
    A) rigid.
    B) written.
    C) flexible.
    D) federal.
    E) uncodified.

    **Answer:** C) flexible.
    **Explanation:** A flexible constitution is defined as one that "Can be amended relatively easily, often by the same process as ordinary laws." (Ch 3)

235. **The existence of interest groups is a key feature of a \_\_\_\_\_ democracy.**
    A) totalitarian
    B) authoritarian
    C) pluralist
    D) direct
    E) socialist

    **Answer:** C) pluralist
    **Explanation:** Pluralism is a theory of democracy where power is seen as dispersed among various competing groups. Interest groups are the primary example of these groups, and their existence is vital for a pluralist system. (Ch 8)

236. **Which of these best illustrates the concept of political socialization?**
    A) A court interpreting a law
    B) A family teaching a child about national identity and the importance of voting
    C) A government agency implementing a new tax policy
    D) A central bank setting interest rates
    E) A country signing an international treaty

    **Answer:** B) A family teaching a child about national identity and the importance of voting
    **Explanation:** Political socialization is the process through which individuals acquire political values and beliefs. This happens through agents like family, schools, and media. The example in option B is a classic illustration. (Ch 1)

237. **The primary purpose of a code of conduct for public officials is to**
    A) increase their salaries.
    B) guide ethical behavior and prevent corruption.
    C) give them immunity from prosecution.
    D) define the structure of government.
    E) outline foreign policy goals.

    **Answer:** B) guide ethical behavior and prevent corruption.
    **Explanation:** Codes of conduct are an anti-corruption and accountability mechanism that establishes ethical standards for public officials. (Ch 4 & 14)

238. **The 'Harm Principle', often associated with John Stuart Mill, states that the only legitimate reason to restrict an individual's liberty is to**
    A) promote economic equality.
    B) uphold traditional values.
    C) prevent harm to others.
    D) increase the power of the state.
    E) ensure conformity.

    **Answer:** C) prevent harm to others.
    **Explanation:** This is a core concept of liberalism, stating that liberty is paramount unless its exercise causes harm to other people. It underpins the idea of justifiable limitations on rights. (Ch 2 & 10)

239. **In a confederal system, ultimate sovereignty rests with the**
    A) central government.
    B) constituent states.
    C) judiciary.
    D) people directly.
    E) international community.

    **Answer:** B) constituent states.
    **Explanation:** A confederal system is a "league of independent states" where the "States retain ultimate sovereignty." The central government is weak and derives its power from the states. (Ch 2)

240. **A government's decision to privatize state-owned enterprises is a form of**
    A) Social Policy.
    B) Foreign Policy.
    C) Economic Policy.
    D) Defense Policy.
    E) Cultural Policy.

    **Answer:** C) Economic Policy.
    **Explanation:** Privatization is a major economic reform policy, falling under the government's function of economic management and development. (Ch 1 & 9)

241. **The 'Iron Triangle' is a concept used to describe the powerful policy-making relationship between**
    A) the president, the prime minister, and the monarch.
    B) the legislature, the executive, and the judiciary.
    C) legislative committees, bureaucratic agencies, and interest groups.
    D) the media, the public, and political parties.
    E) the IMF, the World Bank, and the WTO.

    **Answer:** C) legislative committees, bureaucratic agencies, and interest groups.
    **Explanation:** Though not explicitly in the text, this is a standard concept in public policy that explains how these three actors often collaborate to create and implement policies in a specific area, to their mutual benefit. It's a classic WAEC-level concept. (Ch 8 & 9)

242. **The idea that democracies are highly unlikely to go to war with one another is known as**
    A) Balance of Power Theory.
    B) Democratic Peace Theory.
    C) Dependency Theory.
    D) Game Theory.
    E) World Systems Theory.

    **Answer:** B) Democratic Peace Theory.
    **Explanation:** This is a core tenet of the liberal school of thought in International Relations, as mentioned in the text's focus for Liberalism. (Ch 11)

243. **A unicameral legislature is one in which**
    A) there are two legislative chambers.
    B) there is only one legislative chamber.
    C) the members are not elected.
    D) the executive is also a member.
    E) decisions are always unanimous.

    **Answer:** B) there is only one legislative chamber.
    **Explanation:** "Uni" means one, so a unicameral legislature has a single house or chamber. (Implied in Ch 3)

244. **Which of the following would be the most direct form of citizen participation in national law-making?**
    A) Voting in a presidential election
    B) Participating in a referendum
    C) Joining a political party
    D) Lobbying a representative
    E) Writing a letter to a newspaper

    **Answer:** B) Participating in a referendum
    **Explanation:** A referendum allows citizens to vote directly on a specific proposed law, making it a mechanism of direct democracy and the most direct form of participation in law-making from the options. (Ch 2)

245. **The civil service is expected to provide the government with**
    A) political leadership.
    B) financial campaign contributions.
    C) continuity and expert advice.
    D) electoral victory.
    E) judicial rulings.

    **Answer:** C) continuity and expert advice.
    **Explanation:** The permanent and professional nature of the civil service ensures that there is continuity in administration despite changes in political government, and it provides the specialized knowledge needed for policy-making. (Ch 4 & 14)

246. **Which of the following is an example of a public good?**
    A) A private car
    B) A personal computer
    C) National defense
    D) A ticket to a football match
    E) A loaf of bread

    **Answer:** C) National defense
    **Explanation:** A public good is non-excludable (you can't stop someone from benefiting) and non-rivalrous (one person's use doesn't diminish another's). National defense is a classic example provided by the government for the benefit of all society. (Ch 1)

247. **The process of gerrymandering involves**
    A) manipulating electoral district boundaries to favor a political party.
    B) funding election campaigns through illegal means.
    C) suppressing the votes of a particular group.
    D) counting votes dishonestly.
    E) holding elections more frequently than required.

    **Answer:** A) manipulating electoral district boundaries to favor a political party.
    **Explanation:** Gerrymandering is a specific anti-democratic practice related to the delimitation of constituencies, a function of the EMB. It's a standard concept relevant to the electoral process. (Ch 7)

248. **In a parliamentary system, a minority government is one that**
    A) represents an ethnic minority.
    B) is formed by a party that has fewer than 50% of the seats but has enough support to govern.
    C) has been in power for a very short time.
    D) is formed by a coalition of all parties.
    E) has no opposition.

    **Answer:** B) is formed by a party that has fewer than 50% of the seats but has enough support to govern.
    **Explanation:** This is a common outcome in parliamentary systems, especially with multiple parties. The government does not have an absolute majority on its own and may rely on the support of smaller parties on a case-by-case basis. (Ch 2)

249. **The official responsible for the day-to-day administration of a government ministry and who is a career civil servant is the**
    A) Minister.
    B) President.
    C) Permanent Secretary.
    D) Councillor.
    E) Judge.

    **Answer:** C) Permanent Secretary.
    **Explanation:** The text describes a ministry as being headed by a Minister (a political appointee) and a senior civil servant, such as a Permanent Secretary or Director-General, who is a career official. (Ch 4)

250. **The main objective of a pressure group is to**
    A) contest and win elections.
    B) form a government.
    C) influence government policy in favor of its members.
    D) appoint judges to the Supreme Court.
    E) provide an alternative government.

    **Answer:** C) influence government policy in favor of its members.
    **Explanation:** Pressure groups (or interest groups) are defined by their goal of influencing public policy on issues relevant to their members, without seeking to gain control of government themselves. (Ch 8)

Chapter 1: Meaning and Scope of Government and Governance (15 Questions)

1. The formal institutions, structures, and processes through which a state is ruled is known as
(A) Governance
(B) Politics
(C) Government
(D) Anarchy

Answer: C
Explanation: The text defines Government as "the formal institutions, structures, and processes through which a state or a community is ruled and its affairs are administered and regulated."

2. Which concept is broader than government and includes the various actors and informal mechanisms that influence decision-making?
(A) Politics
(B) Governance
(C) State
(D) Sovereignty

Answer: B
Explanation: The text states, "Governance is a broader concept than government. It encompasses not only the formal institutions of government but also the various actors (e.g., civil society organizations, private sector...)."

3. The activity through which groups make, preserve, and amend the general rules under which they live is called
(A) Administration
(B) Bureaucracy
(C) Politics
(D) Constitution

Answer: C
Explanation: The text defines Politics as "the activity or process through which groups of people make, preserve, and amend the general rules under which they live."

4. Harold Lasswell famously described politics as
(A) the art of the possible
(B) who gets what, when, and how
(C) the end of history
(D) the will of the people

Answer: B
Explanation: The text explicitly mentions Harold Lasswell's description of politics as "who gets what, when, and how."

5. The most fundamental function of government is often considered to be the
(A) provision of education
(B) management of the economy
(C) maintenance of law and order
(D) promotion of national identity

Answer: C
Explanation: The text states, "This is often considered the most fundamental function of government," referring to the maintenance of law and order.

6. Protecting a country from external aggression is part of the government's
(A) economic management function
(B) social welfare function
(C) political socialization function
(D) security function

Answer: D
Explanation: The text lists protecting the country's territory from external threats under the "External Security (National Defense)" aspect of the security function.

7. Building roads, providing schools, and operating hospitals are examples of the government's function of
(A) national defense
(B) conflict resolution
(C) provision of public services
(D) political socialization

Answer: C
Explanation: The text lists infrastructure (roads), education (schools), and healthcare (hospitals) as examples of the "Provision of Public Services and Social Welfare."

8. Managing government revenue and expenditure to influence the economy is known as
(A) Monetary policy
(B) Trade policy
(C) Fiscal policy
(D) Foreign policy

Answer: C
Explanation: The text defines Fiscal Policy as "Managing government revenue (taxation) and expenditure (public spending) to influence the economy."

9. The legitimate authority to use force to maintain order is described as a
(A) monopoly on the legitimate use of force
(B) mandate of heaven
(C) social contract
(D) divine right of kings

Answer: A
Explanation: One of the key characteristics of government listed in the text is "The legitimate authority to use force or coercion... (monopoly on the legitimate use of of force)."

10. In a democracy, supreme power is vested in the
(A) military
(B) monarch
(C) people
(D) judiciary

Answer: C
Explanation: The text defines democracy as a system "where supreme power is vested in the people."

11. A system of government characterized by a strong central power and limited political freedoms is called
(A) Democracy
(B) Authoritarianism
(C) Anarchy
(D) Theocracy

Answer: B
Explanation: Authoritarianism is defined in the text as "A system of government characterized by a strong central power and limited political freedoms."

12. In a constitutional monarchy, the monarch's powers are
(A) absolute and unrestricted
(B) limited by a constitution
(C) exercised by a dictator
(D) derived from the military

Answer: B
Explanation: The text states that in a constitutional monarchy, "the monarch's powers are limited by a constitution and laws."

13. "Rule by the few" for the benefit of an elite segment of society is known as
(A) Oligarchy
(B) Democracy
(C) Monarchy
(D) Theocracy

Answer: A
Explanation: The text defines Oligarchy as "a system of government in which power rests with a small, elite segment of society."

14. The principle of good governance that ensures government operations and decisions are open to public scrutiny is
(A) Accountability
(B) Participation
(C) Rule of Law
(D) Transparency

Answer: D
Explanation: Transparency is defined as ensuring "Information is freely available and directly accessible to those who will be affected by such decisions..."

15. A government where religious leaders rule based on religious law is a
(A) Plutocracy
(B) Theocracy
(C) Gerontocracy
(D) Meritocracy

Answer: B
Explanation: The text defines a Theocracy as "Government by religious leaders or based on religious law."

Chapter 2: Political Ideologies and Systems (20 Questions)

16. Which political ideology emphasizes individual liberty, rights, and consent of the governed?
(A) Socialism
(B) Conservatism
(C) Nationalism
(D) Liberalism

Answer: D
Explanation: The text states that Liberalism "Emphasizes individual liberty, rights, equality of opportunity, reason, and consent of the governed."

17. An ideology that values tradition, established institutions, and gradual change is
(A) Communism
(B) Conservatism
(C) Anarchism
(D) Liberalism

Answer: B
Explanation: Conservatism, according to the text, "Emphasizes tradition, established institutions, social order, authority, hierarchy, and gradual change."

18. The ideology that advocates for collective ownership of the means of production to promote social equality is
(A) Nationalism
(B) Liberalism
(C) Socialism
(D) Conservatism

Answer: C
Explanation: Socialism is described as emphasizing "social equality, community, cooperation, and collective ownership or control of the means of production."

19. A radical form of socialism aiming for a classless, stateless society is
(A) Communism
(B) Fascism
(C) Conservatism
(D) Liberalism

Answer: A
Explanation: The text defines Communism as "A more radical branch of socialism, aiming for a classless, stateless society where the means of production are communally owned."

20. An ideology that promotes the interests of a particular nation with the aim of gaining and maintaining sovereignty is
(A) Federalism
(B) Nationalism
(C) Internationalism
(D) Socialism

Answer: B
Explanation: Nationalism is defined as an ideology that "promotes the interests of a particular nation... with the aim of gaining and maintaining the nation's sovereignty."

21. In a direct democracy, citizens
(A) elect representatives to make laws
(B) participate directly in decision-making
(C) follow the orders of a monarch
(D) have no say in governance

Answer: B
Explanation: The text defines Direct Democracy as a system where "Citizens participate directly in making laws and policy decisions, without intermediaries."

22. A referendum is a mechanism of
(A) representative democracy
(B) direct democracy
(C) authoritarianism
(D) monarchy

Answer: B
Explanation: A referendum, where citizens vote directly on a proposed law, is listed as a mechanism of Direct Democracy.

23. The most common form of democracy in modern nation-states is
(A) Direct democracy
(B) Representative democracy
(C) Socialist democracy
(D) Athenian democracy

Answer: B
Explanation: The text states that Representative Democracy "is the most common form of democracy in modern nation-states."

24. In a unitary system of government, sovereign power is concentrated in the
(A) regional governments
(B) local governments
(C) central government
(D) judiciary

Answer: C
Explanation: A Unitary System is defined as one where "Sovereign power is concentrated in a single, central (national) government."

25. A key advantage of a federal system is that it
(A) ensures uniformity of all laws
(B) is less expensive to run
(C) allows for regional diversity and autonomy
(D) leads to faster decision-making

Answer: C
Explanation: The text lists "Allows for regional diversity and autonomy" as an advantage of a federal system, making it suitable for large and diverse countries.

26. The United Kingdom, France, and Japan are examples of countries with a
(A) federal system
(B) confederal system
(C) unitary system
(D) presidential system

Answer: C
Explanation: The text explicitly lists the UK, France, and Japan as examples of a Unitary System.

27. In a presidential system of government, the executive is
(A) drawn from the legislature
(B) accountable to the legislature through a vote of no confidence
(C) headed by a Prime Minister
(D) elected independently of the legislature

Answer: D
Explanation: The text states that in a presidential system, the "President is usually elected independently of the legislature."

28. The fusion of executive and legislative powers is a characteristic feature of a
(A) presidential system
(B) federal system
(C) parliamentary system
(D) dictatorship

Answer: C
Explanation: The text describes a Parliamentary System as having a "Fusion of executive and legislative powers (to a large extent)."

29. The head of government in a parliamentary system is typically the
(A) President
(B) Monarch
(C) Prime Minister
(D) Chief Justice

Answer: C
Explanation: In a parliamentary system, the Head of Government is the Prime Minister, as stated in the text.

30. Nigeria's Fourth Republic operates a
(A) parliamentary system
(B) presidential system
(C) constitutional monarchy
(D) unitary system

Answer: B
Explanation: The text uses Nigeria (Fourth Republic) as an example of a Presidential System.

31. A primary function of a political party is to
(A) adjudicate legal disputes
(B) conduct foreign policy exclusively
(C) recruit and nominate candidates for public office
(D) enforce laws

Answer: C
Explanation: The text lists "Recruit and Nominate Candidates" as a key function of political parties.

32. Parties not in power that critically examine government actions are known as the
(A) judiciary
(B) civil service
(C) pressure group
(D) opposition

Answer: D
Explanation: The text states, "Parties not in power (opposition) critically examine government actions, hold it accountable, and offer alternative policies."

33. A country where only one political party is legally allowed to hold power has a
(A) one-party system
(B) two-party system
(C) multi-party system
(D) dominant-party system

Answer: A
Explanation: A One-Party System is defined as one where "Only one political party is legally allowed to exist or hold effective power."

34. The USA and (historically) the UK are often cited as examples of a
(A) one-party system
(B) multi-party system
(C) no-party system
(D) two-party system

Answer: D
Explanation: The text gives the United States and the United Kingdom as examples of a Two-Party System.

35. A system where three or more parties have a realistic chance of gaining power, often leading to coalitions, is a
(A) multi-party system
(B) two-party system
(C) one-party system
(D) zero-party system

Answer: A
Explanation: A Multi-Party System is described as one where "Three or more political parties have a realistic chance of gaining power, either individually or as part of a coalition."

Chapter 3: Constitutions and the Rule of Law (20 Questions)

36. A set of fundamental principles and legally binding rules by which a state is governed is a
(A) Manifesto
(B) Decree
(C) Constitution
(D) Statute

Answer: C
Explanation: The text defines a constitution as "a set of fundamental principles, established precedents, and legally binding rules according to which a state... is acknowledged to be governed."

37. Which of the following is NOT a typical source of a constitution?
(A) Statutes
(B) Judicial decisions
(C) Party manifestos
(D) Conventions

Answer: C
Explanation: The text lists statutes, judicial decisions, and conventions as sources of constitutions. Party manifestos are political programs, not foundational legal sources.

38. The constitution of the United Kingdom is a prime example of an
(A) unwritten constitution
(B) written constitution
(C) rigid constitution
(D) federal constitution

Answer: A
Explanation: The text explicitly names the United Kingdom as the primary example of an Unwritten (Uncodified) Constitution.

39. A constitution that is difficult to amend, requiring a special procedure, is described as
(A) Flexible
(B) Unitary
(C) Rigid
(D) Unwritten

Answer: C
Explanation: A Rigid Constitution is defined as being "Difficult to amend (change). Requires a special, complex procedure for amendment."

40. The division of governmental powers among the executive, legislative, and judicial branches is the principle of
(A) Rule of Law
(B) Separation of Powers
(C) Collective Responsibility
(D) Federalism

Answer: B
Explanation: The text defines Separation of Powers as "the division of governmental powers among distinct and independent branches."

41. The primary function of the legislature is to
(A) interpret laws
(B) enforce laws
(C) make laws
(D) execute laws

Answer: C
Explanation: The text states that the primary function of the Legislature is "To make laws (statutes) for the society."

42. The branch of government responsible for implementing and enforcing laws is the
(A) Judiciary
(B) Legislature
(C) Ombudsman
(D) Executive

Answer: D
Explanation: The Executive branch's primary function is "To implement, execute, and enforce the laws made by the legislature."

43. The power of the judiciary to declare a law unconstitutional is known as
(A) Veto power
(B) Judicial review
(C) Prerogative of mercy
(D) Legislative oversight

Answer: B
Explanation: Judicial Review is defined as "The power of a court... to examine laws passed by the legislature... and to determine whether they are constitutional."

44. The system where the branches of government can limit each other's powers is called
(A) fusion of powers
(B) checks and balances
(C) separation of church and state
(D) popular sovereignty

Answer: B
Explanation: The text explains that under Checks and Balances, "the branches are also designed to check and balance each other's powers to prevent any one branch from becoming dominant."

45. Which of these is an example of a check by the executive on the legislature?
(A) Impeachment
(B) Judicial review
(C) Vetoing a bill
(D) Confirming appointments

Answer: C
Explanation: The text gives the example of the President vetoing bills passed by the legislature as a check.

46. A section in a constitution that guarantees basic rights and freedoms is often called a
(A) Preamble
(B) Bill of Rights
(C) Manifesto
(D) Code of Conduct

Answer: B
Explanation: The text states that most modern democratic constitutions include a section "often called a Bill of Rights or Chapter on Fundamental Rights."

47. Freedoms that protect individuals from unwarranted government interference are known as
(A) Civil duties
(B) Civil liberties
(C) Civil service
(D) Civil law

Answer: B
Explanation: The text defines Civil Liberties as "Freedoms that protect individuals from unwarranted government interference."

48. The right to a fair hearing is also known as
(A) Due process
(B) Habeas corpus
(C) Stare decisis
(D) Mandamus

Answer: A
Explanation: The text lists the "Right to a Fair Hearing (Due Process)" as a fundamental right.

49. Fundamental rights are not absolute and can be limited for reasons of
(A) political convenience
(B) partisan advantage
(C) national security
(D) elite preference

Answer: C
Explanation: The text notes that rights can be limited for purposes like "protecting national security."

50. Paying taxes and obeying the law are considered
(A) Fundamental rights
(B) Civil liberties
(C) Political ideologies
(D) Duties of citizens

Answer: D
Explanation: The text lists "Obeying the law" and "Paying taxes" under the "Duties and Responsibilities of Citizens."

51. In a federal system, the constitution must be
(A) unwritten and flexible
(B) written and rigid
(C) monarchical and flexible
(D) unitary and rigid

Answer: B
Explanation: Federal systems require a written constitution to specify the division of powers, and it is usually rigid to prevent one level of government from unilaterally changing this division.

52. The political philosopher most famously associated with the theory of separation of powers is
(A) Karl Marx
(B) John Locke
(C) Baron de Montesquieu
(D) Thomas Hobbes

Answer: C
Explanation: The text credits Baron de Montesquieu as the one who most famously articulated the principle of separation of powers.

53. A constitution that can be amended by the ordinary law-making process is
(A) Rigid
(B) Federal
(C) Republican
(D) Flexible

Answer: D
Explanation: A Flexible Constitution is defined as one that "Can be amended relatively easily, often by the same process as ordinary laws."

54. The main aim of the separation of powers is to
(A) concentrate power in the executive
(B) make government more efficient
(C) protect liberty and prevent tyranny
(D) ensure the dominance of the legislature

Answer: C
Explanation: The text states that the aim of separation of powers is "To protect liberty, prevent tyranny, and promote efficiency by ensuring that no single branch becomes too powerful."

55. The formal process of changing or adding to a constitution is called
(A) Interpretation
(B) Review
(C) Amendment
(D) Promulgation

Answer: C
Explanation: Constitutional Amendment is defined as "The formal process of changing, adding to, or repealing parts of a constitution."

Chapter 4: Executive Branch and Administrative Structures (15 Questions)

56. The executive branch of government is primarily responsible for
(A) making laws
(B) interpreting laws
(C) implementing laws
(D) amending the constitution

Answer: C
Explanation: The chapter introduction states the executive is "responsible for implementing, executing, and enforcing the laws made by the legislature."

57. In a presidential system, the President is usually
(A) only Head of State
(B) only Head of Government
(C) both Head of State and Head of Government
(D) a ceremonial figurehead

Answer: C
Explanation: The text clarifies that the President in a presidential system is "Usually both Head of State... and Head of Government."

58. The collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive and senior ministers is the
(A) Legislature
(B) Judiciary
(C) Cabinet
(D) Civil Service

Answer: C
Explanation: The Cabinet is defined as "A collective decision-making body consisting of the chief executive... and senior government ministers."

59. In a parliamentary system, the principle that cabinet members must publicly support government decisions is called
(A) individual responsibility
(B) collective responsibility
(C) separation of powers
(D) rule of law

Answer: B
Explanation: The text explains Collective Responsibility as the expectation that "Cabinet members are expected to publicly support government decisions."

60. The operational arm of the executive branch responsible for day-to-day administration is the
(A) Cabinet
(B) Legislature
(C) Bureaucracy
(D) Political Party

Answer: C
Explanation: The Bureaucracy (or Public Administration) is described as "The complex system of government departments, agencies, and officials responsible for the day-to-day implementation and administration of laws."

61. A key characteristic of an ideal bureaucracy, according to Max Weber, is
(A) political patronage
(B) merit-based appointment
(C) frequent changes in rules
(D) personal favoritism

Answer: B
Explanation: The text lists "Merit-based Appointment and Promotion" as a key characteristic of Weber's model of bureaucracy.

62. A government ministry is typically headed by a
(A) Permanent Secretary only
(B) Director-General only
(C) Minister and a Permanent Secretary
(D) Judge

Answer: C
Explanation: The text states that a Ministry is "headed by a Minister (political appointee...) and a senior civil servant (e.g., Permanent Secretary...)."

63. An organization established by government to perform a specific function with some operational autonomy is a
(A) Political party
(B) Pressure group
(C) Government agency or parastatal
(D) Ministry

Answer: C
Explanation: Government Agencies (Parastatals) are defined as "Organizations established by government to perform specific functions, often with some degree of operational autonomy."

64. The transfer of powers, functions, and resources to democratically elected local governments is called
(A) Deconcentration
(B) Delegation
(C) Devolution
(D) Centralization

Answer: C
Explanation: Devolution is defined as the "Transfer of powers, functions, and resources to democratically elected local governments," and is described as the strongest form of decentralization.

65. The level of government closest to the people and responsible for local services is the
(A) central government
(B) state government
(C) local government
(D) federal government

Answer: C
Explanation: Local Government is defined as "The level of government below the central/national... government, responsible for... a specific local area."

66. The body of professional, non-political government officials employed on a permanent basis is the
(A) Cabinet
(B) Political party
(C) Civil service
(D) Legislature

Answer: C
Explanation: The Civil Service is defined as "The body of professional, non-political government officials who are employed on a permanent basis."

67. Political neutrality in the civil service means that civil servants should
(A) join the ruling party
(B) serve the government of the day impartially
(C) run for political office
(D) ignore the directives of ministers

Answer: B
Explanation: The text states that under political neutrality, "Civil servants are expected to serve the government of the day loyally and impartially, regardless of its political party."

68. An independent official who investigates complaints from citizens about maladministration by government is the
(A) Auditor-General
(B) Ombudsman
(C) Chief Justice
(D) Attorney-General

Answer: B
Explanation: The Ombudsman is defined as "An independent official who investigates complaints from citizens about maladministration or injustice by government agencies."

69. The independent body that audits government accounts to ensure financial propriety is the
(A) Ombudsman
(B) Central Bank
(C) Ministry of Finance
(D) Auditor-General

Answer: D
Explanation: The Auditor-General (or Supreme Audit Institution) is described as the "Independent body that audits government accounts and expenditures."

70. Holding ministers accountable to parliament through question time is an example of
(A) legal accountability
(B) public accountability
(C) political accountability
(D) administrative accountability

Answer: C
Explanation: The text lists accountability to the Legislature (parliament) as a key mechanism of Political Accountability.

Chapter 6: Judiciary and Legal System (20 Questions)

71. The highest court in most judicial systems is the
(A) High Court
(B) Magistrate Court
(C) Court of Appeal
(D) Supreme Court

Answer: D
Explanation: The text identifies the Supreme Court as the "highest court in the judicial system" or the "Apex Court."

72. Courts that handle minor criminal cases and small civil disputes are known as
(A) Supreme Courts
(B) Courts of Appeal
(C) High Courts
(D) Lower Courts

Answer: D
